C Bundler/DuplicatedGem
S Details
L A Gem's requirements should be listed only once in a Gemfile.
S Example
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop'
L   gem 'rubocop'
L 
L   # bad
L   group :development do
L     gem 'rubocop'
L   end
L 
L   group :test do
L     gem 'rubocop'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   group :development, :test do
L     gem 'rubocop'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', groups: [:development, :test]
L 
L   # good - conditional declaration
L   if Dir.exist?(local)
L     gem 'rubocop', path: local
L   elsif ENV['RUBOCOP_VERSION'] == 'master'
L     gem 'rubocop', git: 'https://github.com/rubocop/rubocop.git'
L   else
L     gem 'rubocop', '~> 0.90.0'
L   end
C Bundler/DuplicatedGroup
S Details
L A Gem group, or a set of groups, should be listed only once in a Gemfile.
L 
L For example, if the values of `source`, `git`, `platforms`, or `path`
L surrounding `group` are different, no offense will be registered:
L 
L [source,ruby]
L -----
L platforms :ruby do
L   group :default do
L     gem 'openssl'
L   end
L end
L 
L platforms :jruby do
L   group :default do
L     gem 'jruby-openssl'
L   end
L end
L -----
S Example
L   # bad
L   group :development do
L     gem 'rubocop'
L   end
L 
L   group :development do
L     gem 'rubocop-rails'
L   end
L 
L   # bad (same set of groups declared twice)
L   group :development, :test do
L     gem 'rubocop'
L   end
L 
L   group :test, :development do
L     gem 'rspec'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   group :development do
L     gem 'rubocop'
L   end
L 
L   group :development, :test do
L     gem 'rspec'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', groups: [:development, :test]
L   gem 'rspec', groups: [:development, :test]
C Bundler/GemComment
S Details
L Each gem in the Gemfile should have a comment explaining
L its purpose in the project, or the reason for its version
L or source.
L 
L The optional "OnlyFor" configuration array
L can be used to only register offenses when the gems
L use certain options or have version specifiers.
L 
L When "version_specifiers" is included, a comment
L will be enforced if the gem has any version specifier.
L 
L When "restrictive_version_specifiers" is included, a comment
L will be enforced if the gem has a version specifier that
L holds back the version of the gem.
L 
L For any other value in the array, a comment will be enforced for
L a gem if an option by the same name is present.
L A useful use case is to enforce a comment when using
L options that change the source of a gem:
L 
L - `bitbucket`
L - `gist`
L - `git`
L - `github`
L - `source`
L 
L For a full list of options supported by bundler,
L see https://bundler.io/man/gemfile.5.html
L .
S Example: OnlyFor: [] (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   gem 'foo'
L 
L   # good
L 
L   # Helpers for the foo things.
L   gem 'foo'
S Example: OnlyFor: ['version_specifiers']
L   # bad
L 
L   gem 'foo', '< 2.1'
L 
L   # good
L 
L   # Version 2.1 introduces breaking change baz
L   gem 'foo', '< 2.1'
S Example: OnlyFor: ['restrictive_version_specifiers']
L   # bad
L 
L   gem 'foo', '< 2.1'
L 
L   # good
L 
L   gem 'foo', '>= 1.0'
L 
L   # Version 2.1 introduces breaking change baz
L   gem 'foo', '< 2.1'
S Example: OnlyFor: ['version_specifiers', 'github']
L   # bad
L 
L   gem 'foo', github: 'some_account/some_fork_of_foo'
L 
L   gem 'bar', '< 2.1'
L 
L   # good
L 
L   # Using this fork because baz
L   gem 'foo', github: 'some_account/some_fork_of_foo'
L 
L   # Version 2.1 introduces breaking change baz
L   gem 'bar', '< 2.1'
C Bundler/GemFilename
S Details
L Verifies that a project contains Gemfile or gems.rb file and correct
L associated lock file based on the configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: Gemfile (default)
L   # bad
L   Project contains gems.rb and gems.locked files
L 
L   # bad
L   Project contains Gemfile and gems.locked file
L 
L   # good
L   Project contains Gemfile and Gemfile.lock
S Example: EnforcedStyle: gems.rb
L   # bad
L   Project contains Gemfile and Gemfile.lock files
L 
L   # bad
L   Project contains gems.rb and Gemfile.lock file
L 
L   # good
L   Project contains gems.rb and gems.locked files
C Bundler/GemVersion
S Details
L Enforce that Gem version specifications or a commit reference (branch,
L ref, or tag) are either required or forbidden.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: required (default)
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', '~> 1.12'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', '>= 1.10.0'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', '>= 1.5.0', '< 1.10.0'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', branch: 'feature-branch'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', ref: '74b5bfbb2c4b6fd6cdbbc7254bd7084b36e0c85b'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop', tag: 'v1.17.0'
S Example: EnforcedStyle: forbidden
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop'
L 
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop', '~> 1.12'
L 
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop', '>= 1.10.0'
L 
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop', '>= 1.5.0', '< 1.10.0'
L 
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop', branch: 'feature-branch'
L 
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop', ref: '74b5bfbb2c4b6fd6cdbbc7254bd7084b36e0c85b'
L 
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop', tag: 'v1.17.0'
C Bundler/InsecureProtocolSource
S Details
L Passing symbol arguments to `source` (e.g. `source :rubygems`) is
L deprecated because they default to using HTTP requests. Instead, specify
L `'https://rubygems.org'` if possible, or `'http://rubygems.org'` if not.
L 
L When autocorrecting, this cop will replace symbol arguments with
L `'https://rubygems.org'`.
L 
L This cop will not replace existing sources that use `http://`. This may
L be necessary where HTTPS is not available. For example, where using an
L internal gem server via an intranet, or where HTTPS is prohibited.
L However, you should strongly prefer `https://` where possible, as it is
L more secure.
L 
L If you don't allow `http://`, please set `false` to `AllowHttpProtocol`.
L This option is `true` by default for safe autocorrection.
S Example
L   # bad
L   source :gemcutter
L   source :rubygems
L   source :rubyforge
L 
L   # good
L   source 'https://rubygems.org' # strongly recommended
S Example: AllowHttpProtocol: true (default)
L   # good
L   source 'http://rubygems.org' # use only if HTTPS is unavailable
S Example: AllowHttpProtocol: false
L   # bad
L   source 'http://rubygems.org'
C Bundler/OrderedGems
S Details
L Gems should be alphabetically sorted within groups.
S Example
L   # bad
L   gem 'rubocop'
L   gem 'rspec'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rspec'
L   gem 'rubocop'
L 
L   # good
L   gem 'rubocop'
L 
L   gem 'rspec'
S Example: TreatCommentsAsGroupSeparators: true (default)
L   # good
L   # For code quality
L   gem 'rubocop'
L   # For tests
L   gem 'rspec'
S Example: TreatCommentsAsGroupSeparators: false
L   # bad
L   # For code quality
L   gem 'rubocop'
L   # For tests
L   gem 'rspec'
C Capybara/AbsoluteXPathInElementScope
S Details
L Checks for absolute XPath searches on Capybara elements.
L 
L An XPath starting with `//` searches from the document root, even when
L the query is called on an element. Use `.//` to stay within it.
L Direct RSpec `let` and named `subject` element definitions are
L recognized.
S Example
L   # bad
L   page.find('.dialog').find(:xpath, '//button')
L   dialog = page.find('.dialog')
L   dialog.find(:xpath, '//button')
L   RSpec.describe 'dialog' do
L     let(:dialog) { find('.dialog') }
L     it { dialog.find(:xpath, '//button') }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   page.find('.dialog').find(:xpath, './/button')
L   page.find(:xpath, '//button')
C Capybara/AlwaysTrueCountQuery
S Details
L Checks for positive queries that succeed when there are no matches.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(page).to have_css('.item', minimum: 0)
L   page.has_css?('.item', minimum: 0)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_css('.item')
L   expect(page).to have_css('.item', minimum: 0, maximum: 2)
L   all('.item', minimum: 0)
C Capybara/AmbiguousClick
S Details
L Specify the exact target to click on.
L 
L In projects where accessibility needs to be considered,
L it is crucial to specify the click target precisely.
S Example
L   # bad
L   click_link_or_button('foo')
L   click_on('foo')
L 
L   # good
L   click_link('foo')
L   click_button('foo')
C Capybara/AssertStyle
S Details
L Checks for usage of deprecated assert style method.
S Example
L   # bad
L   page.find(:css, '#first').assert_style(display: 'block')
L 
L   # good
L   page.find(:css, '#first').assert_matches_style(display: 'block')
C Capybara/ChainedFind
S Details
L Avoid chaining `find` methods and combine selectors.
L 
L Combining selectors into a single `find` call is more efficient than
L chaining multiple `find` calls, as it reduces the number of DOM queries.
L Only recognizable Capybara finder chains with string locators are
L checked.
L Set `DefaultSelector` to match `Capybara.default_selector`, or to
L `null` if it varies at runtime. Autocorrection requires `css` and
L literal strings.
S Example
L   # bad
L   page.find('#foo').find('.bar')
L   page.find('#foo').find('.bar').find('.baz')
L 
L   # good
L   page.find('#foo .bar')
L   page.find('#foo .bar .baz')
L   find('#form').find(:button, 'Save')
C Capybara/FindAllFirst
S Details
L Enforces use of `first` instead of `all` with `first` or `[0]`.
S Safety
L   With default options, `all(...).first` and `all(...)[0]` return
L   `nil` when no element matches, while `first(...)` raises an
L   exception. Autocorrection can break code that depends on `nil`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   all('a').first
L   all('a')[0]
L 
L   # good
L   first('a')
C Capybara/IgnoredQueryOption
S Details
L Checks for query options that have no effect in Capybara.
L 
L Count options passed to singular finders are not autocorrected because
L removing them may conceal the author's intent.
L 
L This cop targets Capybara's built-in selectors. Set `DefaultSelector`
L to match `Capybara.default_selector`, or to `null` if it varies at
L runtime. Queries using custom or unknown selectors are skipped.
S Example
L   # bad
L   find('.row', count: 3)
L   find(:css, '.item', exact: true)
L   find('.item', text: /foo/, exact_text: true)
L   all(:css, '.item', match: :first)
L   expect(page).to have_css('.item', count: 3, minimum: 1)
L 
L   # good
L   all('.row', count: 3)
L   find(:css, '.item')
L   find('.item', text: /foo/)
L   all(:css, '.item')
L   expect(page).to have_css('.item', count: 3)
S Example: DefaultSelector: custom
L   # bad
L   find(:css, '.item', text: /foo/, exact_text: true)
L 
L   # good
L   find('.item', text: /foo/, exact_text: true)
C Capybara/ModalMethodWithoutBlock
S Details
L Checks that modal handling methods are called with a block.
S Example
L   # bad
L   accept_confirm
L   page.accept_prompt(with: 'answer')
L 
L   # good
L   accept_confirm { click_button 'Delete' }
L   page.accept_prompt(with: 'answer', &trigger_modal)
C Capybara/NativeAttribute
S Details
L Enforces use of `Element#[]` instead of `native.attribute`.
L 
L Only calls on Capybara finder chains are checked.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because attribute values may
L   differ between the driver API and Capybara's `Element#[]`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   find('.item').native.attribute(:id)
L   page.find('.item').native.attribute('aria-label')
L 
L   # good
L   find('.item')[:id]
L   page.find('.item')['aria-label']
C Capybara/RSpec/CurrentPathExpectation
S Details
L Checks that no expectations are set on Capybara's `current_path`.
L 
L The
L https://www.rubydoc.info/github/teamcapybara/capybara/master/Capybara/RSpecMatchers#have_current_path-instance_method[`have_current_path` matcher]
L should be used on `page` to set expectations on Capybara's
L current path, since it uses
L https://github.com/teamcapybara/capybara/blob/master/README.md#asynchronous-javascript-ajax-and-friends[Capybara's waiting functionality]
L which ensures that preceding actions (like `click_link`) have
L completed.
L 
L This cop does not support autocorrection in some cases.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(current_path).to eq('/callback')
L   expect(page.current_path).to eq('/callback')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_current_path('/callback', ignore_query: true)
L 
L   # bad (does not support autocorrection when `match` with a variable)
L   expect(current_path).to match(variable)
C Capybara/RSpec/CurrentUrlExpectation
S Details
L Checks expectations on `current_url` that bypass Capybara's waiting.
L 
L `have_current_path` can match full URLs and waits for navigation.
L A regexp needs `url: true` so it matches the full URL, not just the
L path. Autocorrection is unsafe because URL parsing and waiting change
L comparison behavior.
L Dynamic `match` patterns and `include`, `start_with`, and `end_with`
L are reported without autocorrection.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(page.current_url).to eq('https://example.com/login')
L   expect(current_url).to match(%r{/login})
L   expect(current_url).to match('login')
L   expect(page.current_url).to include('/login')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_current_path('https://example.com/login')
L   expect(page).to have_current_path(%r{/login}, url: true)
L   expect(page).to have_current_path(/login/, url: true)
C Capybara/RSpec/FindElementValueEq
S Details
L Checks for expectations on the value of an element found by Capybara.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because `find('input').value`
L   checks the value of the first matched element, while `have_field`
L   checks that any field on the page has the expected value.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(find('input').value).to eq('foobar')
L   expect(find(:css, 'input').value).to eq('foobar')
L   expect(find_field('Email').value).to eq('user@example.com')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_field(with: 'foobar')
L   expect(page).to have_field('Email', with: 'user@example.com')
C Capybara/RSpec/HaveContent
S Details
L Checks for usage of `have_content` and `have_no_content`.
L 
L Capybara provides `have_text` and `have_no_text` matchers that are
L more concise and preferred over their aliases `have_content` and
L `have_no_content`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(page).to have_content('capy')
L   expect(page).to have_no_content('bara')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_text('capy')
L   expect(page).to have_no_text('bara')
C Capybara/RSpec/HaveSelector
S Details
L Use CSS or XPath matchers instead of generic selector matchers.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to have_selector(:css, 'bar')
L   expect(foo).to have_no_selector(:css, 'bar')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to have_css('bar')
L   expect(foo).to have_no_css('bar')
L 
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to have_selector(:xpath, 'bar')
L   expect(foo).to have_no_selector(:xpath, 'bar')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to have_xpath('bar')
L   expect(foo).to have_no_xpath('bar')
S Example: DefaultSelector: css (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to have_selector('bar')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to have_css('bar')
S Example: DefaultSelector: xpath
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to have_selector('bar')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to have_xpath('bar')
C Capybara/RSpec/MatchStyle
S Details
L Checks for usage of deprecated style methods in RSpec matchers.
S Example: when using `has_style?`
L   # bad
L   expect(page.find(:css, 'first')
L     .has_style?(display: 'block')).to be true
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page.find(:css, 'first')
L     .matches_style?(display: 'block')).to be true
S Example: when using `have_style`
L   # bad
L   expect(page).to have_style(display: 'block')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to match_style(display: 'block')
C Capybara/RSpec/NegationMatcher
S Details
L Enforces use of `have_no_*` or `not_to` for negated expectations.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: have_no (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(page).not_to have_selector 'a'
L   expect(page).not_to have_css('a')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_no_selector 'a'
L   expect(page).to have_no_css('a')
S Example: EnforcedStyle: not_to
L   # bad
L   expect(page).to have_no_selector 'a'
L   expect(page).to have_no_css('a')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).not_to have_selector 'a'
L   expect(page).not_to have_css('a')
C Capybara/RSpec/NegationMatcherAfterVisit
S Details
L Do not allow negative matchers to be used immediately after `visit`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   visit foo_path
L   expect(page).to have_no_link('bar')
L   expect(page).to have_css('a')
L 
L   # good
L   visit foo_path
L   expect(page).to have_css('a')
L   expect(page).to have_no_link('bar')
L 
L   # bad
L   visit foo_path
L   expect(page).not_to have_link('bar')
L   expect(page).to have_css('a')
L 
L   # good
L   visit foo_path
L   expect(page).to have_css('a')
L   expect(page).not_to have_link('bar')
C Capybara/RSpec/PredicateMatcher
S Details
L Prefer using predicate matcher over using predicate method directly.
L 
L Capybara defines magic matchers for predicate methods.
L This cop recommends to use the predicate matcher instead of using
L predicate method directly.
S Example: Strict: true, EnforcedStyle: inflected (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(foo.matches_css?(bar: 'baz')).to be_truthy
L   expect(foo.matches_selector?(bar: 'baz')).to be_truthy
L   expect(foo.matches_style?(bar: 'baz')).to be_truthy
L   expect(foo.matches_xpath?(bar: 'baz')).to be_truthy
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to match_css(bar: 'baz')
L   expect(foo).to match_selector(bar: 'baz')
L   expect(foo).to match_style(bar: 'baz')
L   expect(foo).to match_xpath(bar: 'baz')
L 
L   # also good - It checks "true" strictly.
L   expect(foo.matches_style?(bar: 'baz')).to be(true)
S Example: Strict: false, EnforcedStyle: inflected
L   # bad
L   expect(foo.matches_style?(bar: 'baz')).to be_truthy
L   expect(foo.matches_style?(bar: 'baz')).to be(true)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to match_style(bar: 'baz')
S Example: Strict: true, EnforcedStyle: explicit
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to match_style(bar: 'baz')
L 
L   # good - the above code is rewritten to it by this cop
L   expect(foo.matches_style?(bar: 'baz')).to be(true)
S Example: Strict: false, EnforcedStyle: explicit
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to match_style(bar: 'baz')
L 
L   # good - the above code is rewritten to it by this cop
L   expect(foo.matches_style?(bar: 'baz')).to be_truthy
C Capybara/RSpec/SpecificMatcher
S Details
L Checks for there is a more specific matcher offered by Capybara.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(page).to have_selector('button')
L   expect(page).to have_no_selector('button.cls')
L   expect(page).to have_css('button')
L   expect(page).to have_no_css('a.cls', href: 'http://example.com')
L   expect(page).to have_css('table.cls')
L   expect(page).to have_css('select')
L   expect(page).to have_css('input', exact_text: 'foo')
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_button
L   expect(page).to have_no_button(class: 'cls')
L   expect(page).to have_button
L   expect(page).to have_no_link('foo', class: 'cls', href: 'http://example.com')
L   expect(page).to have_table(class: 'cls')
L   expect(page).to have_select
L   expect(page).to have_field(with: 'foo')
C Capybara/RSpec/VisibilityMatcher
S Details
L Checks for boolean visibility in Capybara RSpec matchers.
L 
L Capybara lets you find elements that match a certain visibility using
L the `:visible` option. `:visible` accepts both boolean and symbols as
L values, however using booleans can have unwanted effects. `visible:
L false` does not find just invisible elements, but both visible and
L invisible elements. For expressiveness and clarity, use one of the
L symbol values, `:all`, `:hidden` or `:visible`.
L Only `true` is autocorrected, to `:visible`.
L Set `DefaultSelector` to match `Capybara.default_selector`, or to
L `null` if it varies at runtime.
L Read more at: https://www.rubydoc.info/gems/capybara/Capybara%2FNode%2FFinders:all
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because custom filters on
L   built-in selectors may treat `true` and `:visible` differently.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(page).to have_selector('.foo', visible: true)
L   expect(page).to have_css('.foo', visible: false)
L   expect(page).to have_link('my link', visible: false)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(page).to have_selector('.foo', visible: :visible)
L   expect(page).to have_css('.foo', visible: :all)
L   expect(page).to have_link('my link', visible: :hidden)
C Capybara/RedundantWithinFind
S Details
L Checks for redundant `within find(...)` calls.
S Example
L   # bad
L   within find('foo.bar') do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   within 'foo.bar' do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   within find_by_id('foo') do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   within :id, 'foo' do
L     # ...
L   end
C Capybara/SpecificActions
S Details
L Checks for there is a more specific actions offered by Capybara.
S Example
L   # bad
L   find('a[href="/home"]').click
L   find('button.cls').click
L   find('a[href="/home"]', exact_text: 'foo').click
L   find('div button').click
L 
L   # good
L   click_link(href: '/home')
L   click_button(class: 'cls')
L   click_link(href: '/home', exact_text: 'foo')
L   find('div').click_button
C Capybara/SpecificFinders
S Details
L Checks if there is a more specific finder offered by Capybara.
S Example
L   # bad
L   find('#some-id')
L   find('[id=some-id]')
L   find(:css, '#some-id')
L   find(:id, 'some-id')
L   find(:link, 'Home')
L   find(:field, 'Name')
L   find('input[placeholder="Email"]')
L   find(:css, 'input[type="checkbox"]')
L 
L   # good
L   find_by_id('some-id')
L   find_link('Home')
L   find_field('Name')
L   find_field(placeholder: 'Email')
L   find_field(type: 'checkbox')
C Capybara/UnorderedWindowAccess
S Details
L Checks positional access to Capybara windows, whose order is undefined.
S Example
L   # bad
L   popup = windows.last
L   original = page.windows.first
L 
L   # good
L   popup = window_opened_by { click_link 'Open' }
L   original = current_window
C Capybara/VisibilityOption
S Details
L Checks for boolean values passed to Capybara's `visible` option.
L 
L `visible: false` finds both visible and hidden elements, rather than
L only hidden elements. Use `:all`, `:hidden` or `:visible` instead.
L Only `true` is autocorrected, to `:visible`.
L 
L This cop targets Capybara's built-in selectors. Set `DefaultSelector`
L to match `Capybara.default_selector`, or to `null` if it varies at
L runtime. Queries using custom or unknown selectors are skipped.
S Example
L   # bad
L   find('.menu', visible: true)
L   expect(page).to have_css('.menu', visible: false)
L 
L   # good
L   find('.menu', visible: :visible)
L   find('.menu', visible: :all)
L   find('.menu', visible: :hidden)
S Example: DefaultSelector: custom
L   # bad
L   find(:css, '.menu', visible: true)
L 
L   # good
L   find('.menu', visible: true)
C FactoryBot/AssociationStyle
S Details
L Use a consistent style to define associations.
S Safety
L   This cop may cause false-positives in `EnforcedStyle: explicit`
L   case. It recognizes any method call that has no arguments as an
L   implicit association but it might be a user-defined trait call.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: implicit (default)
L   # bad
L   factory :post do
L     association :user
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   factory :post do
L     user
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   factory :post do
L     association :user, :author
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   factory :post do
L     user factory: %i[user author]
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: explicit
L   # bad
L   factory :post do
L     user
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   factory :post do
L     association :user
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   factory :post do
L     user factory: %i[user author]
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   factory :post do
L     association :user, :author
L   end
L 
L   # good (NonImplicitAssociationMethodNames: ['email'])
L   sequence :email do |n|
L     "person#{n}@example.com"
L   end
L 
L   factory :user do
L     email
L   end
C FactoryBot/AttributeDefinedStatically
S Details
L Always declare attribute values as blocks.
S Example
L   # bad
L   kind [:active, :rejected].sample
L 
L   # good
L   kind { [:active, :rejected].sample }
L 
L   # bad
L   closed_at 1.day.from_now
L 
L   # good
L   closed_at { 1.day.from_now }
L 
L   # bad
L   count 1
L 
L   # good
L   count { 1 }
C FactoryBot/ConsistentParenthesesStyle
S Details
L Use a consistent style for parentheses in factory_bot calls.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: require_parentheses` (default)
L   # bad
L   create :user
L   build :login
L 
L   # good
L   create(:user)
L   build(:login)
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: omit_parentheses`
L   # bad
L   create(:user)
L   build(:login)
L 
L   # good
L   create :user
L   build :login
L 
L   # also good
L   # when method name and first argument are not on same line
L   create(
L     :user
L   )
L   build(
L     :user,
L     name: 'foo'
L   )
S Example: `ExplicitOnly: false` (default)
L   # bad - with `EnforcedStyle: require_parentheses`
L   FactoryBot.create :user
L   build :user
L 
L   # good - with `EnforcedStyle: require_parentheses`
L   FactoryBot.create(:user)
L   build(:user)
S Example: `ExplicitOnly: true`
L   # bad - with `EnforcedStyle: require_parentheses`
L   FactoryBot.create :user
L   FactoryBot.build :user
L 
L   # good - with `EnforcedStyle: require_parentheses`
L   FactoryBot.create(:user)
L   FactoryBot.build(:user)
L   create :user
L   build :user
C FactoryBot/CreateList
S Details
L Checks for create_list usage.
L 
L This cop can be configured using the `EnforcedStyle` option
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because replacing `n.times` to
L   `create_list` changes its returned value.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: create_list` (default)
L   # bad
L   3.times { create :user }
L   3.times.map { create :user }
L   [create(:user), create(:user), create(:user)]
L   Array.new(3) { create :user }
L 
L   # good
L   create_list :user, 3
L 
L   # bad
L   3.times { create :user, age: 18 }
L 
L   # good - index is used to alter the created models attributes
L   3.times { |n| create :user, age: n }
L 
L   # good - contains a method call, may return different values
L   3.times { create :user, age: rand }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: n_times`
L   # bad
L   create_list :user, 3
L   [create(:user), create(:user), create(:user)]
L 
L   # good
L   3.times.map { create :user }
S Example: `ExplicitOnly: false` (default)
L   # bad - with `EnforcedStyle: create_list`
L   3.times { FactoryBot.create :user }
L   3.times { create :user }
L 
L   # good - with `EnforcedStyle: create_list`
L   FactoryBot.create_list :user, 3
L   create_list :user, 3
S Example: `ExplicitOnly: true`
L   # bad - with `EnforcedStyle: create_list`
L   3.times { FactoryBot.create :user }
L 
L   # good - with `EnforcedStyle: create_list`
L   FactoryBot.create_list :user, 3
L   create_list :user, 3
L   3.times { create :user }
C FactoryBot/ExcessiveCreateList
S Details
L Check for excessive model creation in a list.
S Example: MaxAmount: 10 (default)
L   # We do not allow more than 10 items to be created
L 
L   # bad
L   create_list(:merge_request, 1000, state: :opened)
L 
L   # good
L   create_list(:merge_request, 10, state: :opened)
S Example: MaxAmount: 20
L   # We do not allow more than 20 items to be created
L 
L   # bad
L   create_list(:merge_request, 1000, state: :opened)
L 
L   # good
L   create_list(:merge_request, 15, state: :opened)
C FactoryBot/FactoryAssociationWithStrategy
S Details
L Use definition in factory association instead of hard coding a strategy.
S Example
L   # bad - only works for one strategy
L   factory :foo do
L     profile { create(:profile) }
L   end
L 
L   # good - implicit
L   factory :foo do
L     profile
L   end
L 
L   # good - explicit
L   factory :foo do
L     association :profile
L   end
L 
L   # good - inline
L   factory :foo do
L     profile { association :profile }
L   end
C FactoryBot/FactoryClassName
S Details
L Use string value when setting the class attribute explicitly.
L 
L This cop would promote faster tests by lazy-loading of
L application files. Also, this could help you suppress potential bugs
L in combination with external libraries by avoiding a preload of
L application files from the factory files.
S Example
L   # bad
L   factory :foo, class: Foo do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   factory :foo, class: 'Foo' do
L   end
C FactoryBot/FactoryNameStyle
S Details
L Checks for name style for argument of FactoryBot::Syntax::Methods.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: symbol (default)
L   # bad
L   create('user')
L   build "user", username: "NAME"
L 
L   # good
L   create(:user)
L   build :user, username: "NAME"
L 
L   # good - namespaced models
L   create('users/internal')
S Example: EnforcedStyle: string
L   # bad
L   create(:user)
L   build :user, username: "NAME"
L 
L   # good
L   create('user')
L   build "user", username: "NAME"
S Example: `ExplicitOnly: false` (default)
L   # bad - with `EnforcedStyle: symbol`
L   FactoryBot.create('user')
L   create('user')
L 
L   # good - with `EnforcedStyle: symbol`
L   FactoryBot.create(:user)
L   create(:user)
S Example: `ExplicitOnly: true`
L   # bad - with `EnforcedStyle: symbol`
L   FactoryBot.create(:user)
L   FactoryBot.build "user", username: "NAME"
L 
L   # good - with `EnforcedStyle: symbol`
L   FactoryBot.create('user')
L   FactoryBot.build "user", username: "NAME"
L   FactoryBot.create(:user)
L   create(:user)
C FactoryBot/IdSequence
S Details
L Do not create a FactoryBot sequence for an id column.
S Example
L   # bad - can lead to conflicts between FactoryBot and DB sequences
L   factory :foo do
L     sequence :id
L   end
L 
L   # good - a non-id column
L   factory :foo do
L     sequence :some_non_id_column
L   end
C FactoryBot/RedundantFactoryOption
S Details
L Checks for redundant `factory` option.
S Example
L   # bad
L   association :user, factory: :user
L 
L   # good
L   association :user
C FactoryBot/SyntaxMethods
S Details
L Use shorthands from `FactoryBot::Syntax::Methods` in your specs.
S Safety
L   The autocorrection is marked as unsafe because the cop
L   cannot verify whether you already include
L   `FactoryBot::Syntax::Methods` in your test suite.
L 
L   If you're using Rails, add the following configuration to
L   `spec/support/factory_bot.rb` and be sure to require that file in
L   `rails_helper.rb`:
L 
L   [source,ruby]
L   ----
L   RSpec.configure do |config|
L     config.include FactoryBot::Syntax::Methods
L   end
L   ----
L 
L   If you're not using Rails:
L 
L   [source,ruby]
L   ----
L   RSpec.configure do |config|
L     config.include FactoryBot::Syntax::Methods
L 
L     config.before(:suite) do
L       FactoryBot.find_definitions
L     end
L   end
L   ----
S Example
L   # bad
L   FactoryBot.create(:bar)
L   FactoryBot.build(:bar)
L   FactoryBot.attributes_for(:bar)
L 
L   # good
L   create(:bar)
L   build(:bar)
L   attributes_for(:bar)
C Gemspec/AddRuntimeDependency
S Details
L Prefer `add_dependency` over `add_runtime_dependency` as the latter is
L considered soft-deprecated.
S Example
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_runtime_dependency('rubocop')
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_dependency('rubocop')
L   end
C Gemspec/AttributeAssignment
S Details
L Use consistent style for Gemspec attributes assignment.
S Example
L   # bad
L   # This example uses two styles for assignment of metadata attribute.
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata = { 'key' => 'value' }
L     spec.metadata['another-key'] = 'another-value'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata['key'] = 'value'
L     spec.metadata['another-key'] = 'another-value'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata = { 'key' => 'value', 'another-key' => 'another-value' }
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   # This example uses two styles for assignment of authors attribute.
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.authors = %w[author-0 author-1]
L     spec.authors[2] = 'author-2'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.authors = %w[author-0 author-1 author-2]
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.authors[0] = 'author-0'
L     spec.authors[1] = 'author-1'
L     spec.authors[2] = 'author-2'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # This example uses consistent assignment per attribute,
L   # even though two different styles are used overall.
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata = { 'key' => 'value' }
L     spec.authors[0] = 'author-0'
L     spec.authors[1] = 'author-1'
L     spec.authors[2] = 'author-2'
L   end
C Gemspec/DependencyVersion
S Details
L Enforce that gem dependency version specifications or a commit reference (branch,
L ref, or tag) are either required or forbidden.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: required (default)
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_dependency 'parser'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_development_dependency 'parser'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_dependency 'parser', '>= 2.3.3.1', '< 3.0'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_development_dependency 'parser', '>= 2.3.3.1', '< 3.0'
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: forbidden
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_dependency 'parser', '>= 2.3.3.1', '< 3.0'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_development_dependency 'parser', '>= 2.3.3.1', '< 3.0'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_dependency 'parser'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_development_dependency 'parser'
L   end
C Gemspec/DeprecatedAttributeAssignment
S Details
L Checks that deprecated attributes are not set in a gemspec file.
L Removing deprecated attributes allows the user to receive smaller packed gems.
S Example
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.name = 'your_cool_gem_name'
L     spec.test_files = Dir.glob('test/**/*')
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.name = 'your_cool_gem_name'
L     spec.test_files += Dir.glob('test/**/*')
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.name = 'your_cool_gem_name'
L   end
C Gemspec/DevelopmentDependencies
S Details
L Enforce that development dependencies for a gem are specified in
L `Gemfile`, rather than in the `gemspec` using
L `add_development_dependency`. Alternatively, using `EnforcedStyle:
L gemspec`, enforce that all dependencies are specified in `gemspec`,
L rather than in `Gemfile`.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: Gemfile (default)
L   # Specify runtime dependencies in your gemspec,
L   # but all other dependencies in your Gemfile.
L 
L   # bad
L   # example.gemspec
L   s.add_development_dependency "foo"
L 
L   # good
L   # Gemfile
L   gem "foo"
L 
L   # good
L   # gems.rb
L   gem "foo"
L 
L   # good (with AllowedGems: ["bar"])
L   # example.gemspec
L   s.add_development_dependency "bar"
S Example: EnforcedStyle: gems.rb
L   # Specify runtime dependencies in your gemspec,
L   # but all other dependencies in your Gemfile.
L   #
L   # Identical to `EnforcedStyle: Gemfile`, but with a different error message.
L   # Rely on Bundler/GemFilename to enforce the use of `Gemfile` vs `gems.rb`.
L 
L   # bad
L   # example.gemspec
L   s.add_development_dependency "foo"
L 
L   # good
L   # Gemfile
L   gem "foo"
L 
L   # good
L   # gems.rb
L   gem "foo"
L 
L   # good (with AllowedGems: ["bar"])
L   # example.gemspec
L   s.add_development_dependency "bar"
S Example: EnforcedStyle: gemspec
L   # Specify all dependencies in your gemspec.
L 
L   # bad
L   # Gemfile
L   gem "foo"
L 
L   # good
L   # example.gemspec
L   s.add_development_dependency "foo"
L 
L   # good (with AllowedGems: ["bar"])
L   # Gemfile
L   gem "bar"
C Gemspec/DuplicatedAssignment
S Details
L An attribute assignment method call should be listed only once
L in a gemspec.
L 
L Assigning to an attribute with the same name using `spec.foo =` or
L `spec.attribute#[]=` will be an unintended usage. On the other hand,
L duplication of methods such as `spec.requirements`,
L `spec.add_runtime_dependency`, and others are permitted because it is
L the intended use of appending values.
S Example
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.name = 'rubocop'
L     spec.name = 'rubocop2'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.name = 'rubocop'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.requirements << 'libmagick, v6.0'
L     spec.requirements << 'A good graphics card'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_dependency('parallel', '~> 1.10')
L     spec.add_dependency('parser', '>= 2.3.3.1', '< 3.0')
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata["key"] = "value"
L     spec.metadata["key"] = "value"
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata["key"] = "value"
L   end
C Gemspec/OrderedDependencies
S Details
L Dependencies in the gemspec should be alphabetically sorted.
S Example
L   # bad
L   spec.add_dependency 'rubocop'
L   spec.add_dependency 'rspec'
L 
L   # good
L   spec.add_dependency 'rspec'
L   spec.add_dependency 'rubocop'
L 
L   # good
L   spec.add_dependency 'rubocop'
L 
L   spec.add_dependency 'rspec'
L 
L   # bad
L   spec.add_development_dependency 'rubocop'
L   spec.add_development_dependency 'rspec'
L 
L   # good
L   spec.add_development_dependency 'rspec'
L   spec.add_development_dependency 'rubocop'
L 
L   # good
L   spec.add_development_dependency 'rubocop'
L 
L   spec.add_development_dependency 'rspec'
L 
L   # bad
L   spec.add_runtime_dependency 'rubocop'
L   spec.add_runtime_dependency 'rspec'
L 
L   # good
L   spec.add_runtime_dependency 'rspec'
L   spec.add_runtime_dependency 'rubocop'
L 
L   # good
L   spec.add_runtime_dependency 'rubocop'
L 
L   spec.add_runtime_dependency 'rspec'
S Example: TreatCommentsAsGroupSeparators: true (default)
L   # good
L   # For code quality
L   spec.add_dependency 'rubocop'
L   # For tests
L   spec.add_dependency 'rspec'
S Example: TreatCommentsAsGroupSeparators: false
L   # bad
L   # For code quality
L   spec.add_dependency 'rubocop'
L   # For tests
L   spec.add_dependency 'rspec'
C Gemspec/RequireMFA
S Details
L Requires a gemspec to have `rubygems_mfa_required` metadata set.
L 
L This setting tells RubyGems that MFA (Multi-Factor Authentication) is
L required for accounts to be able to perform privileged operations, such as
L (see RubyGems' documentation for the full list of privileged
L operations):
L 
L * `gem push`
L * `gem yank`
L * `gem owner --add/remove`
L * adding or removing owners using gem ownership page
L 
L This helps make your gem more secure, as users can be more
L confident that gem updates were pushed by maintainers.
S Example
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     # no `rubygems_mfa_required` metadata specified
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata = {
L       'rubygems_mfa_required' => 'true'
L     }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata['rubygems_mfa_required'] = 'true'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata = {
L       'rubygems_mfa_required' => 'false'
L     }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata = {
L       'rubygems_mfa_required' => 'true'
L     }
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata['rubygems_mfa_required'] = 'false'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.metadata['rubygems_mfa_required'] = 'true'
L   end
C Gemspec/RequiredRubyVersion
S Details
L Checks that `required_ruby_version` in a gemspec file is set to a valid
L value (non-blank) and matches `TargetRubyVersion` as set in RuboCop's
L configuration for the gem.
L 
L This ensures that RuboCop is using the same Ruby version as the gem.
S Example
L   # When `TargetRubyVersion` of .rubocop.yml is `2.5`.
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     # no `required_ruby_version` specified
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.required_ruby_version = '>= 2.4.0'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.required_ruby_version = '>= 2.6.0'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.required_ruby_version = ''
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.required_ruby_version = '>= 2.5.0'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.required_ruby_version = '>= 2.5'
L   end
L 
L   # accepted but not recommended
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.required_ruby_version = ['>= 2.5.0', '< 2.7.0']
L   end
L 
L   # accepted but not recommended, since
L   # Ruby does not really follow semantic versioning
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.required_ruby_version = '~> 2.5'
L   end
C Gemspec/RubyVersionGlobalsUsage
S Details
L Checks that `RUBY_VERSION` and `Ruby::VERSION` constants are not used in gemspec.
L Using `RUBY_VERSION` and `Ruby::VERSION` is dangerous because the value of the
L constant is determined by `rake release`.
L It's possible to have a dependency based on the Ruby version used
L to execute `rake release` and not the user's Ruby version.
S Example
L   # bad
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     if RUBY_VERSION >= '3.0'
L       spec.add_dependency 'gem_a'
L     else
L       spec.add_dependency 'gem_b'
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Gem::Specification.new do |spec|
L     spec.add_dependency 'gem_a'
L   end
C Layout/AccessModifierIndentation
S Details
L Bare access modifiers (those not applying to specific methods) should be
L indented as deep as method definitions, or as deep as the `class`/`module`
L keyword, depending on configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: indent (default)
L   # bad
L   class Plumbus
L   private
L     def smooth; end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Plumbus
L     private
L     def smooth; end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: outdent
L   # bad
L   class Plumbus
L     private
L     def smooth; end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Plumbus
L   private
L     def smooth; end
L   end
C Layout/ArgumentAlignment
S Details
L Checks that the arguments on a multi-line method call are aligned.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: with_first_argument (default)
L   # good
L 
L   foo :bar,
L       :baz,
L       key: value
L 
L   foo(
L     :bar,
L     :baz,
L     key: value
L   )
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   foo :bar,
L     :baz,
L     key: value
L 
L   foo(
L     :bar,
L       :baz,
L       key: value
L   )
S Example: EnforcedStyle: with_fixed_indentation
L   # good
L 
L   foo :bar,
L     :baz,
L     key: value
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   foo :bar,
L       :baz,
L       key: value
C Layout/ArrayAlignment
S Details
L Checks that the elements of a multi-line array literal are
L aligned.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: with_first_element (default)
L   # good
L 
L   array = [1, 2, 3,
L            4, 5, 6]
L   array = ['run',
L            'forrest',
L            'run']
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   array = [1, 2, 3,
L     4, 5, 6]
L   array = ['run',
L        'forrest',
L        'run']
S Example: EnforcedStyle: with_fixed_indentation
L   # good
L 
L   array = [1, 2, 3,
L     4, 5, 6]
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   array = [1, 2, 3,
L            4, 5, 6]
C Layout/AssignmentIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the first line of the
L right-hand-side of a multi-line assignment.
L 
L The indentation of the remaining lines can be corrected with
L other cops such as `Layout/IndentationConsistency` and `Layout/EndAlignment`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   value =
L   if foo
L     'bar'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   value =
L     if foo
L       'bar'
L     end
C Layout/BeginEndAlignment
S Details
L Checks whether the end keyword of `begin` is aligned properly.
L 
L Two modes are supported through the `EnforcedStyleAlignWith` configuration
L parameter. If it's set to `start_of_line` (which is the default), the
L `end` shall be aligned with the start of the line where the `begin`
L keyword is. If it's set to `begin`, the `end` shall be aligned with the
L `begin` keyword.
L 
L `Layout/EndAlignment` cop aligns with keywords (e.g. `if`, `while`, `case`)
L by default. On the other hand, `||= begin` that this cop targets tends to
L align with the start of the line, it defaults to `EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line`.
L These styles can be configured by each cop.
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line (default)
L   # bad
L   foo ||= begin
L             do_something
L           end
L 
L   # good
L   foo ||= begin
L     do_something
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: begin
L   # bad
L   foo ||= begin
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   foo ||= begin
L             do_something
L           end
C Layout/BlockAlignment
S Details
L Checks whether the end keywords are aligned properly for do
L end blocks.
L 
L Three modes are supported through the `EnforcedStyleAlignWith`
L configuration parameter:
L 
L `start_of_block` : the `end` shall be aligned with the
L start of the line where the `do` appeared.
L 
L `start_of_line` : the `end` shall be aligned with the
L start of the line where the expression started.
L 
L `either` (which is the default) : the `end` is allowed to be in either
L location. The autocorrect will default to `start_of_line`.
L 
L When the `do` or `{` appears on a continuation line of multiline
L method arguments, the start of the line where the method is called
L is used as the alignment target instead of that continuation line.
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: either (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   foo.bar
L     .each do
L       baz
L         end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   foo.bar
L     .each do
L       baz
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_block
L   # bad
L 
L   foo.bar
L     .each do
L       baz
L         end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   foo.bar
L     .each do
L       baz
L     end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line
L   # bad
L 
L   foo.bar
L     .each do
L       baz
L         end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   foo.bar
L     .each do
L       baz
L   end
C Layout/BlockEndNewline
S Details
L Checks whether the end statement of a do..end block
L is on its own line.
S Example
L   # bad
L   blah do |i|
L     foo(i) end
L 
L   # good
L   blah do |i|
L     foo(i)
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   blah { |i|
L     foo(i) }
L 
L   # good
L   blah { |i|
L     foo(i)
L   }
C Layout/CaseIndentation
S Details
L Checks how the `when` and ``in``s of a `case` expression
L are indented in relation to its `case` or `end` keyword.
L 
L It will register a separate offense for each misaligned `when` and `in`.
S Example
L   # If Layout/EndAlignment is set to keyword style (default)
L   # *case* and *end* should always be aligned to same depth,
L   # and therefore *when* should always be aligned to both -
L   # regardless of configuration.
L 
L   # bad for all styles
L   case n
L     when 0
L       x * 2
L     else
L       y / 3
L   end
L 
L   case n
L     in pattern
L       x * 2
L     else
L       y / 3
L   end
L 
L   # good for all styles
L   case n
L   when 0
L     x * 2
L   else
L     y / 3
L   end
L 
L   case n
L   in pattern
L     x * 2
L   else
L     y / 3
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: case (default)
L   # if EndAlignment is set to other style such as
L   # start_of_line (as shown below), then *when* alignment
L   # configuration does have an effect.
L 
L   # bad
L   a = case n
L   when 0
L     x * 2
L   else
L     y / 3
L   end
L 
L   a = case n
L   in pattern
L     x * 2
L   else
L     y / 3
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   a = case n
L       when 0
L         x * 2
L       else
L         y / 3
L   end
L 
L   a = case n
L       in pattern
L         x * 2
L       else
L         y / 3
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: end
L   # bad
L   a = case n
L       when 0
L         x * 2
L       else
L         y / 3
L   end
L 
L   a = case n
L       in pattern
L         x * 2
L       else
L         y / 3
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   a = case n
L   when 0
L     x * 2
L   else
L     y / 3
L   end
L 
L   a = case n
L   in pattern
L     x * 2
L   else
L     y / 3
L   end
C Layout/ClassStructure
S Details
L Checks if the code style follows the `ExpectedOrder` configuration:
L 
L `Categories` allows us to map macro names into a category.
L 
L Consider an example of code style that covers the following order:
L 
L * Module inclusion (`include`, `prepend`, `extend`)
L * Constants
L * Associations (`has_one`, `has_many`)
L * Public attribute macros (`attr_accessor`, `attr_writer`, `attr_reader`)
L * Other macros (`validates`, `validate`)
L * Public class methods
L * Initializer
L * Public instance methods
L * Protected attribute macros (`attr_accessor`, `attr_writer`, `attr_reader`)
L * Protected instance methods
L * Private attribute macros (`attr_accessor`, `attr_writer`, `attr_reader`)
L * Private instance methods
L 
L NOTE: Simply enabling the cop with `Enabled: true` will not use
L the example order shown below.
L To enforce the order of macros like `attr_reader`,
L you must define both `ExpectedOrder` *and* `Categories`.
L 
L You can configure the following order:
L 
L [source,yaml]
L ----
L  Layout/ClassStructure:
L    ExpectedOrder:
L      - module_inclusion
L      - constants
L      - association
L      - public_attribute_macros
L      - public_delegate
L      - macros
L      - public_class_methods
L      - initializer
L      - public_methods
L      - protected_attribute_macros
L      - protected_methods
L      - private_attribute_macros
L      - private_delegate
L      - private_methods
L ----
L 
L Instead of putting all literals in the expected order, it is also
L possible to group categories of macros. Visibility levels are handled
L automatically.
L 
L [source,yaml]
L ----
L  Layout/ClassStructure:
L    Categories:
L      association:
L        - has_many
L        - has_one
L      attribute_macros:
L        - attr_accessor
L        - attr_reader
L        - attr_writer
L      macros:
L        - validates
L        - validate
L      module_inclusion:
L        - include
L        - prepend
L        - extend
L ----
L 
L If you only set `ExpectedOrder`
L without defining `Categories`,
L macros such as `attr_reader` or `has_many`
L will not be recognized as part of a category, and their order will not be validated.
L For example, the following will NOT raise any offenses, even if the order is incorrect:
L 
L [source,yaml]
L ----
L Layout/ClassStructure:
L   Enabled: true
L   ExpectedOrder:
L     - public_attribute_macros
L     - initializer
L ----
L 
L To make it work as expected, you must also specify `Categories` like this:
L 
L [source,yaml]
L ----
L Layout/ClassStructure:
L   ExpectedOrder:
L     - public_attribute_macros
L     - initializer
L   Categories:
L     attribute_macros:
L       - attr_reader
L       - attr_writer
L       - attr_accessor
L ----
S Safety
L   Autocorrection is unsafe because class methods and module inclusion
L   can behave differently, based on which methods or constants have
L   already been defined.
L 
L   Constants will only be moved when they are assigned with literals.
S Example
L   # bad
L   # Expect extend be before constant
L   class Person < ApplicationRecord
L     has_many :orders
L     ANSWER = 42
L 
L     extend SomeModule
L     include AnotherModule
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Person
L     # extend and include go first
L     extend SomeModule
L     include AnotherModule
L 
L     # inner classes
L     CustomError = Class.new(StandardError)
L 
L     # constants are next
L     SOME_CONSTANT = 20
L 
L     # afterwards we have public attribute macros
L     attr_reader :name
L 
L     # followed by other macros (if any)
L     validates :name
L 
L     # then we have public delegate macros
L     delegate :to_s, to: :name
L 
L     # public class methods are next in line
L     def self.some_method
L     end
L 
L     # initialization goes between class methods and instance methods
L     def initialize
L     end
L 
L     # followed by other public instance methods
L     def some_method
L     end
L 
L     # protected attribute macros and methods go next
L     protected
L 
L     attr_reader :protected_name
L 
L     def some_protected_method
L     end
L 
L     # private attribute macros, delegate macros and methods
L     # are grouped near the end
L     private
L 
L     attr_reader :private_name
L 
L     delegate :some_private_delegate, to: :name
L 
L     def some_private_method
L     end
L   end
C Layout/ClosingHeredocIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of here document closings.
S Example
L   # bad
L   class Foo
L     def bar
L       <<~SQL
L         'Hi'
L     SQL
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo
L     def bar
L       <<~SQL
L         'Hi'
L       SQL
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   # heredoc contents is before closing heredoc.
L   foo arg,
L       <<~EOS
L     Hi
L       EOS
L 
L   # good
L   foo arg,
L       <<~EOS
L     Hi
L   EOS
L 
L   # good
L   foo arg,
L       <<~EOS
L         Hi
L       EOS
C Layout/ClosingParenthesisIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of hanging closing parentheses in
L method calls, method definitions, and grouped expressions. A hanging
L closing parenthesis means `)` preceded by a line break.
S Example
L   # bad
L   some_method(
L     a,
L     b
L     )
L 
L   some_method(
L     a, b
L     )
L 
L   some_method(a, b, c
L     )
L 
L   some_method(a,
L               b,
L               c
L     )
L 
L   some_method(a,
L     x: 1,
L     y: 2
L     )
L 
L   # Scenario 1: When First Parameter Is On Its Own Line
L 
L   # good: when first param is on a new line, right paren is *always*
L   #       outdented by IndentationWidth
L   some_method(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   some_method(
L     a, b
L   )
L 
L   # Scenario 2: When First Parameter Is On The Same Line
L 
L   # good: when all other params are also on the same line, outdent
L   #       right paren by IndentationWidth
L   some_method(a, b, c
L              )
L 
L   # good: when all other params are on multiple lines, but are lined
L   #       up, align right paren with left paren
L   some_method(a,
L               b,
L               c
L              )
L 
L   # good: when other params are not lined up on multiple lines, outdent
L   #       right paren by IndentationWidth
L   some_method(a,
L     x: 1,
L     y: 2
L   )
C Layout/CommentIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of comments.
S Example
L   # bad
L     # comment here
L   def method_name
L   end
L 
L     # comment here
L   a = 'hello'
L 
L   # yet another comment
L     if true
L       true
L     end
L 
L   # good
L   # comment here
L   def method_name
L   end
L 
L   # comment here
L   a = 'hello'
L 
L   # yet another comment
L   if true
L     true
L   end
S Example: AllowForAlignment: false (default)
L   # bad
L   a = 1 # A really long comment
L         # spanning two lines.
L 
L   # good
L   # A really long comment spanning one line.
L   a = 1
S Example: AllowForAlignment: true
L   # good
L   a = 1 # A really long comment
L         # spanning two lines.
C Layout/ConditionPosition
S Details
L Checks for conditions that are not on the same line as
L if/while/until.
S Example
L   # bad
L   if
L     some_condition
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if some_condition
L     do_something
L   end
C Layout/DefEndAlignment
S Details
L Checks whether the end keywords of method definitions are
L aligned properly.
L 
L Two modes are supported through the EnforcedStyleAlignWith configuration
L parameter. If it's set to `start_of_line` (which is the default), the
L `end` shall be aligned with the start of the line where the `def`
L keyword is. If it's set to `def`, the `end` shall be aligned with the
L `def` keyword.
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   private def foo
L               end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   private def foo
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: def
L   # bad
L 
L   private def foo
L               end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   private def foo
L           end
C Layout/DotPosition
S Details
L Checks the . position in multi-line method calls.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: leading (default)
L   # bad
L   something.
L     method
L 
L   # good
L   something
L     .method
S Example: EnforcedStyle: trailing
L   # bad
L   something
L     .method
L 
L   # good
L   something.
L     method
C Layout/ElseAlignment
S Details
L Checks the alignment of else keywords. Normally they should
L be aligned with an if/unless/while/until/begin/def/rescue keyword, but there
L are special cases when they should follow the same rules as the
L alignment of end.
S Example
L   # bad
L   if something
L     code
L    else
L     code
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   if something
L     code
L    elsif something
L     code
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if something
L     code
L   else
L     code
L   end
C Layout/EmptyComment
S Details
L Checks empty comment.
S Example
L   # bad
L 
L   #
L   class Foo
L   end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   #
L   # Description of `Foo` class.
L   #
L   class Foo
L   end
S Example: AllowBorderComment: true (default)
L   # good
L 
L   def foo
L   end
L 
L   #################
L 
L   def bar
L   end
S Example: AllowBorderComment: false
L   # bad
L 
L   def foo
L   end
L 
L   #################
L 
L   def bar
L   end
S Example: AllowMarginComment: true (default)
L   # good
L 
L   #
L   # Description of `Foo` class.
L   #
L   class Foo
L   end
S Example: AllowMarginComment: false
L   # bad
L 
L   #
L   # Description of `Foo` class.
L   #
L   class Foo
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLineAfterGuardClause
S Details
L Enforces empty line after guard clause.
L 
L This cop allows a SimpleCov directive comment after guard clause because
L SimpleCov excludes code from the coverage report by wrapping it in such directives.
L Both the legacy `# :nocov:` comment and the newer `# simplecov:disable` /
L `# simplecov:enable` comments are recognized:
L 
L [source,ruby]
L ----
L def foo
L   # :nocov:
L   return if condition
L   # :nocov:
L   bar
L end
L 
L def foo
L   # simplecov:disable
L   return if condition
L   # simplecov:enable
L   bar
L end
L ----
L 
L Refer to SimpleCov's documentation for more details:
L https://github.com/simplecov-ruby/simplecov#ignoringskipping-code
S Example
L   # bad
L   def foo
L     return if need_return?
L     bar
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo
L     return if need_return?
L 
L     bar
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo
L     return if something?
L     return if something_different?
L 
L     bar
L   end
L 
L   # also good
L   def foo
L     if something?
L       do_something
L       return if need_return?
L     end
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLineAfterMagicComment
S Details
L Checks for a newline after the final magic comment.
L 
L `NumberOfEmptyLines` configures the minimum number of empty lines required. Set it
L to `2` when using YARD, which otherwise treats the magic comments as documentation
L for the first module or class in the file.
L 
L NOTE: `Layout/EmptyLines` has to be disabled for values greater than `1`, as it
L removes the extra empty lines this cop adds, and autocorrecting with both enabled
L loops between them.
S Example
L   # good
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
L 
L   # Some documentation for Person
L   class Person
L     # Some code
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
L   # Some documentation for Person
L   class Person
L     # Some code
L   end
S Example: NumberOfEmptyLines: 1 (default)
L   # good
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
L 
L   class Person
L   end
S Example: NumberOfEmptyLines: 2
L   # bad
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
L 
L   class Person
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
L 
L 
L   class Person
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLineAfterMultilineCondition
S Details
L Enforces empty line after multiline condition.
S Example
L   # bad
L   if multiline &&
L     condition
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if multiline &&
L     condition
L 
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   case x
L   when foo,
L     bar
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   case x
L   when foo,
L     bar
L 
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   begin
L     do_something
L   rescue FooError,
L     BarError
L     handle_error
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   begin
L     do_something
L   rescue FooError,
L     BarError
L 
L     handle_error
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLineBetweenDefs
S Details
L Checks whether class/module/method definitions are
L separated by one or more empty lines.
L 
L `NumberOfEmptyLines` can be an integer (default is 1) or
L an array (e.g. [1, 2]) to specify a minimum and maximum
L number of empty lines permitted.
L 
L `AllowAdjacentOneLineDefs` configures whether adjacent
L one-line definitions are considered an offense.
S Example: EmptyLineBetweenMethodDefs: true (default)
L   # checks for empty lines between method definitions.
L 
L   # bad
L   def a
L   end
L   def b
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def a
L   end
L 
L   def b
L   end
S Example: EmptyLineBetweenClassDefs: true (default)
L   # checks for empty lines between class definitions.
L 
L   # bad
L   class A
L   end
L   class B
L   end
L   def b
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class A
L   end
L 
L   class B
L   end
L 
L   def b
L   end
S Example: EmptyLineBetweenModuleDefs: true (default)
L   # checks for empty lines between module definitions.
L 
L   # bad
L   module A
L   end
L   module B
L   end
L   def b
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   module A
L   end
L 
L   module B
L   end
L 
L   def b
L   end
S Example: AllowAdjacentOneLineDefs: true (default)
L   # good
L   class ErrorA < BaseError; end
L   class ErrorB < BaseError; end
L 
L   # good
L   class ErrorA < BaseError; end
L 
L   class ErrorB < BaseError; end
L 
L   # good - DefLikeMacros: [memoize]
L   memoize :attribute_a
L   memoize :attribute_b
L 
L   # good
L   memoize :attribute_a
L 
L   memoize :attribute_b
S Example: AllowAdjacentOneLineDefs: false
L   # bad
L   class ErrorA < BaseError; end
L   class ErrorB < BaseError; end
L 
L   # good
L   class ErrorA < BaseError; end
L 
L   class ErrorB < BaseError; end
L 
L   # bad - DefLikeMacros: [memoize]
L   memoize :attribute_a
L   memoize :attribute_b
L 
L   # good
L   memoize :attribute_a
L 
L   memoize :attribute_b
C Layout/EmptyLines
S Details
L Checks for two or more consecutive blank lines.
S Example
L   # bad - It has two empty lines.
L   some_method
L   # one empty line
L   # two empty lines
L   some_method
L 
L   # good
L   some_method
L   # one empty line
L   some_method
C Layout/EmptyLinesAfterModuleInclusion
S Details
L Checks for an empty line after a module inclusion method (`extend`,
L `include` and `prepend`), or a group of them.
S Example
L   # bad
L   class Foo
L     include Bar
L     attr_reader :baz
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo
L     include Bar
L 
L     attr_reader :baz
L   end
L 
L   # also good - multiple module inclusions grouped together
L   class Foo
L     extend Bar
L     include Baz
L     prepend Qux
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundAccessModifier
S Details
L Access modifiers should be surrounded by blank lines.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: around (default)
L   # bad
L   class Foo
L     def bar; end
L     private
L     def baz; end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo
L     def bar; end
L 
L     private
L 
L     def baz; end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: only_before
L   # bad
L   class Foo
L     def bar; end
L     private
L     def baz; end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo
L     def bar; end
L 
L     private
L     def baz; end
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundArguments
S Details
L Checks if empty lines exist around the arguments
L of a method invocation.
S Example
L   # bad
L   do_something(
L     foo
L 
L   )
L 
L   process(bar,
L 
L           baz: qux,
L           thud: fred)
L 
L   some_method(
L 
L     [1,2,3],
L     x: y
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   do_something(
L     foo
L   )
L 
L   process(bar,
L           baz: qux,
L           thud: fred)
L 
L   some_method(
L     [1,2,3],
L     x: y
L   )
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundAttributeAccessor
S Details
L Checks for a newline after an attribute accessor or a group of them.
L `alias` syntax and `alias_method`, `public`, `protected`, and `private` methods are allowed
L by default. These are customizable with `AllowAliasSyntax` and `AllowedMethods` options.
S Example
L   # bad
L   attr_accessor :foo
L   def do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   attr_accessor :foo
L 
L   def do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   attr_accessor :foo
L   attr_reader :bar
L   attr_writer :baz
L   attr :qux
L 
L   def do_something
L   end
S Example: AllowAliasSyntax: true (default)
L   # good
L   attr_accessor :foo
L   alias :foo? :foo
L 
L   def do_something
L   end
S Example: AllowAliasSyntax: false
L   # bad
L   attr_accessor :foo
L   alias :foo? :foo
L 
L   def do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   attr_accessor :foo
L 
L   alias :foo? :foo
L 
L   def do_something
L   end
S Example: AllowedMethods: ['private']
L   # good
L   attr_accessor :foo
L   private :foo
L 
L   def do_something
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundBeginBody
S Details
L Checks if empty lines exist around the bodies of begin-end
L blocks.
S Example
L   # bad
L   begin
L 
L     # ...
L 
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   begin
L     # ...
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundBlockBody
S Details
L Checks if empty lines around the bodies of blocks match
L the configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_empty_lines (default)
L   # bad
L   foo do |bar|
L 
L     # ...
L 
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   foo do |bar|
L     # ...
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: empty_lines
L   # bad
L   foo do |bar|
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   foo do |bar|
L 
L     # ...
L 
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundClassBody
S Details
L Checks if empty lines around the bodies of classes match
L the configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_empty_lines (default)
L   # bad
L   class Foo
L 
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: empty_lines
L   # bad
L   class Foo
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo
L 
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: empty_lines_except_namespace
L   # good
L 
L   class Foo
L     class Bar
L 
L       # ...
L 
L     end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: empty_lines_special
L   # good
L   class Foo
L 
L     def bar; end
L 
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: beginning_only
L   # good
L 
L   class Foo
L 
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: ending_only
L   # good
L 
L   class Foo
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundExceptionHandlingKeywords
S Details
L Checks if empty lines exist around the bodies of `begin`
L sections. This cop doesn't check empty lines at `begin` body
L beginning/end and around method definition body.
L `Layout/EmptyLinesAroundBeginBody` or `Layout/EmptyLinesAroundMethodBody`
L can be used for this purpose.
S Example
L   # good
L 
L   begin
L     do_something
L   rescue
L     do_something2
L   else
L     do_something3
L   ensure
L     do_something4
L   end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   def foo
L     do_something
L   rescue
L     do_something2
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   begin
L     do_something
L 
L   rescue
L 
L     do_something2
L 
L   else
L 
L     do_something3
L 
L   ensure
L 
L     do_something4
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   def foo
L     do_something
L 
L   rescue
L 
L     do_something2
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundMethodBody
S Details
L Checks if empty lines exist around the bodies of methods.
S Example
L   # good
L

L     # ...
L   end

L 
L   def bar
L 
L     # ...
L 
L   end
C Layout/EmptyLinesAroundModuleBody
S Details
L Checks if empty lines around the bodies of modules match
L the configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_empty_lines (default)
L   # bad
L   module Foo
L 
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   module Foo

L       # ...
L     end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: empty_lines
L   # bad
L   module Foo
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   module Foo
L 
L     def bar
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: empty_lines_except_namespace
L   # good

L     module Bar
L 
L       # ...
L 
L     end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: empty_lines_special
L   # good
L   module Foo
L 
L     def bar; end
L 
L   end
C Layout/EndAlignment
S Details
L Checks whether the end keywords are aligned properly.
L 
L Three modes are supported through the `EnforcedStyleAlignWith`
L configuration parameter:
L 
L If it's set to `keyword` (which is the default), the `end`
L shall be aligned with the start of the keyword (if, class, etc.).
L 
L If it's set to `variable` the `end` shall be aligned with the
L left-hand-side of the variable assignment, if there is one.
L 
L If it's set to `start_of_line`, the `end` shall be aligned with the
L start of the line where the matching keyword appears.
L 
L This `Layout/EndAlignment` cop aligns with keywords (e.g. `if`, `while`, `case`)
L by default. On the other hand, `Layout/BeginEndAlignment` cop aligns with
L `EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line` by default because `||= begin` tends
L to align with the start of the line. `Layout/DefEndAlignment` cop also aligns with
L `EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line` by default.
L These styles can be configured by each cop.
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: keyword (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   variable = if true
L       end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   variable = if true
L              end
L 
L   variable =
L     if true
L     end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: variable
L   # bad
L 
L   variable = if true
L       end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   variable = if true
L   end
L 
L   variable =
L     if true
L     end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line
L   # bad
L 
L   variable = if true
L       end
L 
L   puts(if true
L        end)
L 
L   # good
L 
L   variable = if true
L   end
L 
L   puts(if true
L   end)
L 
L   variable =
L     if true
L     end
C Layout/EndOfLine
S Details
L Checks for Windows-style line endings in the source code.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: native (default)
L   # The `native` style means that CR+LF (Carriage Return + Line Feed) is
L   # enforced on Windows, and LF is enforced on other platforms.
L 
L   # bad
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is LF on Windows.
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is CR+LF on other than Windows.
L 
L   # good
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is CR+LF on Windows.
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is LF on other than Windows.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: lf
L   # The `lf` style means that LF (Line Feed) is enforced on
L   # all platforms.
L 
L   # bad
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is CR+LF on all platforms.
L 
L   # good
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is LF on all platforms.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: crlf
L   # The `crlf` style means that CR+LF (Carriage Return + Line Feed) is
L   # enforced on all platforms.
L 
L   # bad
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is LF on all platforms.
L 
L   # good
L   puts 'Hello' # Return character is CR+LF on all platforms.
C Layout/ExtraSpacing
S Details
L Checks for extra/unnecessary whitespace.
S Example
L   # good if AllowForAlignment is true
L   name      = "RuboCop"
L   # Some comment and an empty line
L 
L   website  += "/rubocop/rubocop" unless cond
L   puts        "rubocop"          if     debug
L 
L   # bad for any configuration
L   set_app("RuboCop")
L   website  = "https://github.com/rubocop/rubocop"
L 
L   # good only if AllowBeforeTrailingComments is true
L   object.method(arg)  # this is a comment
L 
L   # good even if AllowBeforeTrailingComments is false or not set
L   object.method(arg) # this is a comment
L 
L   # good with either AllowBeforeTrailingComments or AllowForAlignment
L   object.method(arg)         # this is a comment
L   another_object.method(arg) # this is another comment
L   some_object.method(arg)    # this is some comment
C Layout/FirstArgumentIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the first argument in a method call.
L Arguments after the first one are checked by `Layout/ArgumentAlignment`,
L not by this cop.
L 
L For indenting the first parameter of method _definitions_, check out
L `Layout/FirstParameterIndentation`.
L 
L This cop will respect `Layout/ArgumentAlignment` and will not work when
L `EnforcedStyle: with_fixed_indentation` is specified for `Layout/ArgumentAlignment`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   some_method(
L   first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L   first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(nested_call(
L   nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L   nested_call(
L   nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   some_method nested_call(
L   nested_first_param),
L   second_param
S Example: EnforcedStyle: special_for_inner_method_call_in_parentheses (default)
L   # Same as `special_for_inner_method_call` except that the special rule
L   # only applies if the outer method call encloses its arguments in
L   # parentheses.
L 
L   # good
L   some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(nested_call(
L                       nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L     nested_call(
L       nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   some_method nested_call(
L     nested_first_param),
L   second_param
S Example: EnforcedStyle: consistent
L   # The first argument should always be indented one step more than the
L   # preceding line.
L 
L   # good
L   some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(nested_call(
L     nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L     nested_call(
L       nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   some_method nested_call(
L     nested_first_param),
L   second_param
S Example: EnforcedStyle: consistent_relative_to_receiver
L   # The first argument should always be indented one level relative to
L   # the parent that is receiving the argument
L 
L   # good
L   some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L           first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(nested_call(
L                       nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L           nested_call(
L             nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   some_method nested_call(
L                 nested_first_param),
L   second_params
S Example: EnforcedStyle: special_for_inner_method_call
L   # The first argument should normally be indented one step more than
L   # the preceding line, but if it's an argument for a method call that
L   # is itself an argument in a method call, then the inner argument
L   # should be indented relative to the inner method.
L 
L   # good
L   some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(nested_call(
L                       nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   foo = some_method(
L     nested_call(
L       nested_first_param),
L   second_param)
L 
L   some_method nested_call(
L                 nested_first_param),
L   second_param
C Layout/FirstArrayElementIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the first element in an array literal
L where the opening bracket and the first element are on separate lines.
L The other elements' indentations are handled by `Layout/ArrayAlignment` cop.
L 
L This cop will respect `Layout/ArrayAlignment` and will not work when
L `EnforcedStyle: with_fixed_indentation` is specified for `Layout/ArrayAlignment`.
L 
L By default, array literals that are arguments in a method call with
L parentheses, and where the opening square bracket of the array is on the
L same line as the opening parenthesis of the method call, shall have
L their first element indented one step (two spaces) more than the
L position inside the opening parenthesis.
L 
L Other array literals shall have their first element indented one step
L more than the start of the line where the opening square bracket is.
L 
L This default style is called 'special_inside_parentheses'. Alternative
L styles are 'consistent' and 'align_brackets'. Here are examples:
S Example: EnforcedStyle: special_inside_parentheses (default)
L   # The `special_inside_parentheses` style enforces that the first
L   # element in an array literal where the opening bracket and first
L   # element are on separate lines is indented one step (two spaces) more
L   # than the position inside the opening parenthesis.
L 
L   # bad
L   array = [
L     :value
L   ]
L   and_in_a_method_call([
L     :no_difference
L                        ])
L 
L   # good
L   array = [
L     :value
L   ]
L   but_in_a_method_call([
L                          :its_like_this
L                        ])
S Example: EnforcedStyle: consistent
L   # The `consistent` style enforces that the first element in an array
L   # literal where the opening bracket and the first element are on
L   # separate lines is indented the same as an array literal which is not
L   # defined inside a method call.
L 
L   # bad
L   array = [
L     :value
L   ]
L   but_in_a_method_call([
L                          :its_like_this
L   ])
L 
L   # good
L   array = [
L     :value
L   ]
L   and_in_a_method_call([
L     :no_difference
L   ])
S Example: EnforcedStyle: align_brackets
L   # The `align_brackets` style enforces that the opening and closing
L   # brackets are indented to the same position.
L 
L   # bad
L   and_now_for_something = [
L                             :completely_different
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   and_now_for_something = [
L                             :completely_different
L                           ]
C Layout/FirstArrayElementLineBreak
S Details
L Checks for a line break before the first element in a
L multi-line array.
L 
L NOTE: An array whose first element is an array literal is not checked when
L `Layout/SpaceInsideArrayLiteralBrackets` is enabled with `EnforcedStyle: compact`,
L since that style joins the two opening brackets.
S Example
L   # bad
L   [ :a,
L     :b]
L 
L   # good
L   [
L     :a,
L     :b]
L 
L   # good
L   [:a, :b]
S Example: AllowImplicitArrayLiterals: false (default)
L   # bad
L   a = b,
L       c
L 
L   # good
L   a =
L     b,
L     c
S Example: AllowImplicitArrayLiterals: true
L   # good
L   a = b,
L       c
L 
L   a =
L     b,
L     c
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   [ :a, {
L     :b => :c
L   }]
L 
L   # good
L   [
L     :a, {
L     :b => :c
L   }]
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # good
L   [:a, {
L     :b => :c
L   }]
C Layout/FirstHashElementIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the first key in a hash literal
L where the opening brace and the first key are on separate lines. The
L other keys' indentations are handled by the HashAlignment cop.
L 
L By default, `Hash` literals that are arguments in a method call with
L parentheses, and where the opening curly brace of the hash is on the
L same line as the opening parenthesis of the method call, shall have
L their first key indented one step (two spaces) more than the position
L inside the opening parenthesis.
L 
L Other hash literals shall have their first key indented one step more
L than the start of the line where the opening curly brace is.
L 
L This default style is called 'special_inside_parentheses'. Alternative
L styles are 'consistent' and 'align_braces'. Here are examples:
S Example: EnforcedStyle: special_inside_parentheses (default)
L   # The `special_inside_parentheses` style enforces that the first key
L   # in a hash literal where the opening brace and the first key are on
L   # separate lines is indented one step (two spaces) more than the
L   # position inside the opening parentheses.
L 
L   # bad
L   hash = {
L     key: :value
L          }
L   in_a_method_call({
L     foo: :bar
L   })
L   takes_multi_pairs_hash(x: {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   },
