L                          y: {
L                            c: 1,
L                            d: 2
L                          })
L 
L   # good
L   hash = {
L     key: :value
L   }
L   in_a_method_call({
L                      foo: :bar
L                    })
L   takes_multi_pairs_hash(x: {
L                            a: 1,
L                            b: 2
L                          },
L                          y: {
L                            c: 1,
L                            d: 2
L                          })
S Example: EnforcedStyle: consistent
L   # The `consistent` style enforces that the first key in a hash
L   # literal where the opening brace and the first key are on
L   # separate lines is indented the same as a hash literal which is not
L   # defined inside a method call.
L 
L   # bad
L   hash = {
L     key: :value
L          }
L   in_a_method_call({
L                     foo: :bar
L                   })
L 
L   # good
L   hash = {
L     key: :value
L   }
L   in_a_method_call({
L     foo: :bar
L   })
S Example: EnforcedStyle: align_braces
L   # The `align_brackets` style enforces that the opening and closing
L   # braces are indented to the same position.
L 
L   # bad
L   and_now_for_something = {
L                             completely: :different
L   }
L   takes_multi_pairs_hash(x: {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   },
L                           y: {
L                                c: 1,
L                                d: 2
L                              })
L 
L   # good
L   and_now_for_something = {
L                             completely: :different
L                           }
L   takes_multi_pairs_hash(x: {
L                               a: 1,
L                               b: 2
L                             },
L                          y: {
L                               c: 1,
L                               d: 2
L                             })
C Layout/FirstHashElementLineBreak
S Details
L Checks for a line break before the first element in a
L multi-line hash.
S Example
L   # bad
L   { a: 1,
L     b: 2}
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2 }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     a: 1, b: {
L     c: 3
L   }}
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   { a: 1, b: {
L     c: 3
L   }}
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # bad
L   { a: 1,
L     b: {
L     c: 3
L   }}
L 
L   # good
L   { a: 1, b: {
L     c: 3
L   }}
C Layout/FirstMethodArgumentLineBreak
S Details
L Checks for a line break before the first argument in a
L multi-line method call.
S Example
L   # bad
L   method(foo, bar,
L     baz)
L 
L   # good
L   method(
L     foo, bar,
L     baz)
L 
L     # ignored
L     method foo, bar,
L       baz
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   method(foo, bar, {
L     baz: "a",
L     qux: "b",
L   })
L 
L   # good
L   method(
L     foo, bar, {
L     baz: "a",
L     qux: "b",
L   })
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # bad
L   method(foo,
L     bar,
L     {
L       baz: "a",
L       qux: "b",
L     }
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   method(foo, bar, {
L     baz: "a",
L     qux: "b",
L   })
L 
L   # good
L   method(
L     foo,
L     bar,
L     {
L       baz: "a",
L       qux: "b",
L     }
L   )
S Example: AllowedMethods: ['some_method']
L   # good
L   some_method(foo, bar,
L     baz)
C Layout/FirstMethodParameterLineBreak
S Details
L Checks for a line break before the first parameter in a
L multi-line method parameter definition.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def method(foo, bar,
L       baz)
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def method(
L       foo, bar,
L       baz)
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # ignored
L   def method foo,
L       bar
L     do_something
L   end
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   def method(foo, bar, baz = {
L     :a => "b",
L   })
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def method(
L     foo, bar, baz = {
L     :a => "b",
L   })
L     do_something
L   end
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # good
L   def method(foo, bar, baz = {
L     :a => "b",
L   })
L     do_something
L   end
C Layout/FirstParameterIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the first parameter in a method
L definition. Parameters after the first one are checked by
L `Layout/ParameterAlignment`, not by this cop.
L 
L For indenting the first argument of method _calls_, check out
L `Layout/FirstArgumentIndentation`, which supports options related to
L nesting that are irrelevant for method _definitions_.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def some_method(
L   first_param,
L   second_param)
L     123
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: consistent (default)
L   # The first parameter should always be indented one step more than the
L   # preceding line.
L 
L   # good
L   def some_method(
L     first_param,
L   second_param)
L     123
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: align_parentheses
L   # The first parameter should always be indented one step more than the
L   # opening parenthesis.
L 
L   # good
L   def some_method(
L                    first_param,
L   second_param)
L     123
L   end
C Layout/HashAlignment
S Details
L Checks that the keys, separators, and values of a multi-line hash
L literal are aligned according to configuration. The configuration
L options are:
L 
L * key (left align keys, one space before hash rockets and values)
L * separator (align hash rockets and colons, right align keys)
L * table (left align keys, hash rockets, and values)
L 
L The treatment of hashes passed as the last argument to a method call
L can also be configured. The options are:
L 
L * always_inspect
L * always_ignore
L * ignore_implicit (without curly braces)
L 
L Alternatively you can specify multiple allowed styles. That's done by
L passing a list of styles to EnforcedHashRocketStyle and EnforcedColonStyle.
S Example: EnforcedHashRocketStyle: key (default)
L   # bad
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L      :ba => baz
L   }
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L     :ba  => baz
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L     :ba => baz
L   }
S Example: EnforcedHashRocketStyle: separator
L   # bad
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L     :ba => baz
L   }
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L     :ba  => baz
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L      :ba => baz
L   }
S Example: EnforcedHashRocketStyle: table
L   # bad
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L      :ba => baz
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     :foo => bar,
L     :ba  => baz
L   }
S Example: EnforcedColonStyle: key (default)
L   # bad
L   {
L     foo: bar,
L      ba: baz
L   }
L   {
L     foo: bar,
L     ba:  baz
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     foo: bar,
L     ba: baz
L   }
S Example: EnforcedColonStyle: separator
L   # bad
L   {
L     foo: bar,
L     ba: baz
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     foo: bar,
L      ba: baz
L   }
S Example: EnforcedColonStyle: table
L   # bad
L   {
L     foo: bar,
L     ba: baz
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     foo: bar,
L     ba:  baz
L   }
S Example: EnforcedLastArgumentHashStyle: always_inspect (default)
L   # Inspect both implicit and explicit hashes.
L 
L   # bad
L   do_something(foo: 1,
L     bar: 2)
L 
L   # bad
L   do_something({foo: 1,
L     bar: 2})
L 
L   # good
L   do_something(foo: 1,
L                bar: 2)
L 
L   # good
L   do_something(
L     foo: 1,
L     bar: 2
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   do_something({foo: 1,
L                 bar: 2})
L 
L   # good
L   do_something({
L     foo: 1,
L     bar: 2
L   })
S Example: EnforcedLastArgumentHashStyle: always_ignore
L   # Ignore both implicit and explicit hashes.
L 
L   # good
L   do_something(foo: 1,
L     bar: 2)
L 
L   # good
L   do_something({foo: 1,
L     bar: 2})
S Example: EnforcedLastArgumentHashStyle: ignore_implicit
L   # Ignore only implicit hashes.
L 
L   # bad
L   do_something({foo: 1,
L     bar: 2})
L 
L   # good
L   do_something(foo: 1,
L     bar: 2)
S Example: EnforcedLastArgumentHashStyle: ignore_explicit
L   # Ignore only explicit hashes.
L 
L   # bad
L   do_something(foo: 1,
L     bar: 2)
L 
L   # good
L   do_something({foo: 1,
L     bar: 2})
C Layout/HeredocArgumentClosingParenthesis
S Details
L Checks for the placement of the closing parenthesis
L in a method call that passes a HEREDOC string as an argument.
L It should be placed at the end of the line containing the
L opening HEREDOC tag.
S Example
L   # bad
L 
L      foo(<<-SQL
L        bar
L      SQL
L      )
L 
L      foo(<<-SQL, 123, <<-NOSQL,
L        bar
L      SQL
L        baz
L      NOSQL
L      )
L 
L      foo(
L        bar(<<-SQL
L          baz
L        SQL
L        ),
L        123,
L      )
L 
L   # good
L 
L      foo(<<-SQL)
L        bar
L      SQL
L 
L      foo(<<-SQL, 123, <<-NOSQL)
L        bar
L      SQL
L        baz
L      NOSQL
L 
L      foo(
L        bar(<<-SQL),
L          baz
L        SQL
L        123,
L      )
C Layout/HeredocIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the here document bodies. The bodies
L are indented one step.
L 
L NOTE: When ``Layout/LineLength``'s `AllowHeredoc` is false (not default),
L       this cop does not add any offenses for long here documents to
L       avoid ``Layout/LineLength``'s offenses.
S Example
L   # bad
L   <<-RUBY
L   something
L   RUBY
L 
L   # good
L   <<~RUBY
L     something
L   RUBY
S Example: AllCops:ActiveSupportExtensionsEnabled: false (default)
L   # good
L   <<-RUBY.squish
L       something
L   RUBY
S Example: AllCops:ActiveSupportExtensionsEnabled: true
L   # bad
L   <<-RUBY.squish
L       something
L   RUBY
L 
L   # good
L   <<~RUBY.squish
L     something
L   RUBY
C Layout/IndentationConsistency
S Details
L Checks for inconsistent indentation.
L 
L The difference between `indented_internal_methods` and `normal` is
L that the `indented_internal_methods` style prescribes that in
L classes and modules the `protected` and `private` modifier keywords
L shall be indented the same as public methods and that protected and
L private members shall be indented one step more than the modifiers.
L Other than that, both styles mean that entities on the same logical
L depth shall have the same indentation.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: normal (default)
L   # bad
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L        puts 'world'
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L       puts 'world'
L     end
L 
L     protected
L 
L       def foo
L       end
L 
L     private
L 
L       def bar
L       end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L       puts 'world'
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L       puts 'world'
L     end
L 
L     protected
L 
L     def foo
L     end
L 
L     private
L 
L     def bar
L     end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: indented_internal_methods
L   # bad
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L        puts 'world'
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L       puts 'world'
L     end
L 
L     protected
L 
L     def foo
L     end
L 
L     private
L 
L     def bar
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L       puts 'world'
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L       puts 'world'
L     end
L 
L     protected
L 
L       def foo
L       end
L 
L     private
L 
L       def bar
L       end
L   end
C Layout/IndentationStyle
S Details
L Checks that the indentation method is consistent.
L Either tabs only or spaces only are used for indentation.
L 
L With `EnforcedStyle: tabs`, spaces that follow the indenting tabs are
L taken to be alignment rather than indentation, and are left alone.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: spaces (default)
L   # bad
L   # This example uses a tab to indent bar.
L   def foo
L     bar
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # This example uses spaces to indent bar.
L   def foo
L     bar
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: tabs
L   # bad
L   # This example uses spaces to indent bar.
L   def foo
L     bar
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # This example uses a tab to indent bar.
L   def foo
L     bar
L   end
C Layout/IndentationWidth
S Details
L Checks for indentation that doesn't use the specified number of spaces.
L The indentation width can be configured using the `Width` setting. The default width is 2.
L The block body indentation for method chain blocks can be configured using the
L `EnforcedStyleAlignWith` setting.
L 
L See also the `Layout/IndentationConsistency` cop which is the companion to this one.
S Example: Width: 2 (default)
L   # bad
L   class A
L    def test
L     puts 'hello'
L    end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class A
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L     end
L   end
S Example
L   # bad
L   value = (
L   foo - bar
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   value = (
L     foo - bar
L   )
S Example: AllowedPatterns: ['^\s*module']
L   # bad
L   module A
L   class B
L     def test
L     puts 'hello'
L     end
L   end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   module A
L   class B
L     def test
L       puts 'hello'
L     end
L   end
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: start_of_line (default)
L   # good
L   records.uniq { |el| el[:profile_id] }
L          .map do |message|
L     SomeJob.perform_later(message[:id])
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyleAlignWith: relative_to_receiver
L   # good
L   records.uniq { |el| el[:profile_id] }
L          .map do |message|
L            SomeJob.perform_later(message[:id])
L          end
C Layout/InitialIndentation
S Details
L Checks for indentation of the first non-blank non-comment
L line in a file.
S Example
L   # bad
L      class A
L        def foo; end
L      end
L 
L   # good
L   class A
L     def foo; end
L   end
C Layout/LeadingCommentSpace
S Details
L Checks whether comments have a leading space after the
L `#` denoting the start of the comment. The leading space is not
L required for some RDoc special syntax, like `#++`, `#--`,
L `#:nodoc`, `=begin`- and `=end` comments, "shebang" directives,
L or rackup options.
S Example
L   # bad
L   #Some comment
L 
L   # good
L   # Some comment
S Example: AllowDoxygenCommentStyle: false (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   #**
L   # Some comment
L   # Another line of comment
L   #*
S Example: AllowDoxygenCommentStyle: true
L   # good
L 
L   #**
L   # Some comment
L   # Another line of comment
L   #*
S Example: AllowGemfileRubyComment: false (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   #ruby=2.7.0
L   #ruby-gemset=myproject
S Example: AllowGemfileRubyComment: true
L   # good
L 
L   #ruby=2.7.0
L   #ruby-gemset=myproject
S Example: AllowRBSInlineAnnotation: false (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   include Enumerable #[Integer]
L 
L   attr_reader :name #: String
L   attr_reader :age  #: Integer?
L 
L   #: (
L   #|   Integer,
L   #|   String
L   #| ) -> void
L   def foo; end
S Example: AllowRBSInlineAnnotation: true
L   # good
L 
L   include Enumerable #[Integer]
L 
L   attr_reader :name #: String
L   attr_reader :age  #: Integer?
L 
L   #: (
L   #|   Integer,
L   #|   String
L   #| ) -> void
L   def foo; end
S Example: AllowSteepAnnotation: false (default)
L   # bad
L   [1, 2, 3].each_with_object([]) do |n, list| #$ Array[Integer]
L     list << n
L   end
L 
L   name = 'John'      #: String
S Example: AllowSteepAnnotation: true
L   # good
L 
L   [1, 2, 3].each_with_object([]) do |n, list| #$ Array[Integer]
L     list << n
L   end
L 
L   name = 'John'      #: String
S Example: AllowYARDCommentBlockSeparator: false (default)
L   # bad
L 
L   # Copyright (c) Example Corp
L   #-
L   class Client
L   end
S Example: AllowYARDCommentBlockSeparator: true
L   # good
L 
L   # Copyright (c) Example Corp
L   #-
L   class Client
L   end
C Layout/LeadingEmptyLines
S Details
L Checks for unnecessary leading blank lines at the beginning
L of a file.
S Example
L   # bad
L   # (start of file)
L 
L   class Foo
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   # (start of file)
L 
L   # a comment
L 
L   # good
L   # (start of file)
L   class Foo
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # (start of file)
L   # a comment
C Layout/LineContinuationLeadingSpace
S Details
L Checks that strings broken over multiple lines (by a backslash) contain
L trailing spaces instead of leading spaces (default) or leading spaces
L instead of trailing spaces.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: trailing (default)
L   # bad
L   'this text contains a lot of' \
L   '               spaces'
L 
L   # good
L   'this text contains a lot of               ' \
L   'spaces'
L 
L   # bad
L   'this text is too' \
L   ' long'
L 
L   # good
L   'this text is too ' \
L   'long'
S Example: EnforcedStyle: leading
L   # bad
L   'this text contains a lot of               ' \
L   'spaces'
L 
L   # good
L   'this text contains a lot of' \
L   '               spaces'
L 
L   # bad
L   'this text is too ' \
L   'long'
L 
L   # good
L   'this text is too' \
L   ' long'
C Layout/LineContinuationSpacing
S Details
L Checks that the backslash of a line continuation is separated from
L preceding text by exactly one space (default) or zero spaces.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space (default)
L   # bad
L   'a'\
L   'b'  \
L   'c'
L 
L   # good
L   'a' \
L   'b' \
L   'c'
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space
L   # bad
L   'a' \
L   'b'  \
L   'c'
L 
L   # good
L   'a'\
L   'b'\
L   'c'
C Layout/LineEndStringConcatenationIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the next line after a line that ends with a string
L literal and a backslash.
L 
L If `EnforcedStyle: aligned` is set, the concatenated string parts shall be aligned with the
L first part. There are some exceptions, such as implicit return values, where the
L concatenated string parts shall be indented regardless of `EnforcedStyle` configuration.
L 
L If `EnforcedStyle: indented` is set, it's the second line that shall be indented one step
L more than the first line. Lines 3 and forward shall be aligned with line 2.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def some_method
L     'x' \
L     'y' \
L     'z'
L   end
L 
L   my_hash = {
L     first: 'a message' \
L       'in two parts'
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   def some_method
L     'x' \
L       'y' \
L       'z'
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: aligned (default)
L   # bad
L   puts 'x' \
L     'y'
L 
L   my_hash = {
L     first: 'a message' \
L       'in two parts'
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   puts 'x' \
L        'y'
L 
L   my_hash = {
L     first: 'a message' \
L            'in two parts'
L   }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: indented
L   # bad
L   result = 'x' \
L            'y'
L 
L   my_hash = {
L     first: 'a message' \
L            'in two parts'
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   result = 'x' \
L     'y'
L 
L   my_hash = {
L     first: 'a message' \
L       'in two parts'
L   }
C Layout/LineLength
S Details
L Checks the length of lines in the source code.
L The maximum length is configurable.
L The tab size is configured in the `IndentationWidth`
L of the `Layout/IndentationStyle` cop.
L It also ignores a shebang line by default.
L 
L This cop has some autocorrection capabilities.
L It can programmatically shorten certain long lines by
L inserting line breaks into expressions that can be safely
L split across lines. These include arrays, hashes, and
L method calls with argument lists.
L 
L If autocorrection is enabled, the following cops
L are recommended to further format the broken lines.
L (Many of these are enabled by default.)
L 
L * `Layout/ArgumentAlignment`
L * `Layout/ArrayAlignment`
L * `Layout/BlockAlignment`
L * `Layout/BlockEndNewline`
L * `Layout/ClosingParenthesisIndentation`
L * `Layout/FirstArgumentIndentation`
L * `Layout/FirstArrayElementIndentation`
L * `Layout/FirstHashElementIndentation`
L * `Layout/FirstParameterIndentation`
L * `Layout/HashAlignment`
L * `Layout/IndentationWidth`
L * `Layout/MultilineArrayLineBreaks`
L * `Layout/MultilineBlockLayout`
L * `Layout/MultilineHashBraceLayout`
L * `Layout/MultilineHashKeyLineBreaks`
L * `Layout/MultilineMethodArgumentLineBreaks`
L * `Layout/MultilineMethodParameterLineBreaks`
L * `Layout/ParameterAlignment`
L * `Style/BlockDelimiters`
L 
L Together, these cops will pretty print hashes, arrays,
L method calls, etc. For example, let's say the max columns
L is 25:
S Example
L   # bad
L   {foo: "0000000000", bar: "0000000000", baz: "0000000000"}
L 
L   # good
L   {foo: "0000000000",
L   bar: "0000000000", baz: "0000000000"}
L 
L   # good (with recommended cops enabled)
L   {
L     foo: "0000000000",
L     bar: "0000000000",
L     baz: "0000000000",
L   }
C Layout/MultilineArrayBraceLayout
S Details
L Checks that the closing brace in an array literal is either
L on the same line as the last array element or on a new line.
L 
L When using the `symmetrical` (default) style:
L 
L If an array's opening brace is on the same line as the first element
L of the array, then the closing brace should be on the same line as
L the last element of the array.
L 
L If an array's opening brace is on the line above the first element
L of the array, then the closing brace should be on the line below
L the last element of the array.
L 
L When using the `new_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line array literal must be on the line
L after the last element of the array.
L 
L When using the `same_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line array literal must be on the same
L line as the last element of the array.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: symmetrical (default)
L   # bad
L   [ :a,
L     :b
L   ]
L 
L   # bad
L   [
L     :a,
L     :b ]
L 
L   # good
L   [ :a,
L     :b ]
L 
L   # good
L   [
L     :a,
L     :b
L   ]
S Example: EnforcedStyle: new_line
L   # bad
L   [
L     :a,
L     :b ]
L 
L   # bad
L   [ :a,
L     :b ]
L 
L   # good
L   [ :a,
L     :b
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   [
L     :a,
L     :b
L   ]
S Example: EnforcedStyle: same_line
L   # bad
L   [ :a,
L     :b
L   ]
L 
L   # bad
L   [
L     :a,
L     :b
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   [
L     :a,
L     :b ]
L 
L   # good
L   [ :a,
L     :b ]
C Layout/MultilineArrayLineBreaks
S Details
L Ensures that each item in a multi-line array
L starts on a separate line.
S Example
L   # bad
L   [
L     a, b,
L     c
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   [
L     a,
L     b,
L     c
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   [
L     a,
L     b,
L     foo(
L       bar
L     )
L   ]
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   [a, b, foo(
L     bar
L   )]
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # good
L   [a, b, foo(
L     bar
L   )]
C Layout/MultilineAssignmentLayout
S Details
L Checks whether the multiline assignments have a newline
L after the assignment operator.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: new_line (default)
L   # bad
L   foo = if expression
L     'bar'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   foo =
L     if expression
L       'bar'
L     end
L 
L   # good
L   foo =
L     begin
L       compute
L     rescue => e
L       nil
L     end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: same_line
L   # good
L   foo = if expression
L     'bar'
L   end
S Example: SupportedTypes: ['block', 'case', 'class', 'if', 'kwbegin', 'module'] (default)
L   # good
L   foo =
L     if expression
L       'bar'
L     end
L 
L   # good
L   foo =
L     [1].map do |i|
L       i + 1
L     end
S Example: SupportedTypes: ['block']
L   # good
L   foo = if expression
L     'bar'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   foo =
L     [1].map do |i|
L       'bar' * i
L     end
C Layout/MultilineBlockLayout
S Details
L Checks whether the multiline do end blocks have a newline
L after the start of the block. Additionally, it checks whether the block
L arguments, if any, are on the same line as the start of the
L block. Putting block arguments on separate lines, because the whole
L line would otherwise be too long, is accepted.
S Example
L   # bad
L   blah do |i| foo(i)
L     bar(i)
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   blah do
L     |i| foo(i)
L     bar(i)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   blah do |i|
L     foo(i)
L     bar(i)
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   blah { |i| foo(i)
L     bar(i)
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   blah { |i|
L     foo(i)
L     bar(i)
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   blah { |
L     long_list,
L     of_parameters,
L     that_would_not,
L     fit_on_one_line
L   |
L     foo(i)
L     bar(i)
L   }
C Layout/MultilineHashBraceLayout
S Details
L Checks that the closing brace in a hash literal is either
L on the same line as the last hash element, or a new line.
L 
L When using the `symmetrical` (default) style:
L 
L If a hash's opening brace is on the same line as the first element
L of the hash, then the closing brace should be on the same line as
L the last element of the hash.
L 
L If a hash's opening brace is on the line above the first element
L of the hash, then the closing brace should be on the line below
L the last element of the hash.
L 
L When using the `new_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line hash literal must be on the line
L after the last element of the hash.
L 
L When using the `same_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line hash literal must be on the same
L line as the last element of the hash.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: symmetrical (default)
L   # bad
L   { a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   }
L   # bad
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2 }
L 
L   # good
L   { a: 1,
L     b: 2 }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: new_line
L   # bad
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2 }
L 
L   # bad
L   { a: 1,
L     b: 2 }
L 
L   # good
L   { a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: same_line
L   # bad
L   { a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   }
L 
L   # bad
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2 }
L 
L   # good
L   { a: 1,
L     b: 2 }
C Layout/MultilineHashKeyLineBreaks
S Details
L Ensures that each key in a multi-line hash
L starts on a separate line.
S Example
L   # bad
L   {
L     a: 1, b: 2,
L     c: 3
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: 2,
L     c: 3
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   {
L     a: 1,
L     b: {
L       c: 3,
L     }
L   }
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   { a: 1, b: {
L     c: 3,
L   }}
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # good
L   { a: 1, b: {
L     c: 3,
L   }}
C Layout/MultilineMethodArgumentLineBreaks
S Details
L Ensures that each argument in a multi-line method call
L starts on a separate line.
L 
L NOTE: This cop does not move the first argument, if you want that to
L be on a separate line, see `Layout/FirstMethodArgumentLineBreak`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   foo(a, b,
L     c
L   )
L 
L   # bad
L   foo(a, b, {
L     foo: "bar",
L   })
L 
L   # good
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b,
L     c
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   foo(a, b, c)
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   foo(a, b,
L     c
L   )
L 
L   # bad
L   foo(
L     a, b, {
L       foo: "bar",
L     }
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b,
L     {
L       foo: "bar",
L     }
L   )
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # bad
L   foo(a, b,
L     c
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   foo(
L     a, b, {
L       foo: "bar",
L     }
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b,
L     {
L       foo: "bar",
L     }
L   )
C Layout/MultilineMethodCallBraceLayout
S Details
L Checks that the closing brace in a method call is either
L on the same line as the last method argument, or a new line.
L 
L When using the `symmetrical` (default) style:
L 
L If a method call's opening brace is on the same line as the first
L argument of the call, then the closing brace should be on the same
L line as the last argument of the call.
L 
L If a method call's opening brace is on the line above the first
L argument of the call, then the closing brace should be on the line
L below the last argument of the call.
L 
L When using the `new_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line method call must be on the line
L after the last argument of the call.
L 
L When using the `same_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line method call must be on the same
L line as the last argument of the call.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: symmetrical (default)
L   # bad
L   foo(a,
L     b
L   )
L 
L   # bad
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b)
L 
L   # good
L   foo(a,
L     b)
L 
L   # good
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
S Example: EnforcedStyle: new_line
L   # bad
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b)
L 
L   # bad
L   foo(a,
L     b)
L 
L   # good
L   foo(a,
L     b
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
S Example: EnforcedStyle: same_line
L   # bad
L   foo(a,
L     b
L   )
L 
L   # bad
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b)
L 
L   # good
L   foo(a,
L     b)
C Layout/MultilineMethodCallIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the method name part in method calls
L that span more than one line.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: aligned (default)
L   # bad
L   while myvariable
L   .b
L     # do something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   while myvariable
L         .b
L     # do something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   Thing.a
L        .b
L        .c
S Example: EnforcedStyle: indented
L   # good
L   while myvariable
L     .b
L 
L     # do something
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: indented_relative_to_receiver
L   # good
L   while myvariable
L           .a
L           .b
L 
L     # do something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   myvariable = Thing
L                  .a
L                  .b
L                  .c
C Layout/MultilineMethodDefinitionBraceLayout
S Details
L Checks that the closing brace in a method definition is either
L on the same line as the last method parameter, or a new line.
L 
L When using the `symmetrical` (default) style:
L 
L If a method definition's opening brace is on the same line as the
L first parameter of the definition, then the closing brace should be
L on the same line as the last parameter of the definition.
L 
L If a method definition's opening brace is on the line above the first
L parameter of the definition, then the closing brace should be on the
L line below the last parameter of the definition.
L 
L When using the `new_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line method definition must be on the line
L after the last parameter of the definition.
L 
L When using the `same_line` style:
L 
L The closing brace of a multi-line method definition must be on the same
L line as the last parameter of the definition.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: symmetrical (default)
L   # bad
L   def foo(a,
L     b
L   )
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(a,
L     b)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: new_line
L   # bad
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b)
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   def foo(a,
L     b)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(a,
L     b
L   )
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: same_line
L   # bad
L   def foo(a,
L     b
L   )
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(a,
L     b)
L   end
C Layout/MultilineMethodParameterLineBreaks
S Details
L Ensures that each parameter in a multi-line method definition
L starts on a separate line.
L 
L NOTE: This cop does not move the first argument, if you want that to
L be on a separate line, see `Layout/FirstMethodParameterLineBreak`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def foo(a, b,
L     c
L   )
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b,
L     c
L   )
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(
L     a,
L     b = {
L       foo: "bar",
L     }
L   )
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(a, b, c)
L   end
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: false (default)
L   # bad
L   def foo(a, b = {
L     foo: "bar",
L   })
L   end
S Example: AllowMultilineFinalElement: true
L   # good
L   def foo(a, b = {
L     foo: "bar",
L   })
L   end
C Layout/MultilineOperationIndentation
S Details
L Checks the indentation of the right hand side operand in binary operations that
L span more than one line.
L 
L The `aligned` style checks that operators are aligned if they are part of an `if` or `while`
L condition, an explicit `return` statement, etc. In other contexts, the second operand should
L be indented regardless of enforced style.
L 
L In both styles, operators should be aligned when an assignment begins on the next line.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: aligned (default)
L   # bad
L   if a +
L       b
L     something &&
L     something_else
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if a +
L      b
L     something &&
L       something_else
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: indented
L   # bad
L   if a +
L      b
L     something &&
L     something_else
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if a +
L       b
L     something &&
L       something_else
L   end
C Layout/ParameterAlignment
S Details
L Checks that the parameters on a multi-line method call or definition are aligned.
L 
L To set the alignment of the first argument, use the
L `Layout/FirstParameterIndentation` cop.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: with_first_parameter (default)
L   # good
L 
L   def foo(bar,
L           baz)
L     123
L   end
L 
L   def foo(
L     bar,
L     baz
L   )
L     123
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   def foo(bar,
L        baz)
L     123
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   def foo(
L     bar,
L        baz)
L     123
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: with_fixed_indentation
L   # good
L 
L   def foo(bar,
L     baz)
L     123
L   end
L 
L   def foo(
L     bar,
L     baz
L   )
L     123
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   def foo(bar,
L           baz)
L     123
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   def foo(
L     bar,
L        baz)
L     123
L   end
C Layout/RedundantLineBreak
S Details
L Checks whether certain expressions, e.g. method calls, that could fit
L completely on a single line, are broken up into multiple lines unnecessarily.
S Example
L   # bad
L   foo(
L     a,
L     b
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   foo(a, b)
L 
L   # bad
L   puts 'string that fits on ' \
L        'a single line'
L 
L   # good
L   puts 'string that fits on a single line'
L 
L   # bad
L   things
L     .select { |thing| thing.cond? }
L     .join('-')
L 
L   # good
L   things.select { |thing| thing.cond? }.join('-')
S Example: InspectBlocks: false (default)
L   # good
L   foo(a) do |x|
L     puts x
L   end
S Example: InspectBlocks: true
L   # bad
L   foo(a) do |x|
L     puts x
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   foo(a) { |x| puts x }
C Layout/RescueEnsureAlignment
S Details
L Checks whether the rescue and ensure keywords are aligned
L properly.
S Example
L   # bad
L   begin
L     something
L     rescue
L     puts 'error'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   begin
L     something
L   rescue
L     puts 'error'
L   end
C Layout/SingleLineBlockChain
S Details
L Checks if method calls are chained onto single line blocks. It considers that a
L line break before the dot improves the readability of the code.
S Example
L   # bad
L   example.select { |item| item.cond? }.join('-')
L 
L   # good
L   example.select { |item| item.cond? }
L          .join('-')
L 
L   # good (not a concern for this cop)
L   example.select do |item|
L     item.cond?
L   end.join('-')
C Layout/SpaceAfterColon
S Details
L Checks for colon (`:`) not followed by some kind of space.
L N.B. this cop does not handle spaces after a ternary operator, which are
L instead handled by `Layout/SpaceAroundOperators`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def f(a:, b:2); {a:3}; end
L 
L   # good
L   def f(a:, b: 2); {a: 3}; end
C Layout/SpaceAfterComma
S Details
L Checks for comma (`,`) not followed by some kind of space.
S Example
L   # bad
L   [1,2]
L   { foo:bar,}
L 
L   # good
L   [1, 2]
L   { foo:bar, }
C Layout/SpaceAfterMethodName
S Details
L Checks for space between a method name and a left parenthesis in defs.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def func (x) end
L   def method= (y) end
L 
L   # good
L   def func(x) end
L   def method=(y) end
C Layout/SpaceAfterNot
S Details
L Checks for space after `!`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   ! something
L 
L   # good
L   !something
C Layout/SpaceAfterSemicolon
S Details
L Checks for semicolon (`;`) not followed by some kind of space.
S Example
L   # bad
L   x = 1;y = 2
L 
L   # good
L   x = 1; y = 2
C Layout/SpaceAroundBlockParameters
S Details
L Checks the spacing inside and after block parameters pipes. Line breaks
L inside parameter pipes are checked by `Layout/MultilineBlockLayout` and
L not by this cop. Spaces inside a lambda's parameter parentheses are left
L to `Layout/SpaceInsideParens` when that cop is enabled, so the examples
L below apply to a lambda only when it is not.
S Example: EnforcedStyleInsidePipes: no_space (default)
L   # bad
L   {}.each { | x,  y |puts x }
L   ->( x,  y ) { puts x }
L 
L   # good
L   {}.each { |x, y| puts x }
L   ->(x, y) { puts x }
S Example: EnforcedStyleInsidePipes: space
L   # bad
L   {}.each { |x,  y| puts x }
L   ->(x,  y) { puts x }
L 
L   # good
L   {}.each { | x, y | puts x }
L   ->( x, y ) { puts x }
C Layout/SpaceAroundEqualsInParameterDefault
S Details
L Checks that the equals signs in parameter default assignments
L have or don't have surrounding space depending on configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space (default)
L   # bad
L   def some_method(arg1=:default, arg2=nil, arg3=[])
L     # do something...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def some_method(arg1 = :default, arg2 = nil, arg3 = [])
L     # do something...
L   end
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space
L   # bad
L   def some_method(arg1 = :default, arg2 = nil, arg3 = [])
L     # do something...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def some_method(arg1=:default, arg2=nil, arg3=[])
L     # do something...
L   end
C Layout/SpaceAroundKeyword
S Details
L Checks the spacing around the keywords.
S Example
L   # bad
L   something 'test'do|x|
L   end
L 
L   while(something)
L   end
L 
L   something = 123if test
L 
L   return(foo + bar)
L 
L   # good
L   something 'test' do |x|
L   end
L 
L   while (something)
L   end
L 
L   something = 123 if test
L 
L   return (foo + bar)
C Layout/SpaceAroundMethodCallOperator
S Details
L Checks method call operators to not have spaces around them.
S Example
L   # bad
L   foo. bar
L   foo .bar
L   foo . bar
L   foo. bar .buzz
L   foo
L     . bar
L     . buzz
L   foo&. bar
L   foo &.bar
L   foo &. bar
L   foo &. bar&. buzz
L   RuboCop:: Cop
L   RuboCop:: Cop:: Base
L   :: RuboCop::Cop
L 
L   # good
L   foo.bar
L   foo.bar.buzz
L   foo
L     .bar
L     .buzz
L   foo&.bar
L   foo&.bar&.buzz
L   RuboCop::Cop
L   RuboCop::Cop::Base
L   ::RuboCop::Cop
C Layout/SpaceAroundOperators
S Details
L Checks that operators have space around them, except for ** which
L should or shouldn't have surrounding space depending on configuration.
L It allows vertical alignment consisting of one or more whitespace
L around operators.
L 
L This cop has `AllowForAlignment` option. When `true`, allows most
L uses of extra spacing if the intent is to align with an operator on
L the previous or next line, not counting empty lines or comment lines.
S Example
L   # bad
L   total = 3*4
L   "apple"+"juice"
L   my_number = 38/4
L 
L   # good
L   total = 3 * 4
L   "apple" + "juice"
L   my_number = 38 / 4
S Example: AllowForAlignment: true (default)
L   # good
L   {
L     1 =>  2,
L     11 => 3
L   }
S Example: AllowForAlignment: false
L   # bad
L   {
L     1 =>  2,
L     11 => 3
L   }
S Example: EnforcedStyleForExponentOperator: no_space (default)
L   # bad
L   a ** b
L 
L   # good
L   a**b
S Example: EnforcedStyleForExponentOperator: space
L   # bad
L   a**b
L 
L   # good
L   a ** b
S Example: EnforcedStyleForRationalLiterals: no_space (default)
L   # bad
L   1 / 48r
L 
L   # good
L   1/48r
S Example: EnforcedStyleForRationalLiterals: space
L   # bad
L   1/48r
L 
L   # good
L   1 / 48r
C Layout/SpaceBeforeBlockBraces
S Details
L Checks that block braces have or don't have a space before the opening
L brace depending on configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space (default)
L   # bad
L   foo.map{ |a|
L     a.bar.to_s
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   foo.map { |a|
L     a.bar.to_s
L   }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space
L   # bad
L   foo.map { |a|
L     a.bar.to_s
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   foo.map{ |a|
L     a.bar.to_s
L   }
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces: space (default)
L   # bad
L   7.times{}
L 
L   # good
L   7.times {}
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces: no_space
L   # bad
L   7.times {}
L 
L   # good
L   7.times{}
C Layout/SpaceBeforeBrackets
S Details
L Checks for space between the name of a receiver and a left
L bracket.
S Example
L   # bad
L   collection [index_or_key]
L 
L   # good
L   collection[index_or_key]
C Layout/SpaceBeforeComma
S Details
L Checks for comma (`,`) preceded by space.
S Example
L   # bad
L   [1 , 2 , 3]
L   a(1 , 2)
L   each { |a , b| }
L 
L   # good
L   [1, 2, 3]
L   a(1, 2)
L   each { |a, b| }
C Layout/SpaceBeforeComment
S Details
L Checks for missing space between a token and a comment on the
L same line.
S Example
L   # bad
L   1 + 1# this operation does ...
L 
L   # good
L   1 + 1 # this operation does ...
C Layout/SpaceBeforeFirstArg
S Details
L Checks that exactly one space is used between a method name and the
L first argument for method calls without parentheses.
L 
L Alternatively, extra spaces can be added to align the argument with
L something on a preceding or following line, if the AllowForAlignment
L config parameter is true.
S Example
L   # bad
L   something  x
L   something   y, z
L   something'hello'
L 
L   # good
L   something x
L   something y, z
L   something 'hello'
C Layout/SpaceBeforeSemicolon
S Details
L Checks for semicolon (`;`) preceded by space.
S Example
L   # bad
L   x = 1 ; y = 2
L 
L   # good
L   x = 1; y = 2
C Layout/SpaceInLambdaLiteral
S Details
L Checks for spaces between `->` and opening parameter
L parenthesis (`(`) in lambda literals.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: require_no_space (default)
L   # bad
L   a = -> (x, y) { x + y }
L 
L   # good
L   a = ->(x, y) { x + y }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: require_space
L   # bad
L   a = ->(x, y) { x + y }
L 
L   # good
L   a = -> (x, y) { x + y }
C Layout/SpaceInsideArrayLiteralBrackets
S Details
L Checks that brackets used for array literals have or don't have
L surrounding space depending on configuration.
L 
L Array pattern matching is handled in the same way.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space (default)
L   # The `no_space` style enforces that array literals have
L   # no surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   array = [ a, b, c, d ]
L   array = [ a, [ b, c ]]
L 
L   # good
L   array = [a, b, c, d]
L   array = [a, [b, c]]
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space
L   # The `space` style enforces that array literals have
L   # surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   array = [a, b, c, d]
L   array = [ a, [ b, c ]]
L 
L   # good
L   array = [ a, b, c, d ]
L   array = [ a, [ b, c ] ]
S Example: EnforcedStyle: compact
L   # The `compact` style normally requires a space inside
L   # array brackets, with the exception that successive left
L   # or right brackets are collapsed together in nested arrays.
L 
L   # bad
L   array = [a, b, c, d]
L   array = [ a, [ b, c ] ]
L   array = [
L     [ a ],
L     [ b, c ]
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   array = [ a, b, c, d ]
L   array = [ a, [ b, c ]]
L   array = [[ a ],
L     [ b, c ]]
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets: no_space (default)
L   # The `no_space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets style enforces that
L   # empty array brackets do not contain spaces.
L 
L   # bad
L   foo = [ ]
L   bar = [     ]
L 
L   # good
L   foo = []
L   bar = []
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets: space
L   # The `space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets style enforces that
L   # empty array brackets contain exactly one space.
L 
L   # bad
L   foo = []
L   bar = [    ]
L 
L   # good
L   foo = [ ]
L   bar = [ ]
C Layout/SpaceInsideArrayPercentLiteral
S Details
L Checks for unnecessary additional spaces inside array percent literals
L (i.e. %i/%w).
L 
L Note that blank percent literals (e.g. `%i( )`) are checked by
L `Layout/SpaceInsidePercentLiteralDelimiters`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   %w(foo  bar  baz)
L   # good
L   %i(foo bar baz)
C Layout/SpaceInsideBlockBraces
S Details
L Checks that block braces have or don't have surrounding space inside
L them on configuration. For blocks taking parameters, it checks that the
L left brace has or doesn't have trailing space depending on
L configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space (default)
L   # The `space` style enforces that block braces have
L   # surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   some_array.each {puts e}
L 
L   # good
L   some_array.each { puts e }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space
L   # The `no_space` style enforces that block braces don't
L   # have surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   some_array.each { puts e }
L 
L   # good
L   some_array.each {puts e}
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces: no_space (default)
L   # The `no_space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces style enforces that
L   # block braces don't have a space in between when empty.
L 
L   # bad
L   some_array.each {   }
L   some_array.each {  }
L   some_array.each { }
L 
L   # good
L   some_array.each {}
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces: space
L   # The `space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces style enforces that
L   # block braces have at least a space in between when empty.
L 
L   # bad
L   some_array.each {}
L 
L   # good
L   some_array.each { }
L   some_array.each {  }
L   some_array.each {   }
S Example: SpaceBeforeBlockParameters: true (default)
L   # The SpaceBeforeBlockParameters style set to `true` enforces that
L   # there is a space between `{` and `|`. Overrides `EnforcedStyle`
L   # if there is a conflict.
L 
L   # bad
L   [1, 2, 3].each {|n| n * 2 }
L 
L   # good
L   [1, 2, 3].each { |n| n * 2 }
S Example: SpaceBeforeBlockParameters: false
L   # The SpaceBeforeBlockParameters style set to `false` enforces that
L   # there is no space between `{` and `|`. Overrides `EnforcedStyle`
L   # if there is a conflict.
L 
L   # bad
L   [1, 2, 3].each { |n| n * 2 }
L 
L   # good
L   [1, 2, 3].each {|n| n * 2 }
C Layout/SpaceInsideHashLiteralBraces
S Details
L Checks that braces used for hash literals have or don't have
L surrounding space depending on configuration.
L 
L Hash pattern matching is handled in the same way.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space (default)
L   # The `space` style enforces that hash literals have
L   # surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   h = {a: 1, b: 2}
L   foo = {{ a: 1 } => { b: { c: 2 }}}
L 
L   # good
L   h = { a: 1, b: 2 }
L   foo = { { a: 1 } => { b: { c: 2 } } }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space
L   # The `no_space` style enforces that hash literals have
L   # no surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   h = { a: 1, b: 2 }
L   foo = {{ a: 1 } => { b: { c: 2 }}}
L 
L   # good
L   h = {a: 1, b: 2}
L   foo = {{a: 1} => {b: {c: 2}}}
S Example: EnforcedStyle: compact
L   # The `compact` style normally requires a space inside
L   # hash braces, with the exception that successive left
L   # braces or right braces are collapsed together in nested hashes.
L 
L   # bad
L   h = { a: { b: 2 } }
L   foo = { { a: 1 } => { b: { c: 2 } } }
L 
L   # good
L   h = { a: { b: 2 }}
L   foo = {{ a: 1 } => { b: { c: 2 }}}
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces: no_space (default)
L   # The `no_space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces style enforces that
L   # empty hash braces do not contain spaces.
L 
L   # bad
L   foo = { }
L   bar = {    }
L   baz = {
L   }
L 
L   # good
L   foo = {}
L   bar = {}
L   baz = {}
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces: space
L   # The `space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBraces style enforces that
L   # empty hash braces contain space.
L 
L   # bad
L   foo = {}
L 
L   # good
L   foo = { }
L   foo = {    }
L   foo = {
L   }
C Layout/SpaceInsideParens
S Details
L Checks for spaces inside ordinary round parentheses.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space (default)
L   # The `no_space` style enforces that parentheses do not have spaces.
L 
L   # bad
L   f( 3)
L   g = (a + 3 )
L   f( )
L 
L   # good
L   f(3)
L   g = (a + 3)
L   f()
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space
L   # The `space` style enforces that parentheses have a space at the
L   # beginning and end.
L   # Note: Empty parentheses should not have spaces.
L 
L   # bad
L   f(3)
L   g = (a + 3)
L   y( )
L 
L   # good
L   f( 3 )
L   g = ( a + 3 )
L   y()
S Example: EnforcedStyle: compact
L   # The `compact` style enforces that parentheses have a space at the
L   # beginning with the exception that successive parentheses are allowed.
L   # Note: Empty parentheses should not have spaces.
L 
L   # bad
L   f(3)
L   g = (a + 3)
L   y( )
L   g( f( x ) )
L   g( f( x( 3 ) ), 5 )
L   g( ( ( 3 + 5 ) * f) ** x, 5 )
L 
L   # good
L   f( 3 )
L   g = ( a + 3 )
L   y()
L   g( f( x ))
L   g( f( x( 3 )), 5 )
L   g((( 3 + 5 ) * f ) ** x, 5 )
C Layout/SpaceInsidePercentLiteralDelimiters
S Details
L Checks for unnecessary additional spaces inside the delimiters of
L %i/%w/%x literals.
S Example
L   # bad
L   %i( foo bar baz )
L 
L   # good
L   %i(foo bar baz)
L 
L   # bad
L   %w( foo bar baz )
L 
L   # good
L   %w(foo bar baz)
L 
L   # bad
L   %x(  ls -l )
L 
L   # good
L   %x(ls -l)
L 
L   # bad
L   %w( )
L   %w(
L   )
L 
L   # good
L   %w()
C Layout/SpaceInsideRangeLiteral
S Details
L Checks for spaces inside range literals.
S Example
L   # bad
L   1 .. 3
L 
L   # good
L   1..3
L 
L   # bad
L   'a' .. 'z'
L 
L   # good
L   'a'..'z'
C Layout/SpaceInsideReferenceBrackets
S Details
L Checks that reference brackets have or don't have
L surrounding space depending on configuration.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space (default)
L   # The `no_space` style enforces that reference brackets have
L   # no surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   hash[ :key ]
L   array[ index ]
L 
L   # good
L   hash[:key]
L   array[index]
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space
L   # The `space` style enforces that reference brackets have
L   # surrounding space.
L 
L   # bad
L   hash[:key]
L   array[index]
L 
L   # good
L   hash[ :key ]
L   array[ index ]
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets: no_space (default)
L   # The `no_space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets style enforces that
L   # empty reference brackets do not contain spaces.
L 
L   # bad
L   foo[ ]
L   foo[     ]
L   foo[
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   foo[]
S Example: EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets: space
L   # The `space` EnforcedStyleForEmptyBrackets style enforces that
L   # empty reference brackets contain exactly one space.
L 
L   # bad
L   foo[]
L   foo[    ]
L   foo[
L   ]
L 
L   # good
L   foo[ ]
C Layout/SpaceInsideStringInterpolation
S Details
L Checks for whitespace within string interpolations.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: no_space (default)
L   # bad
L      var = "This is the #{ space } example"
L 
L   # good
L      var = "This is the #{no_space} example"
S Example: EnforcedStyle: space
L   # bad
L      var = "This is the #{no_space} example"
L 
L   # good
L      var = "This is the #{ space } example"
C Layout/TrailingEmptyLines
S Details
L Looks for trailing blank lines and a final newline in the
L source code.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: final_newline (default)
L   # `final_newline` looks for one newline at the end of files.
L 
L   # bad
L   class Foo; end
L 
L   # EOF
L 
L   # bad
L   class Foo; end # EOF
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo; end
L   # EOF
S Example: EnforcedStyle: final_blank_line
L   # `final_blank_line` looks for one blank line followed by a new line
L   # at the end of files.
L 
L   # bad
L   class Foo; end
L   # EOF
L 
L   # bad
L   class Foo; end # EOF
L 
L   # good
L   class Foo; end
L 
L   # EOF
C Layout/TrailingWhitespace
S Details
L Looks for trailing whitespace in the source code.
S Example
L   # The line in this example contains spaces after the 0.
L   # bad
L   x = 0
L 
L   # The line in this example ends directly after the 0.
L   # good
L   x = 0
S Example: AllowInHeredoc: false (default)
L   # The line in this example contains spaces after the 0.
L   # bad
L   code = <<~RUBY
L     x = 0
L   RUBY
L 
L   # ok
L   code = <<~RUBY
L     x = 0 #{}
L   RUBY
L 
L   # good
L   trailing_whitespace = ' '
L   code = <<~RUBY
L     x = 0#{trailing_whitespace}
L   RUBY
S Example: AllowInHeredoc: true
L   # The line in this example contains spaces after the 0.
L   # good
L   code = <<~RUBY
L     x = 0
L   RUBY
C Lint/AmbiguousAssignment
S Details
L Checks for mistyped shorthand assignments.
S Example
L   # bad
L   x =- y
L   x =+ y
L   x =* y
L   x =! y
L 
L   # good
L   x -= y # or x = -y
L   x += y # or x = +y
L   x *= y # or x = *y
L   x != y # or x = !y
C Lint/AmbiguousBlockAssociation
S Details
L Checks for ambiguous block association with method
L when param passed without parentheses.
L 
L This cop also detects `do...end` blocks that are likely intended for
L an enumerable method in the arguments but actually bind to the outer
L method call. For example, in `render json: data.map do |x| x end`,
L Ruby parses the `do...end` block as belonging to `render`, not `map`.
L 
L This cop can customize allowed methods with `AllowedMethods`.
L By default, there are no allowed methods.
S Example
L   # bad
L   some_method a { |val| puts val }
L 
L   # good
L   # With parentheses, there's no ambiguity.
L   some_method(a { |val| puts val })
L   # or (different meaning)
L   some_method(a) { |val| puts val }
L 
L   # bad
L   render json: data.map do |item|
L     item.to_h
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   render json: data.map { |item| item.to_h }
L 
L   # good
L   mapped = data.map { |item| item.to_h }
L   render json: mapped
L 
L   # good
L   # Operator methods require no disambiguation
L   foo == bar { |b| b.baz }
L 
L   # good
L   # Lambda arguments require no disambiguation
L   foo = ->(bar) { bar.baz }
S Example: AllowedMethods: [] (default)
L   # bad
L   expect { do_something }.to change { object.attribute }
S Example: AllowedMethods: [change]
L   # good
L   expect { do_something }.to change { object.attribute }
S Example: AllowedPatterns: [] (default)
L   # bad
L   expect { do_something }.to change { object.attribute }
S Example: AllowedPatterns: ['change']
L   # good
L   expect { do_something }.to change { object.attribute }
L   expect { do_something }.to not_change { object.attribute }
C Lint/AmbiguousOperator
S Details
L Checks for ambiguous operators in the first argument of a
L method invocation without parentheses.
S Example
L   # bad
L 
L   # The `*` is interpreted as a splat operator but it could possibly be
L   # a `*` method invocation (i.e. `do_something.*(some_array)`).
L   do_something *some_array
L 
L   # good
L 
L   # With parentheses, there's no ambiguity.
L   do_something(*some_array)
C Lint/AmbiguousOperatorPrecedence
S Details
L Looks for expressions containing multiple binary operators
L where precedence is ambiguous due to lack of parentheses. For example,
L in `1 + 2 * 3`, the multiplication will happen before the addition, but
L lexically it appears that the addition will happen first.
L 
L The cop does not consider unary operators (ie. `!a` or `-b`) or comparison
L operators (ie. `a =~ b`) because those are not ambiguous.
L 
L NOTE: Ranges are handled by `Lint/AmbiguousRange`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   a + b * c
L   a || b && c
L   a ** b + c
L 
L   # good (different precedence)
L   a + (b * c)
L   a || (b && c)
L   (a ** b) + c
L 
L   # good (same precedence)
L   a + b + c
L   a * b / c % d
C Lint/AmbiguousRange
S Details
L Checks for ambiguous ranges.
L 
L Ranges have quite low precedence, which leads to unexpected behavior when
L using a range with other operators. This cop avoids that by making ranges
L explicit by requiring parenthesis around complex range boundaries (anything
L that is not a literal: numerics, strings, symbols, etc.).
L 
L This cop can be configured with `RequireParenthesesForMethodChains` in order to
L specify whether method chains (including `self.foo`) should be wrapped in parens
L by this cop.
L 
L NOTE: Regardless of this configuration, if a method receiver is a basic literal
L value, it will be wrapped in order to prevent the ambiguity of `1..2.to_a`.
S Safety
L   The cop autocorrects by wrapping the entire boundary in parentheses, which
L   makes the outcome more explicit but is possible to not be the intention of the
L   programmer. For this reason, this cop's autocorrect is unsafe (it will not
L   change the behavior of the code, but will not necessarily match the
L   intent of the program).
S Example
L   # bad
L   x || 1..2
L   x - 1..2
L   (x || 1..2)
L   x || 1..y || 2
L   1..2.to_a
L 
L   # good, unambiguous
L   1..2
L   'a'..'z'
L   :bar..:baz
L   MyClass::MIN..MyClass::MAX
L   @min..@max
L   a..b
L   -a..b
L 
L   # good, ambiguity removed
L   x || (1..2)
L   (x - 1)..2
L   (x || 1)..2
L   (x || 1)..(y || 2)
L   (1..2).to_a
S Example: RequireParenthesesForMethodChains: false (default)
L   # good
L   a.foo..b.bar
L   (a.foo)..(b.bar)
S Example: RequireParenthesesForMethodChains: true
L   # bad
L   a.foo..b.bar
L 
L   # good
L   (a.foo)..(b.bar)
C Lint/AmbiguousRegexpLiteral
S Details
L Checks for ambiguous regexp literals in the first argument of
L a method invocation without parentheses.
S Example
L   # bad
L 
L   # This is interpreted as a method invocation with a regexp literal,
L   # but it could possibly be `/` method invocations.
L   # (i.e. `do_something./(pattern)./(i)`)
L   do_something /pattern/i
L 
L   # good
L 
L   # With parentheses, there's no ambiguity.
L   do_something(/pattern/i)
C Lint/ArgumentMismatch
S Details
L Checks for calls that pass the wrong number of positional arguments to a
L method whose definition is known from the project index.
L 
L The check is powered by the project-wide index, so it only runs when
L `AllCops/UseProjectIndex` is enabled and the `rubydex` gem is installed.
L Without the index the cop does nothing.
L 
L Only calls on constant receivers (`Foo.bar`) are considered, and only
L when the receiver resolves in the index, its entire ancestry is resolved
L (so a method inherited from a gem or the standard library never produces
L an offense), and the called method resolves to a single, unambiguous
L signature. Calls that forward arguments (`*`, `**`, `...`) are skipped
L because their argument count is not statically known. Keyword arguments
L passed to a method that declares no keyword parameters are counted as the
L single positional hash they collapse into, matching Ruby's semantics.
L 
L Constructor calls (`Foo.new`) and keyword-argument validation are out of
L scope: only positional arity of explicitly defined singleton methods is
L checked. `new` is skipped outright, because `Class#new` is not in the
L index: any `new` the index does find sits below it in the method
L resolution order, where Ruby would never reach it.
S Example
L   # Given the project defines:
L   #   class Report
L   #     def self.generate(source, format); end
L   #   end
L 
L   # bad
L   Report.generate(source)
L 
L   # bad
L   Report.generate(source, :pdf, :extra)
L 
L   # good
L   Report.generate(source, :pdf)
C Lint/ArrayLiteralInRegexp
S Details
L Checks for an array literal interpolated inside a regexp.
L 
L When interpolating an array literal, it is converted to a string. This means
L that when inside a regexp, it acts as a character class but with additional
L quotes, spaces and commas that are likely not intended. For example,
L `/#{%w[a b c]}/` parses as `/["a", "b", "c"]/` (or `/["a, bc]/` without
L repeated characters).
L 
L The cop can autocorrect to a character class (if all items in the array are a
L single character) or alternation (if the array contains longer items).
L 
L NOTE: This only considers interpolated arrays that contain only strings, symbols,
L integers, and floats. Any other type is not easily convertible to a character class
L or regexp alternation.
S Safety
L   Autocorrection is unsafe because it will change the regexp pattern, by
L   removing the additional quotes, spaces and commas from the character class.
S Example
L   # bad
L   /#{%w[a b c]}/
L 
L   # good
L   /[abc]/
L 
L   # bad
L   /#{%w[foo bar baz]}/
L 
L   # good
L   /(?:foo|bar|baz)/
L 
L   # bad - construct a regexp rather than interpolate an array of identifiers
L   /#{[foo, bar]}/
C Lint/AssignmentInCondition
S Details
L Checks for assignments in the conditions of
L if/while/until.
L 
L `AllowSafeAssignment` option for safe assignment.
L By safe assignment we mean putting parentheses around
L an assignment to indicate "I know I'm using an assignment
L as a condition. It's not a mistake."
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because it assumes that
L   the author meant to use an assignment result as a condition.
S Example
L   # bad
L   if some_var = value
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if some_var == value
L     do_something
L   end
S Example: AllowSafeAssignment: true (default)
L   # good
L   if (some_var = value)
L     do_something
L   end
S Example: AllowSafeAssignment: false
L   # bad
L   if (some_var = value)
L     do_something
L   end
C Lint/BigDecimalNew
S Details
L `BigDecimal.new()` is deprecated since BigDecimal 1.3.3.
L This cop identifies places where `BigDecimal.new()`
L can be replaced by `BigDecimal()`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   BigDecimal.new(123.456, 3)
L 
L   # good
L   BigDecimal(123.456, 3)
C Lint/BinaryOperatorWithIdenticalOperands
S Details
L Checks for places where binary operator has identical operands.
L 
L It covers comparison operators: `==`, `===`, `=~`, `>`, `>=`, `<`, ``<=``;
L bitwise operators: `|`, `^`, `&`;
L boolean operators: `&&`, `||`
L and "spaceship" operator - ``<=>``.
L 
L Simple arithmetic operations are allowed by this cop: `+`, `*`, `**`, `<<` and `>>`.
L Although these can be rewritten in a different way, it should not be necessary to
L do so. Operations such as `-` or `/` where the result will always be the same
L (`x - x` will always be 0; `x / x` will always be 1) are offenses, but these
L are covered by `Lint/NumericOperationWithConstantResult` instead.
S Safety
L   This cop is unsafe as it does not consider side effects when calling methods
L   and thus can generate false positives, for example:
L 
L   [source,ruby]
L   ----
L   if wr.take_char == '\0' && wr.take_char == '\0'
L     # ...
L   end
L   ----
S Example
L   # bad
L   x.top >= x.top
L 
L   if a.x != 0 && a.x != 0
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   def child?
L     left_child || left_child
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   x + x
L   1 << 1
C Lint/BooleanSymbol
S Details
L Checks for `:true` and `:false` symbols.
L In most cases it would be a typo.
S Safety
L   Autocorrection is unsafe for this cop because code relying
L   on `:true` or `:false` symbols will break when those are
L   changed to actual booleans.
S Example
L   # bad
L   :true
L 
L   # good
L   true
L 
L   # bad
L   :false
L 
L   # good
L   false
C Lint/CircularArgumentReference
S Details
L Checks for circular argument references in optional keyword
L arguments and optional ordinal arguments.
L 
L NOTE: This syntax was made invalid on Ruby 2.7 - Ruby 3.3 but is allowed
L again since Ruby 3.4.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def bake(pie: pie)
L     pie.heat_up
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def bake(pie:)
L     pie.refrigerate
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def bake(pie: self.pie)
L     pie.feed_to(user)
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   def cook(dry_ingredients = dry_ingredients)
L     dry_ingredients.reduce(&:+)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def cook(dry_ingredients = self.dry_ingredients)
L     dry_ingredients.combine
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   def foo(pie = pie = pie)
L     pie.heat_up
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(pie)
L     pie.heat_up
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   def foo(pie = cake = pie)
L     [pie, cake].each(&:heat_up)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def foo(cake = pie)
L     [pie, cake].each(&:heat_up)
L   end
C Lint/ConstantDefinitionInBlock
S Details
L Do not define constants within a block, since the block's scope does not
L isolate or namespace the constant in any way.
L 
L If you are trying to define that constant once, define it outside of
L the block instead, or use a variable or method if defining the constant
L in the outer scope would be problematic.
L 
L For meta-programming, use `const_set`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   task :lint do
L     FILES_TO_LINT = Dir['lib/*.rb']
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   describe 'making a request' do
L     class TestRequest; end
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   module M
L     extend ActiveSupport::Concern
L     included do
L       LIST = []
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   task :lint do
L     files_to_lint = Dir['lib/*.rb']
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'making a request' do
L     let(:test_request) { Class.new }
L     # see also `stub_const` for RSpec
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   module M
L     extend ActiveSupport::Concern
L     included do
L       const_set(:LIST, [])
L     end
L   end
S Example: AllowedMethods: ['enums'] (default)
L   # good
L 
L   # `enums` for Typed Enums via `T::Enum` in Sorbet.
L   # https://sorbet.org/docs/tenum
L   class TestEnum < T::Enum
L     enums do
L       Foo = new("foo")
L     end
L   end
C Lint/ConstantOverwrittenInRescue
S Details
L Checks for overwriting an exception with an exception result by using ``rescue =>``.
L 
L You intended to write as `rescue StandardError`.
L However, you have written `rescue => StandardError`.
L In that case, the result of `rescue` will overwrite `StandardError`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   begin
L     something
L   rescue => StandardError
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   begin
L     something
L   rescue StandardError
L   end
C Lint/ConstantReassignment
S Details
L Checks for constant reassignments.
L 
L Emulates Ruby's runtime warning "already initialized constant X"
L when a constant is reassigned in the same file and namespace.
L 
L The cop tracks constants defined via `NAME = value` syntax as well as
L class/module keyword definitions. It detects reassignment when a constant
L is first defined one way and then redefined using the `NAME = value` syntax.
L 
L The cop cannot catch all offenses, like, for example, when using metaprogramming
L (`Module#const_set`).
L 
L By default the cop also cannot detect reassignment across files.
L When `AllCops/UseProjectIndex` is enabled and the `rubydex` gem is installed,
L the cop additionally consults the project-wide index and reports reassignments
L whose previous definition lives in another file.
L 
L The cop only takes into account constants assigned in a "simple" way: directly
L inside class/module definition, or within another constant. Other type of assignments
L (e.g., inside a conditional) are disregarded.
L 
L The cop also tracks constant removal using `Module#remove_const` with symbol
L or string argument.
S Example
L   # bad
L   X = :foo
L   X = :bar
L 
L   # bad
L   class A
L     X = :foo
L     X = :bar
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   module A
L     X = :foo
L     X = :bar
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   class FooError < StandardError; end
L   FooError = Class.new(RuntimeError)
L 
L   # bad
L   module M; end
L   M = 1
L 
L   # good - keep only one assignment
L   X = :bar
L 
L   class A
L     X = :bar
L   end
L 
L   module A
L     X = :bar
L   end
L 
L   # good - use OR assignment
L   X = :foo
L   X ||= :bar
L 
L   # good - use conditional assignment
L   X = :foo
L   X = :bar unless defined?(X)
L 
L   # good - remove the assigned constant first
L   class A
L     X = :foo
L     remove_const :X
L     X = :bar
L   end
C Lint/ConstantResolution
S Details
L Checks that certain constants are fully qualified.
L 
L This is not enabled by default because it would mark a lot of offenses
L unnecessarily.
L 
L Generally, gems should fully qualify all constants to avoid conflicts with
L the code that uses the gem. Enable this cop without using `Only`/`Ignore`
L 
L Large projects will over time end up with one or two constant names that
L are problematic because of a conflict with a library or just internally
L using the same name for a namespace and a class. To avoid too many unnecessary
L offenses, enable this cop with `Only: [The, Constant, Names, Causing, Issues]`
L 
L NOTE: `Style/RedundantConstantBase` cop is disabled if this cop is enabled,
L to prevent conflicting rules. This is because it respects user configurations
L that want to enable this cop which is disabled by default.
L 
L When `AllCops/UseProjectIndex` is enabled and the `rubydex` gem is
L installed, only genuinely ambiguous constants are reported: those
L that resolve to a different declaration through the surrounding
L nesting than they would fully qualified. This makes the cop practical
L to enable without `Only`/`Ignore` lists.
S Example
L   # By default checks every constant
L 
L   # bad
L   User
L 
L   # bad
L   User::Login
L 
L   # good
L   ::User
L 
L   # good
L   ::User::Login
S Example: Only: ['Login']
L   # Restrict this cop to only being concerned about certain constants
L 
L   # bad
L   Login
L 
L   # good
L   ::Login
L 
L   # good
L   User::Login
S Example: Ignore: ['Login']
L   # Restrict this cop not being concerned about certain constants
L 
L   # bad
L   User
L 
L   # good
L   ::User::Login
L 
L   # good
L   Login
C Lint/CopDirectiveSyntax
S Details
L Checks that `# rubocop:enable ...` and `# rubocop:disable ...` statements
L are strictly formatted.
L 
L A comment can be added to the directive by prefixing it with `--`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength Style/Encoding
L 
L   # good
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength, Style/Encoding
L 
L   # bad
L   # rubocop:disable
L 
L   # good
L   # rubocop:disable all
L 
L   # bad - only the first directive takes effect
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength # rubocop:disable Style/Encoding
L 
L   # good
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength, Style/Encoding
L 
L   # bad
L   # rubocop:wrongmode Layout/LineLength
L 
L   # good
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength
L 
L   # bad
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength comment
L 
L   # good
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength -- comment
L 
L   # bad
L   # rucocop:disable Layout/LineLength
L 
L   # good
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength
L 
L   # bad
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLenght
L 
L   # good
L   # rubocop:disable Layout/LineLength
C Lint/DataDefineOverride
S Details
L Checks unexpected overrides of the `Data` built-in methods
L via `Data.define`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   Bad = Data.define(:members, :clone, :to_s)
L   b = Bad.new(members: [], clone: true, to_s: 'bad')
L   b.members #=> [] (overriding `Data#members`)
L   b.clone #=> true (overriding `Object#clone`)
L   b.to_s #=> "bad" (overriding `Data#to_s`)
L 
L   # good
L   Good = Data.define(:id, :name)
L   g = Good.new(id: 1, name: "foo")
L   g.members #=> [:id, :name]
L   g.clone #=> #<data Good id=1, name="foo">
C Lint/Debugger
S Details
L Checks for debug calls (such as `debugger` or `binding.pry`) that should
L not be kept for production code.
L 
L The cop can be configured using `DebuggerMethods`. By default, a number of gems
L debug entrypoints are configured (`Kernel`, `Byebug`, `Capybara`, `debug.rb`,
L `Pry`, `Rails`, `RubyJard`, and `WebConsole`). Additional methods can be added.
L 
L Specific default groups can be disabled if necessary:
L 
L [source,yaml]
L ----
L Lint/Debugger:
L   DebuggerMethods:
L     WebConsole: ~
L ----
L 
L You can also add your own methods by adding a new category:
L 
L [source,yaml]
L ----
L Lint/Debugger:
L   DebuggerMethods:
L     MyDebugger:
L       MyDebugger.debug_this
L ----
L 
L Some gems also ship files that will start a debugging session when required,
L for example `require 'debug/start'` from `ruby/debug`. These requires can
L be configured through `DebuggerRequires`. It has the same structure as
L `DebuggerMethods`, which you can read about above.
S Example
L   # bad (ok during development)
L 
L   # using pry
L   def some_method
L     binding.pry
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # bad (ok during development)
L 
L   # using byebug
L   def some_method
L     byebug
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L 
L   def some_method
L     do_something
L   end
S Example: DebuggerMethods: [my_debugger]
L   # bad (ok during development)
L 
L   def some_method
L     my_debugger
L   end
S Example: DebuggerRequires: [my_debugger/start]
L   # bad (ok during development)
L 
L   require 'my_debugger/start'
C Lint/DeprecatedClassMethods
S Details
L Checks for uses of the deprecated class method usages.
S Example
L   # bad
L   File.exists?(some_path)
L   Dir.exists?(some_path)
L   iterator?
L   attr :name, true
L   attr :name, false
L   ENV.freeze # Calling `Env.freeze` raises `TypeError` since Ruby 2.7.
L   ENV.clone
L   ENV.dup # Calling `Env.dup` raises `TypeError` since Ruby 3.1.
L   Socket.gethostbyname(host)
L   Socket.gethostbyaddr(host)
L 
L   # good
L   File.exist?(some_path)
L   Dir.exist?(some_path)
L   block_given?
L   attr_accessor :name
L   attr_reader :name
L   ENV # `ENV.freeze` cannot prohibit changes to environment variables.
L   ENV.to_h
L   ENV.to_h # `ENV.dup` cannot dup `ENV`, use `ENV.to_h` to get a copy of `ENV` as a hash.
L   Addrinfo.getaddrinfo(nodename, service)
L   Addrinfo.tcp(host, port).getnameinfo
C Lint/DeprecatedConstants
S Details
L Checks for deprecated constants.
L 
L It has `DeprecatedConstants` config. If there is an alternative method, you can set
L alternative value as `Alternative`. And you can set the deprecated version as
L `DeprecatedVersion`. These options can be omitted if they are not needed.
L 
L   DeprecatedConstants:
L     'DEPRECATED_CONSTANT':
L       Alternative: 'alternative_value'
L       DeprecatedVersion: 'deprecated_version'
L 
L By default, `NIL`, `TRUE`, `FALSE`, `Net::HTTPServerException`, `Random::DEFAULT`,
L `Struct::Group`, and `Struct::Passwd` are configured.
S Example
L   # bad
L   NIL
L   TRUE
L   FALSE
L   Net::HTTPServerException
L   Random::DEFAULT # Return value of Ruby 2 is `Random` instance, Ruby 3.0 is `Random` class.
L   Struct::Group
L   Struct::Passwd
L 
L   # good
L   nil
L   true
L   false
L   Net::HTTPClientException
L   Random.new # `::DEFAULT` has been deprecated in Ruby 3, `.new` is compatible with Ruby 2.
L   Etc::Group
L   Etc::Passwd
C Lint/DeprecatedOpenSSLConstant
S Details
L Algorithmic constants for `OpenSSL::Cipher` and `OpenSSL::Digest`
L deprecated since OpenSSL version 2.2.0. Prefer passing a string
L instead.
S Example
L   # bad
L   OpenSSL::Cipher::AES.new(128, :GCM)
L 
L   # good
L   OpenSSL::Cipher.new('aes-128-gcm')
L 
L   # bad
L   OpenSSL::Digest::SHA256.new
L 
L   # good
L   OpenSSL::Digest.new('SHA256')
L 
L   # bad
L   OpenSSL::Digest::SHA256.digest('foo')
L 
L   # good
L   OpenSSL::Digest.digest('SHA256', 'foo')
C Lint/DeprecatedReference
S Details
L Checks for calls to methods and references to constants that are documented
L as deprecated with a YARD `@deprecated` tag.
L 
L The check is powered by the project-wide index, so it only runs when
L `AllCops/UseProjectIndex` is enabled and the `rubydex` gem is installed.
L Without the index the cop does nothing.
L 
L Only references that can be resolved without type inference are checked:
L constants, method calls without an explicit receiver (or with `self`),
L which are looked up in the enclosing class or module and its ancestry,
L and calls whose receiver is a constant, which are looked up in that
L namespace's singleton class. Calls on arbitrary objects are not checked.
L 
L References made from a definition that is itself deprecated are allowed,
L so deprecated implementations can keep calling each other.
S Example
L   # Given a deprecated method and constant:
L   #
L   #   class Api
L   #     # @deprecated Use `#new_method` instead.
L   #     def old_method
L   #     end
L   #
L   #     # @deprecated
L   #     OLD_TIMEOUT = 10
L   #   end
L 
L   # bad
L   class Client < Api
L     def call
L       old_method
L     end
L 
L     def timeout
L       OLD_TIMEOUT
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Client < Api
L     def call
L       new_method
L     end
L 
L     def timeout
L       NEW_TIMEOUT
L     end
L   end
C Lint/DisjunctiveAssignmentInConstructor
S Details
L Checks constructors for disjunctive assignments (`||=`) that should
L be plain assignments.
L 
L So far, this cop is only concerned with disjunctive assignment of
L instance variables.
L 
L In ruby, an instance variable is nil until a value is assigned, so the
L disjunction is unnecessary. A plain assignment has the same effect.
S Safety
L   This cop is unsafe because it can register a false positive when a
L   method is redefined in a subclass that calls super. For example:
L 
L   [source,ruby]
L   ----
L   class Base
L     def initialize
L       @config ||= 'base'
L     end
L   end
L 
L   class Derived < Base
L     def initialize
L       @config = 'derived'
L       super
L     end
L   end
L   ----
L 
L   Without the disjunctive assignment, `Derived` will be unable to override
L   the value for `@config`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   def initialize
L     @x ||= 1
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   def initialize
L     @x = 1
L   end
C Lint/DuplicateBranch
S Details
L Checks that there are no repeated bodies
L within `if/unless`, `case-when`, `case-in` and `rescue` constructs.
L 
L With `IgnoreLiteralBranches: true`, branches are not registered
L as offenses if they return a basic literal value (string, symbol,
L integer, float, rational, complex, `true`, `false`, or `nil`), or
L return an array, hash, regexp or range that only contains one of
L the above basic literal values.
L 
L With `IgnoreConstantBranches: true`, branches are not registered
L as offenses if they return a constant value.
L 
L With `IgnoreDuplicateElseBranch: true`, in conditionals with multiple branches,
L duplicate 'else' branches are not registered as offenses.
S Example
L   # bad
L   if foo
L     do_foo
L     do_something_else
L   elsif bar
L     do_foo
L     do_something_else
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if foo || bar
L     do_foo
L     do_something_else
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   case x
L   when foo
L     do_foo
L   when bar
L     do_foo
L   else
L     do_something_else
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   case x
L   when foo, bar
L     do_foo
L   else
L     do_something_else
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   begin
L     do_something
L   rescue FooError
L     handle_error
L   rescue BarError
L     handle_error
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   begin
L     do_something
L   rescue FooError, BarError
L     handle_error
L   end
S Example: IgnoreLiteralBranches: true
L   # good
L   case size
L   when "small" then 100
L   when "medium" then 250
L   when "large" then 1000
L   else 250
L   end
S Example: IgnoreConstantBranches: true
L   # good
L   case size
L   when "small" then SMALL_SIZE
L   when "medium" then MEDIUM_SIZE
L   when "large" then LARGE_SIZE
L   else MEDIUM_SIZE
L   end
S Example: IgnoreDuplicateElseBranch: true
L   # good
L   if foo
L     do_foo
L   elsif bar
L     do_bar
L   else
L     do_foo
L   end
C Lint/DuplicateCaseCondition
S Details
L Checks that there are no repeated conditions
L used in case 'when' expressions.
S Example
L   # bad
L   case x
L   when 'first'
L     do_something
L   when 'first'
L     do_something_else
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   case x
L   when 'first'
L     do_something
L   when 'second'
L     do_something_else
L   end
C Lint/DuplicateElsifCondition
S Details
L Checks that there are no repeated conditions used in if 'elsif'.
S Example
L   # bad
L   if x == 1
L     do_something
L   elsif x == 1
L     do_something_else
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   if x == 1
L     do_something
L   elsif x == 2
L     do_something_else
L   end
C Lint/DuplicateHashKey
S Details
L Checks for duplicated keys in hash literals.
L This cop considers both primitive types and constants for the hash keys.
L 
L This cop mirrors a warning in Ruby 2.2.
S Example
L   # bad
L   hash = { food: 'apple', food: 'orange' }
L 
L   # good
L   hash = { food: 'apple', other_food: 'orange' }
C Lint/DuplicateMagicComment
S Details
L Checks for duplicated magic comments.
S Example
L   # bad
L 
L   # encoding: ascii
L   # encoding: ascii
L 
L   # good
L 
L   # encoding: ascii
L 
L   # bad
L 
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
L 
L   # good
L 
L   # frozen_string_literal: true
C Lint/DuplicateMatchPattern
S Details
L Checks that there are no repeated patterns used in `in` keywords.
S Example
L   # bad
L   case x
L   in 'first'
L     do_something
L   in 'first'
L     do_something_else
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   case x
L   in 'first'
L     do_something
L   in 'second'
L     do_something_else
L   end
L
