L   expect { foo }.not_to output.to_stdout
L   expect { bar }.to output.to_stderr
C RSpec/Eq
S Details
L Use `eq` instead of `be ==` to compare objects.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to be == 42
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to eq 42
C RSpec/ExampleLength
S Details
L Checks for long examples.
L 
L A long example is usually more difficult to understand. Consider
L extracting out some behavior, e.g. with a `let` block, or a helper
L method.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it do
L     service = described_class.new
L     more_setup
L     more_setup
L     result = service.call
L     expect(result).to be(true)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     service = described_class.new
L     result = service.call
L     expect(result).to be(true)
L   end
L 
L You can set constructs you want to fold with `CountAsOne`.
L Available are: 'array', 'hash', 'heredoc', and 'method_call'.
L Each construct will be counted as one line regardless of
L its actual size.
S Example: CountAsOne: ['array', 'heredoc', 'method_call']
L   it do
L     array = [         # +1
L       1,
L       2
L     ]
L 
L     hash = {          # +3
L       key: 'value'
L     }
L 
L     msg = <<~HEREDOC  # +1
L       Heredoc
L       content.
L     HEREDOC
L 
L     foo(            # +1
L       1,
L       2
L     )
L   end               # 6 points
C RSpec/ExampleWithoutDescription
S Details
L Checks for examples without a description.
L 
L RSpec allows for auto-generated example descriptions when there is no
L description provided or the description is an empty one.
L It is acceptable to use `specify` without a description
L 
L This cop removes empty descriptions.
L It also defines whether auto-generated description is allowed, based
L on the configured style.
L 
L This cop can be configured using the `EnforcedStyle` option
S Example
L   # always good
L   specify do
L     result = service.call
L     expect(result).to be(true)
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: always_allow` (default)
L   # bad
L   it('') { is_expected.to be_good }
L   specify '' do
L     result = service.call
L     expect(result).to be(true)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to be_good }
L   specify do
L     result = service.call
L     expect(result).to be(true)
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: single_line_only`
L   # bad
L   it('') { is_expected.to be_good }
L   it do
L     result = service.call
L     expect(result).to be(true)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to be_good }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: disallow`
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to be_good }
L   it do
L     result = service.call
L     expect(result).to be(true)
L   end
C RSpec/ExampleWording
S Details
L Checks for common mistakes in example descriptions.
L 
L This cop will correct docstrings that begin with 'should' and 'it'.
L This cop will also look for insufficient examples and call them out.
S Safety
L   The autocorrect is experimental - use with care! It can be configured
L   with CustomTransform (e.g. have => has) and IgnoredWords (e.g. only).
L 
L   While the autocorrect will not break your code (it only modifies test
L   description strings, not the actual test logic), it may produce
L   grammatically incorrect English in some cases. Always review the diff
L   when using autocorrect to ensure the descriptions remain natural and
L   accurate.
L 
L   This is not classified as an unsafe autocorrect because it does not
L   affect code behavior, but manual review of changes is strongly
L   recommended.
L 
L Use the DisallowedExamples setting to prevent unclear or insufficient
L descriptions. Please note that this config will not be treated as
L case sensitive.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it 'should find nothing' do
L   end
L 
L   it 'will find nothing' do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'finds nothing' do
L   end
S Example
L   # bad
L   it 'it does things' do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'does things' do
L   end
S Example: `DisallowedExamples: ['works']` (default)
L   # bad
L   it 'works' do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'marks the task as done' do
L   end
C RSpec/ExcessiveDocstringSpacing
S Details
L Checks for excessive whitespace in example descriptions.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it '  has  excessive   spacing  ' do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'has excessive spacing' do
L   end
S Example
L   # bad
L   context '  when a condition   is met  ' do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   context 'when a condition is met' do
L   end
C RSpec/ExpectActual
S Details
L Checks for `expect(...)` calls containing literal values.
L 
L Autocorrection is performed when the expected is not a literal.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(5).to eq(price)
L   expect(/foo/).to eq(pattern)
L   expect("John").to eq(name)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(price).to eq(5)
L   expect(pattern).to eq(/foo/)
L   expect(name).to eq("John")
L 
L   # bad (not supported autocorrection)
L   expect(42).to be_even
L   expect(false).to eq(true)
L   expect("user").to be_present
C RSpec/ExpectChange
S Details
L Checks for consistent style of change matcher.
L 
L Enforces either passing a receiver and message as method arguments,
L or a block.
L 
L This cop can be configured using the `EnforcedStyle` option.
L 
L When using compound expectations with `change` and a negated matcher
L (e.g., `not_change`), you can configure the `NegatedMatcher` option
L to ensure consistent style enforcement across both matchers.
S Safety
L   Autocorrection is unsafe because `method_call` style calls the
L   receiver *once* and sends the message to it before and after
L   calling the `expect` block, whereas `block` style calls the
L   expression *twice*, including the receiver.
L 
L   If your receiver is dynamic (e.g., the result of a method call) and
L   you expect it to be called before and after the `expect` block,
L   changing from `block` to `method_call` style may break your test.
L 
L   [source,ruby]
L   ----
L   expect { run }.to change { my_method.message }
L   # `my_method` is called before and after `run`
L 
L   expect { run }.to change(my_method, :message)
L   # `my_method` is called once, but `message` is called on it twice
L   ----
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: method_call` (default)
L   # bad
L   expect { run }.to change { Foo.bar }
L   expect { run }.to change { foo.baz }
L 
L   # good
L   expect { run }.to change(Foo, :bar)
L   expect { run }.to change(foo, :baz)
L   # also good when there are arguments or chained method calls
L   expect { run }.to change { Foo.bar(:count) }
L   expect { run }.to change { user.reload.name }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: block`
L   # bad
L   expect { run }.to change(Foo, :bar)
L 
L   # good
L   expect { run }.to change { Foo.bar }
S Example: `NegatedMatcher: not_change` (with compound expectations)
L   # bad
L   expect { run }.to change(Foo, :bar).and not_change { Foo.baz }
L 
L   # good
L   expect { run }.to change(Foo, :bar).and not_change(Foo, :baz)
C RSpec/ExpectInHook
S Details
L Do not use `expect` in hooks such as `before`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   before do
L     expect(something).to eq 'foo'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   after do
L     expect_any_instance_of(Something).to receive(:foo)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     expect(something).to eq 'foo'
L   end
C RSpec/ExpectInLet
S Details
L Do not use `expect` in let.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) do
L     expect(something).to eq 'foo'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     expect(something).to eq 'foo'
L   end
C RSpec/ExpectOutput
S Details
L Checks for opportunities to use `expect { ... }.to output`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   $stdout = StringIO.new
L   my_app.print_report
L   $stdout = STDOUT
L   expect($stdout.string).to eq('Hello World')
L 
L   # good
L   expect { my_app.print_report }.to output('Hello World').to_stdout
C RSpec/Focus
S Details
L Checks if examples are focused.
L 
L This cop does not support autocorrection in some cases.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe MyClass, focus: true do
L   end
L 
L   describe MyClass, :focus do
L   end
L 
L   fdescribe MyClass do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe MyClass do
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   fdescribe 'test' do; end
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'test' do; end
L 
L   # bad
L   shared_examples 'test', focus: true do; end
L 
L   # good
L   shared_examples 'test' do; end
L 
L   # bad
L   shared_context 'test', focus: true do; end
L 
L   # good
L   shared_context 'test' do; end
L 
L   # bad (does not support autocorrection)
L   focus 'test' do; end
C RSpec/HookArgument
S Details
L Checks the arguments passed to `before`, `around`, and `after`.
L 
L This cop checks for consistent style when specifying RSpec
L hooks which run for each example. There are three supported
L styles: "implicit", "each", and "example." All styles have
L the same behavior.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: implicit` (default)
L   # bad
L   before(:each) do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   before(:example) do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   before do
L     # ...
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: each`
L   # bad
L   before(:example) do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   before do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   before(:each) do
L     # ...
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: example`
L   # bad
L   before(:each) do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   before do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   before(:example) do
L     # ...
L   end
C RSpec/HooksBeforeExamples
S Details
L Checks for before/around/after hooks that come after an example.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it 'checks what foo does' do
L     expect(foo).to be
L   end
L 
L   before { prepare }
L   after { clean_up }
L 
L   # good
L   before { prepare }
L   after { clean_up }
L 
L   it 'checks what foo does' do
L     expect(foo).to be
L   end
C RSpec/IdenticalEqualityAssertion
S Details
L Checks for equality assertions with identical expressions on both sides.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(foo.bar).to eq(foo.bar)
L   expect(foo.bar).to eql(foo.bar)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo.bar).to eq(2)
L   expect(foo.bar).to eql(2)
C RSpec/ImplicitBlockExpectation
S Details
L Check that implicit block expectation syntax is not used.
L 
L Prefer using explicit block expectations.
S Example
L   # bad
L   subject { -> { do_something } }
L   it { is_expected.to change(something).to(new_value) }
L 
L   # good
L   it 'changes something to a new value' do
L     expect { do_something }.to change(something).to(new_value)
L   end
C RSpec/ImplicitExpect
S Details
L Check that a consistent implicit expectation style is used.
L 
L This cop can be configured using the `EnforcedStyle` option
L and supports the `--auto-gen-config` flag.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: is_expected` (default)
L   # bad
L   it { should be_truthy }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to be_truthy }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: should`
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to be_truthy }
L 
L   # good
L   it { should be_truthy }
C RSpec/ImplicitSubject
S Details
L Checks for usage of implicit subject (`is_expected` / `should`).
L 
L This cop can be configured using the `EnforcedStyle` option
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: single_line_only` (default)
L   # bad
L   it do
L     is_expected.to be_truthy
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to be_truthy }
L   it do
L     expect(subject).to be_truthy
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: single_statement_only`
L   # bad
L   it do
L     foo = 1
L     is_expected.to be_truthy
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     foo = 1
L     expect(subject).to be_truthy
L   end
L   it do
L     is_expected.to be_truthy
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: disallow`
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to be_truthy }
L 
L   # good
L   it { expect(subject).to be_truthy }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: require_implicit`
L   # bad
L   it { expect(subject).to be_truthy }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to be_truthy }
L 
L   # bad
L   it do
L     expect(subject).to be_truthy
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     is_expected.to be_truthy
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it { expect(named_subject).to be_truthy }
C RSpec/IncludeExamples
S Details
L Checks for usage of `include_examples`.
L 
L `include_examples`, unlike `it_behaves_like`, does not create its
L own context. As such, using `subject`, `let`, `before`/`after`, etc.
L within shared examples included with `include_examples` can have
L unexpected behavior and side effects.
L 
L Prefer using `it_behaves_like` instead.
S Safety
L   `include_examples` and `it_behaves_like` have different scoping
L   behaviors.
L   Changing `include_examples` to `it_behaves_like` creates a new
L   context, altering setup dependencies, which can lead to unexpected
L   test failures.
L   Specifically, the scope of hooks (`before`, `after`, `around`)
L   changes, which may prevent expected setup from being inherited
L   correctly.
L 
L   Additionally, `let` and `subject` are affected by scoping rules.
L   When `include_examples` is used, `let` and `subject` defined within
L   `shared_examples` are evaluated in the caller's context, allowing
L   access to their values.
L   In contrast, `it_behaves_like` creates a new context, preventing
L   access to `let` or `subject` values from the caller's context.
L 
L   [source,ruby]
L   ----
L   shared_examples "mock behavior" do
L     before do
L       allow(service).to receive(:call).and_return("mocked response")
L     end
L 
L     it "returns mocked response" do
L       expect(service.call).to eq "mocked response"
L     end
L   end
L 
L   context "working example with include_examples" do
L     let(:service) { double(:service) }
L 
L     include_examples "mock behavior"
L 
L     it "uses the mocked service" do
L       expect(service.call).to eq "mocked response" # Passes
L     end
L   end
L 
L   context "broken example with it_behaves_like" do
L     let(:service) { double(:service) }
L 
L     it_behaves_like "mock behavior"
L 
L     it "unexpectedly does not use the mocked service" do
L       # Fails because `it_behaves_like` does not apply the mock setup
L       expect(service.call).to eq "mocked response"
L     end
L   end
L   ----
S Example
L   # bad
L   include_examples 'examples'
L 
L   # good
L   it_behaves_like 'examples'
C RSpec/IndexedLet
S Details
L Do not set up test data using indexes (e.g., `item_1`, `item_2`).
L 
L It makes reading the test harder because it's not clear what exactly
L is tested by this particular example.
L 
L The configurable options `AllowedIdentifiers` and `AllowedPatterns`
L will also read those set in `Naming/VariableNumber`.
S Example: `Max: 1 (default)`
L   # bad
L   let(:item_1) { create(:item) }
L   let(:item_2) { create(:item) }
L 
L   let(:item1) { create(:item) }
L   let(:item2) { create(:item) }
L 
L   # good
L 
L   let(:visible_item) { create(:item, visible: true) }
L   let(:invisible_item) { create(:item, visible: false) }
S Example: `Max: 2`
L   # bad
L   let(:item_1) { create(:item) }
L   let(:item_2) { create(:item) }
L   let(:item_3) { create(:item) }
L 
L   # good
L   let(:item_1) { create(:item) }
L   let(:item_2) { create(:item) }
S Example: `AllowedIdentifiers: ['item_1', 'item_2']`
L   # good
L   let(:item_1) { create(:item) }
L   let(:item_2) { create(:item) }
S Example: `AllowedPatterns: ['item']`
L   # good
L   let(:item_1) { create(:item) }
L   let(:item_2) { create(:item) }
C RSpec/InstanceSpy
S Details
L Checks for `instance_double` used with `have_received`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it do
L     foo = instance_double(Foo).as_null_object
L     expect(foo).to have_received(:bar)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     foo = instance_spy(Foo)
L     expect(foo).to have_received(:bar)
L   end
C RSpec/InstanceVariable
S Details
L Checks for instance variable usage in specs.
L 
L This cop can be configured with the option `AssignmentOnly` which
L will configure the cop to only register offenses on instance
L variable usage if the instance variable is also assigned within
L the spec
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe MyClass do
L     before { @foo = [] }
L     it { expect(@foo).to be_empty }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe MyClass do
L     let(:foo) { [] }
L     it { expect(foo).to be_empty }
L   end
S Example: with AssignmentOnly configuration
L   # rubocop.yml
L   # RSpec/InstanceVariable:
L   #   AssignmentOnly: true
L 
L   # bad
L   describe MyClass do
L     before { @foo = [] }
L     it { expect(@foo).to be_empty }
L   end
L 
L   # allowed
L   describe MyClass do
L     it { expect(@foo).to be_empty }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe MyClass do
L     let(:foo) { [] }
L     it { expect(foo).to be_empty }
L   end
C RSpec/IsExpectedSpecify
S Details
L Check for `specify` with `is_expected` and one-liner expectations.
S Example
L   # bad
L   specify { is_expected.to be_truthy }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to be_truthy }
L 
L   # good
L   specify do
L     # ...
L   end
L   specify { expect(sqrt(4)).to eq(2) }
C RSpec/ItBehavesLike
S Details
L Checks that only one `it_behaves_like` style is used.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: it_behaves_like` (default)
L   # bad
L   it_should_behave_like 'a foo'
L 
L   # good
L   it_behaves_like 'a foo'
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: it_should_behave_like`
L   # bad
L   it_behaves_like 'a foo'
L 
L   # good
L   it_should_behave_like 'a foo'
C RSpec/IteratedExpectation
S Details
L Check that `all` matcher is used instead of iterating over an array.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it 'validates users' do
L     [user1, user2, user3].each { |user| expect(user).to be_valid }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'validates users' do
L     expect([user1, user2, user3]).to all(be_valid)
L   end
C RSpec/LeadingSubject
S Details
L Enforce that subject is the first definition in the test.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let(:params) { blah }
L   subject { described_class.new(params) }
L 
L   before { do_something }
L   subject { described_class.new(params) }
L 
L   it { expect_something }
L   subject { described_class.new(params) }
L   it { expect_something_else }
L 
L 
L   # good
L   subject { described_class.new(params) }
L   let(:params) { blah }
L 
L   # good
L   subject { described_class.new(params) }
L   before { do_something }
L 
L   # good
L   subject { described_class.new(params) }
L   it { expect_something }
L   it { expect_something_else }
C RSpec/LeakyConstantDeclaration
S Details
L Checks that no class, module, or constant is declared.
L 
L Constants, including classes and modules, when declared in a block
L scope, are defined in global namespace, and leak between examples.
L 
L If several examples may define a `DummyClass`, instead of being a
L blank slate class as it will be in the first example, subsequent
L examples will be reopening it and modifying its behavior in
L unpredictable ways.
L Even worse when a class that exists in the codebase is reopened.
L 
L Anonymous classes are fine, since they don't result in global
L namespace name clashes.
S Example: Constants leak between examples
L   # bad
L   describe SomeClass do
L     OtherClass = Struct.new
L     CONSTANT_HERE = 'I leak into global namespace'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe SomeClass do
L     before do
L       stub_const('OtherClass', Struct.new)
L       stub_const('CONSTANT_HERE', 'I only exist during this example')
L     end
L   end
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe SomeClass do
L     class FooClass < described_class
L       def double_that
L         some_base_method * 2
L       end
L     end
L 
L     it { expect(FooClass.new.double_that).to eq(4) }
L   end
L 
L   # good - anonymous class, no constant needs to be defined
L   describe SomeClass do
L     let(:foo_class) do
L       Class.new(described_class) do
L         def double_that
L           some_base_method * 2
L         end
L       end
L     end
L 
L     it { expect(foo_class.new.double_that).to eq(4) }
L   end
L 
L   # good - constant is stubbed
L   describe SomeClass do
L     before do
L       foo_class = Class.new(described_class) do
L                     def do_something
L                     end
L                   end
L       stub_const('FooClass', foo_class)
L     end
L 
L     it { expect(FooClass.new.double_that).to eq(4) }
L   end
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe SomeClass do
L     module SomeModule
L       class SomeClass
L         def do_something
L         end
L       end
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe SomeClass do
L     before do
L       foo_class = Class.new(described_class) do
L                     def do_something
L                     end
L                   end
L       stub_const('SomeModule::SomeClass', foo_class)
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/LeakyLocalVariable
S Details
L Checks for local variables from outer scopes used inside examples.
L 
L Local variables assigned outside an example but used within it act
L as shared state, which can make tests non-deterministic.
S Example
L   # bad - outside variable used in a hook
L   user = create(:user)
L 
L   before { user.update(admin: true) }
L 
L   # good
L   let(:user) { create(:user) }
L 
L   before { user.update(admin: true) }
L 
L   # bad - outside variable used in an example
L   user = create(:user)
L 
L   it 'is persisted' do
L     expect(user).to be_persisted
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   let(:user) { create(:user) }
L 
L   it 'is persisted' do
L     expect(user).to be_persisted
L   end
L 
L   # also good - assigning the variable within the example
L   it 'is persisted' do
L     user = create(:user)
L 
L     expect(user).to be_persisted
L   end
L 
L   # bad - outside variable passed to included examples
L   attrs = ['foo', 'bar']
L 
L   it_behaves_like 'some examples', attrs
L 
L   # good
L   it_behaves_like 'some examples' do
L     let(:attrs) { ['foo', 'bar'] }
L   end
L 
L   # good - when variable is used only as example description
L   attribute = 'foo'
L 
L   it "#{attribute} is persisted" do
L     expectations
L   end
L 
L   # good - when variable is used only in example metadata
L   skip_message = 'not yet implemented'
L 
L   it 'does something', skip: skip_message do
L     expectations
L   end
L 
L   # good - when variable is used only to include other examples
L   examples = foo ? 'some examples' : 'other examples'
L 
L   it_behaves_like examples, another_argument
C RSpec/LetBeforeExamples
S Details
L Checks for `let` definitions that come after an example.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) { bar }
L 
L   it 'checks what foo does' do
L     expect(foo).to be
L   end
L 
L   let(:some) { other }
L 
L   it 'checks what some does' do
L     expect(some).to be
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   let(:foo) { bar }
L   let(:some) { other }
L 
L   it 'checks what foo does' do
L     expect(foo).to be
L   end
L 
L   it 'checks what some does' do
L     expect(some).to be
L   end
C RSpec/LetSetup
S Details
L Checks unreferenced `let!` calls being used for test setup.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let!(:my_widget) { create(:widget) }
L 
L   it 'counts widgets' do
L     expect(Widget.count).to eq(1)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'counts widgets' do
L     create(:widget)
L     expect(Widget.count).to eq(1)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   before { create(:widget) }
L 
L   it 'counts widgets' do
L     expect(Widget.count).to eq(1)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'a widget' do
L     let!(:my_widget) { create(:widget) }
L     context 'when visiting its page' do
L       let!(:my_widget) { create(:widget, name: 'Special') }
L       it 'counts widgets' do
L         expect(Widget.count).to eq(1)
L       end
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/MatchArray
S Details
L Checks where `match_array` is used.
L 
L This cop checks for the following:
L 
L - Prefer `contain_exactly` when matching an array with values.
L - Prefer `eq` when using `match_array` with an empty array literal.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to match_array([content1, content2]) }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to contain_exactly(content1, content2) }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to match_array([content] + array) }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to match_array(%w(tremble in fear foolish mortals)) }
C RSpec/MatchWithSimpleRegex
S Details
L Enforces the use of `include` matcher instead of `match` when the
L matcher is a simple string literal without regex-specific features.
L 
L When `match` is used with a regex that contains only literal characters
L (no anchors, character classes, quantifiers, alternations, or
L metacharacters), it's clearer to use the `include` matcher instead.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect('foobar').to match(/foo/)
L   expect(response.body).to match(/http:\/\/example\.com/)
L 
L   # good
L   expect('foobar').to include('foo')
L   expect(response.body).to include('http://example.com')
L 
L   # good - regex features needed
L   expect('foobar').to match(/^foo/)     # anchor
L   expect('foobar').to match(/foo\d+/)   # quantifier
L   expect('foobar').to match(/foo[ob]/)  # character class
C RSpec/MessageChain
S Details
L Check that chains of messages are not being stubbed.
S Example
L   # bad
L   allow(foo).to receive_message_chain(:bar, :baz).and_return(42)
L 
L   # good
L   thing = Thing.new(baz: 42)
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar).and_return(thing)
C RSpec/MessageExpectation
S Details
L Checks for consistent message expectation style.
L 
L This cop can be configured in your configuration using the
L `EnforcedStyle` option and supports `--auto-gen-config`.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: allow` (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar)
L 
L   # good
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar)
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: expect`
L   # bad
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar)
C RSpec/MessageSpies
S Details
L Checks that message expectations are set using spies.
L 
L This cop can be configured in your configuration using the
L `EnforcedStyle` option and supports `--auto-gen-config`.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: have_received` (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar)
L   do_something
L 
L   # good
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar) # or use instance_spy
L   do_something
L   expect(foo).to have_received(:bar)
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: receive`
L   # bad
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar)
L   do_something
L   expect(foo).to have_received(:bar)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar)
L   do_something
C RSpec/MetadataStyle
S Details
L Use consistent metadata style.
L 
L This cop does not support autocorrection in the case of
L `EnforcedStyle: hash` where the trailing metadata type is ambiguous.
L (e.g. `describe 'Something', :a, b`)
S Example: EnforcedStyle: symbol (default)
L   # bad
L   describe 'Something', a: true
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'Something', :a
S Example: EnforcedStyle: hash
L   # bad
L   describe 'Something', :a
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'Something', a: true
C RSpec/MissingExampleGroupArgument
S Details
L Checks that the first argument to an example group is not empty.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe do
L   end
L 
L   RSpec.describe do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe TestedClass do
L   end
L 
L   describe "A feature example" do
L   end
C RSpec/MissingExpectationTargetMethod
S Details
L Checks if `.to`, `not_to` or `to_not` are used.
L 
L The RSpec::Expectations::ExpectationTarget must use `to`, `not_to` or
L `to_not` to run. Therefore, this cop checks if other methods are used.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(something).kind_of? Foo
L   is_expected == 42
L   expect{something}.eq? BarError
L 
L   # good
L   expect(something).to be_a Foo
L   is_expected.to eq 42
L   expect{something}.to raise_error BarError
C RSpec/MultipleDescribes
S Details
L Checks for multiple top-level example groups.
L 
L Multiple descriptions for the same class or module should either
L be nested or separated into different test files.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe MyClass, '.do_something' do
L   end
L   describe MyClass, '.do_something_else' do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe MyClass do
L     describe '.do_something' do
L     end
L     describe '.do_something_else' do
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/MultipleExpectations
S Details
L Checks if examples contain too many `expect` calls.
S See: http://betterspecs.org/#single Single expectation test
L This cop is configurable using the `Max` option
L and works with `--auto-gen-config`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe UserCreator do
L     it 'builds a user' do
L       expect(user.name).to eq("John")
L       expect(user.age).to eq(22)
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe UserCreator do
L     it 'sets the users name' do
L       expect(user.name).to eq("John")
L     end
L 
L     it 'sets the users age' do
L       expect(user.age).to eq(22)
L     end
L   end
S Example: `aggregate_failures: true` (default)
L   # good - the cop ignores when RSpec aggregates failures
L   describe UserCreator do
L     it 'builds a user', :aggregate_failures do
L       expect(user.name).to eq("John")
L       expect(user.age).to eq(22)
L     end
L   end
S Example: `aggregate_failures: false`
L   # Detected as an offense
L   describe UserCreator do
L     it 'builds a user', aggregate_failures: false do
L       expect(user.name).to eq("John")
L       expect(user.age).to eq(22)
L     end
L   end
S Example: `Max: 1` (default)
L   # bad
L   describe UserCreator do
L     it 'builds a user' do
L       expect(user.name).to eq("John")
L       expect(user.age).to eq(22)
L     end
L   end
S Example: `Max: 2`
L   # good
L   describe UserCreator do
L     it 'builds a user' do
L       expect(user.name).to eq("John")
L       expect(user.age).to eq(22)
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/MultipleMemoizedHelpers
S Details
L Checks if example groups contain too many `let` and `subject` calls.
L 
L This cop is configurable using the `Max` option and the `AllowSubject`
L which will configure the cop to only register offenses on calls to
L `let` and not calls to `subject`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe MyClass do
L     let(:foo) { [] }
L     let(:bar) { [] }
L     let!(:baz) { [] }
L     let(:qux) { [] }
L     let(:quux) { [] }
L     let(:quuz) { {} }
L   end
L 
L   describe MyClass do
L     let(:foo) { [] }
L     let(:bar) { [] }
L     let!(:baz) { [] }
L 
L     context 'when stuff' do
L       let(:qux) { [] }
L       let(:quux) { [] }
L       let(:quuz) { {} }
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe MyClass do
L     let(:bar) { [] }
L     let!(:baz) { [] }
L     let(:qux) { [] }
L     let(:quux) { [] }
L     let(:quuz) { {} }
L   end
L 
L   describe MyClass do
L     context 'when stuff' do
L       let(:foo) { [] }
L       let(:bar) { [] }
L       let!(:booger) { [] }
L     end
L 
L     context 'when other stuff' do
L       let(:qux) { [] }
L       let(:quux) { [] }
L       let(:quuz) { {} }
L     end
L   end
S Example: when disabling AllowSubject configuration
L   # rubocop.yml
L   # RSpec/MultipleMemoizedHelpers:
L   #   AllowSubject: false
L 
L   # bad - `subject` counts towards memoized helpers
L   describe MyClass do
L     subject { {} }
L     let(:foo) { [] }
L     let(:bar) { [] }
L     let!(:baz) { [] }
L     let(:qux) { [] }
L     let(:quux) { [] }
L   end
S Example: with Max configuration
L   # rubocop.yml
L   # RSpec/MultipleMemoizedHelpers:
L   #   Max: 1
L 
L   # bad
L   describe MyClass do
L     let(:foo) { [] }
L     let(:bar) { [] }
L   end
C RSpec/MultipleSubjects
S Details
L Checks if an example group defines `subject` multiple times.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe Foo do
L     subject(:user) { User.new }
L     subject(:post) { Post.new }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Foo do
L     let(:user) { User.new }
L     subject(:post) { Post.new }
L   end
L 
L   # bad (does not support autocorrection)
L   describe Foo do
L     subject!(:user) { User.new }
L     subject!(:post) { Post.new }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Foo do
L     before do
L       User.new
L       Post.new
L     end
L   end
L 
L This cop does not support autocorrection in some cases.
L The autocorrect behavior for this cop depends on the type of
L duplication:
L 
L   - If multiple named subjects are defined then this probably indicates
L     that the overwritten subjects (all subjects except the last
L     definition) are effectively being used to define helpers. In this
L     case they are replaced with `let`.
L 
L   - If multiple unnamed subjects are defined though then this can *only*
L     be dead code and we remove the overwritten subject definitions.
L 
L   - If subjects are defined with `subject!` then we don't autocorrect.
L     This is enough of an edge case that people can just move this to
L     a `before` hook on their own
C RSpec/NamedSubject
S Details
L Checks for explicitly referenced test subjects.
L 
L RSpec lets you declare an "implicit subject" using `subject { ... }`
L which allows for tests like `it { is_expected.to be_valid }`.
L If you need to reference your test subject you should explicitly
L name it using `subject(:your_subject_name) { ... }`. Your test subjects
L should be the most important object in your tests so they deserve
L a descriptive name.
L 
L This cop can be configured in your configuration using `EnforcedStyle`,
L and `IgnoreSharedExamples` which will not report offenses for implicit
L subjects in shared example groups.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: always` (default)
L   # bad
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     subject { described_class.new }
L 
L     it 'is valid' do
L       expect(subject.valid?).to be(true)
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     subject(:user) { described_class.new }
L 
L     it 'is valid' do
L       expect(user.valid?).to be(true)
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # also good
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     subject(:user) { described_class.new }
L 
L     it { is_expected.to be_valid }
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: named_only`
L   # bad
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     subject(:user) { described_class.new }
L 
L     it 'is valid' do
L       expect(subject.valid?).to be(true)
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     subject(:user) { described_class.new }
L 
L     it 'is valid' do
L       expect(user.valid?).to be(true)
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # also good
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     subject { described_class.new }
L 
L     it { is_expected.to be_valid }
L   end
L 
L   # acceptable
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     subject { described_class.new }
L 
L     it 'is valid' do
L       expect(subject.valid?).to be(true)
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/NestedGroups
S Details
L Checks for nested example groups.
L 
L This cop is configurable using the `Max` option
L and supports `--auto-gen-config`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   context 'when using some feature' do
L     let(:some)    { :various }
L     let(:feature) { :setup   }
L 
L     context 'when user is signed in' do  # flagged by rubocop
L       let(:user) do
L         UserCreate.call(user_attributes)
L       end
L 
L       let(:user_attributes) do
L         {
L           name: 'John',
L           age:  22,
L           role: role
L         }
L       end
L 
L       context 'when user is an admin' do # flagged by rubocop
L         let(:role) { 'admin' }
L 
L         it 'blah blah'
L         it 'yada yada'
L       end
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   context 'using some feature as an admin' do
L     let(:some)    { :various }
L     let(:feature) { :setup   }
L 
L     let(:user) do
L       UserCreate.call(
L         name: 'John',
L         age:  22,
L         role: 'admin'
L       )
L     end
L 
L     it 'blah blah'
L     it 'yada yada'
L   end
S Example: `Max: 3` (default)
L   # bad
L   describe Foo do
L     context 'foo' do
L       context 'bar' do
L         context 'baz' do # flagged by rubocop
L         end
L       end
L     end
L   end
S Example: `Max: 2`
L   # bad
L   describe Foo do
L     context 'foo' do
L       context 'bar' do # flagged by rubocop
L         context 'baz' do # flagged by rubocop
L         end
L       end
L     end
L   end
S Example: `AllowedGroups: [] (default)`
L   describe Foo do # <-- nested groups 1
L     context 'foo' do # <-- nested groups 2
L       context 'bar' do # <-- nested groups 3
L       end
L     end
L   end
S Example: `AllowedGroups: [path]`
L   describe Foo do # <-- nested groups 1
L     path '/foo' do # <-- nested groups 1 (not counted)
L       context 'bar' do # <-- nested groups 2
L       end
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/NoExpectationExample
S Details
L Checks if an example contains any expectation.
L 
L All RSpec's example and expectation methods are covered by default.
L If you are using your own custom methods,
L add the following configuration:
L 
L   RSpec:
L     Language:
L       Examples:
L         Regular:
L           - custom_it
L       Expectations:
L         - custom_expect
S Example
L   # bad
L   it do
L     a?
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     expect(a?).to be(true)
L   end
L 
L This cop can be customized with an allowed expectation methods pattern
L with an `AllowedPatterns` option. ^expect_ and ^assert_ are allowed
L by default.
S Example: `AllowedPatterns` configuration
L   # .rubocop.yml
L   # RSpec/NoExpectationExample:
L   #   AllowedPatterns:
L   #     - ^expect_
L   #     - ^assert_
S Example
L   # bad
L   it do
L     not_expect_something
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     expect_something
L   end
L 
L   it do
L     assert_something
L   end
C RSpec/NotToNot
S Details
L Checks for consistent method usage for negating expectations.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: not_to` (default)
L   # bad
L   it '...' do
L     expect(false).to_not be_true
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it '...' do
L     expect(false).not_to be_true
L   end
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: to_not`
L   # bad
L   it '...' do
L     expect(false).not_to be_true
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it '...' do
L     expect(false).to_not be_true
L   end
C RSpec/Output
S Details
L Checks for the use of output calls like puts and print in specs.
S Safety
L   This autocorrection is marked as unsafe because, in rare cases, print
L   statements can be used on purpose for integration testing and deleting
L   them will cause tests to fail.
S Example
L   # bad
L   puts 'A debug message'
L   pp 'A debug message'
L   print 'A debug message'
C RSpec/OverwritingSetup
S Details
L Checks if there is a let/subject that overwrites an existing one.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) { bar }
L   let(:foo) { baz }
L 
L   subject(:foo) { bar }
L   let(:foo) { baz }
L 
L   let(:foo) { bar }
L   let!(:foo) { baz }
L 
L   # good
L   subject(:test) { something }
L   let(:foo) { bar }
L   let(:baz) { baz }
L   let!(:other) { other }
C RSpec/Pending
S Details
L Checks for any pending or skipped examples.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe MyClass do
L     it "should be true"
L   end
L 
L   describe MyClass do
L     it "should be true", skip: true do
L       expect(1).to eq(2)
L     end
L   end
L 
L   describe MyClass do
L     it "should be true" do
L       pending
L     end
L   end
L 
L   describe MyClass do
L     xit "should be true" do
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe MyClass do
L   end
C RSpec/PendingWithoutReason
S Details
L Checks for pending or skipped examples without reason.
S Example
L   # bad
L   pending 'does something' do
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   it 'does something', :pending do
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   it 'does something' do
L     pending
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   xdescribe 'something' do
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   skip 'does something' do
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   it 'does something', :skip do
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   it 'does something' do
L     skip
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   it 'does something'
L 
L   # good
L   it 'does something' do
L     pending 'reason'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'does something' do
L     skip 'reason'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'does something', pending: 'reason' do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'does something', skip: 'reason' do
L   end
C RSpec/PredicateMatcher
S Details
L Prefer using predicate matcher over using predicate method directly.
L 
L RSpec defines magic matchers for predicate methods.
L This cop recommends to use the predicate matcher instead of using
L predicate method directly.
S Example: Strict: true, EnforcedStyle: inflected (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(foo.something?).to be_truthy
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to be_something
L 
L   # also good - It checks "true" strictly.
L   expect(foo.something?).to be(true)
S Example: Strict: false, EnforcedStyle: inflected
L   # bad
L   expect(foo.something?).to be_truthy
L   expect(foo.something?).to be(true)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to be_something
S Example: Strict: true, EnforcedStyle: explicit
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to be_something
L 
L   # good - the above code is rewritten to it by this cop
L   expect(foo.something?).to be(true)
L 
L   # bad - no autocorrect
L   expect(foo)
L     .to be_something(<<~TEXT)
L       bar
L     TEXT
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo.something?(<<~TEXT)).to be(true)
L     bar
L   TEXT
S Example: Strict: false, EnforcedStyle: explicit
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to be_something
L 
L   # good - the above code is rewritten to it by this cop
L   expect(foo.something?).to be_truthy
C RSpec/ReceiveCounts
S Details
L Check for `once` and `twice` receive counts matchers usage.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).exactly(1).times
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).exactly(2).times
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_least(1).times
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_least(2).times
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_most(1).times
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_most(2).times
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).once
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).twice
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_least(:once)
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_least(:twice)
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_most(:once)
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).at_most(:twice).times
C RSpec/ReceiveMessages
S Details
L Prefer `receive_messages` over multiple `receive`s on the same object.
S Safety
L   The autocorrection is marked as unsafe, because it may change the
L   order of stubs. This in turn may cause e.g. variables to be called
L   before they are defined.
S Example
L   # bad
L   before do
L     allow(Service).to receive(:foo).and_return(bar)
L     allow(Service).to receive(:baz).and_return(qux)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   before do
L     allow(Service).to receive_messages(foo: bar, baz: qux)
L   end
L 
L   # good - ignore same message
L   before do
L     allow(Service).to receive(:foo).and_return(bar)
L     allow(Service).to receive(:foo).and_return(qux)
L   end
C RSpec/ReceiveNever
S Details
L Prefer `not_to receive(...)` over `receive(...).never`.
L 
L This cop only flags usage with `expect`. It ignores `allow` because
L `allow(...).to receive(...).never` is a valid way to ensure a method
L is not called, while `allow(...).not_to receive(...)` would have
L different semantics.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).never
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).not_to receive(:bar)
L 
L   # not flagged by this cop
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar).never
C RSpec/RedundantAround
S Details
L Remove redundant `around` hook.
S Example
L   # bad
L   around do |example|
L     example.run
L   end
L 
L   # good
C RSpec/RedundantPredicateMatcher
S Details
L Checks for redundant predicate matcher.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to be_exist(bar)
L   expect(foo).not_to be_include(bar)
L   expect(foo).to be_all(bar)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to exist(bar)
L   expect(foo).not_to include(bar)
L   expect(foo).to all be(bar)
C RSpec/RemoveConst
S Details
L Checks that `remove_const` is not used in specs.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it 'does something' do
L     Object.send(:remove_const, :SomeConstant)
L   end
L 
L   before do
L     SomeClass.send(:remove_const, :SomeConstant)
L   end
C RSpec/RepeatedDescription
S Details
L Check for repeated description strings in example groups.
S Example
L   # bad
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     it 'is valid' do
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L     it 'is valid' do
L       # ...
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     it 'is valid when first and last name are present' do
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L     it 'is valid when last name only is present' do
L       # ...
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   RSpec.describe User do
L     it 'is valid' do
L       # ...
L     end
L 
L     it 'is valid', :flag do
L       # ...
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/RepeatedExample
S Details
L Check for repeated examples within example groups.
S Example
L   it 'is valid' do
L     expect(user).to be_valid
L   end
L 
L   it 'validates the user' do
L     expect(user).to be_valid
L   end
C RSpec/RepeatedExampleGroupBody
S Details
L Check for repeated describe and context block body.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe 'cool feature x' do
L     it { cool_predicate }
L   end
L 
L   describe 'cool feature y' do
L     it { cool_predicate }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'cool feature' do
L     it { cool_predicate }
L   end
L 
L   describe 'another cool feature' do
L     it { another_predicate }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   context 'when case x', :tag do
L     it { cool_predicate }
L   end
L 
L   context 'when case y' do
L     it { cool_predicate }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   context Array do
L     it { is_expected.to respond_to :each }
L   end
L 
L   context Hash do
L     it { is_expected.to respond_to :each }
L   end
C RSpec/RepeatedExampleGroupDescription
S Details
L Check for repeated example group descriptions.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe 'cool feature' do
L     # example group
L   end
L 
L   describe 'cool feature' do
L     # example group
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   context 'when case x' do
L     # example group
L   end
L 
L   describe 'when case x' do
L     # example group
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'cool feature' do
L     # example group
L   end
L 
L   describe 'another cool feature' do
L     # example group
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   context 'when case x' do
L     # example group
L   end
L 
L   context 'when another case' do
L     # example group
L   end
C RSpec/RepeatedIncludeExample
S Details
L Check for repeated include of shared examples.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe 'foo' do
L     include_examples 'cool stuff'
L     include_examples 'cool stuff'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   describe 'foo' do
L     it_behaves_like 'a cool', 'thing'
L     it_behaves_like 'a cool', 'thing'
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   context 'foo' do
L     it_should_behave_like 'a duck'
L     it_should_behave_like 'a duck'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'foo' do
L     include_examples 'cool stuff'
L   end
L 
L   describe 'bar' do
L     include_examples 'cool stuff'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'foo' do
L     it_behaves_like 'a cool', 'thing'
L     it_behaves_like 'a cool', 'person'
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   context 'foo' do
L     it_should_behave_like 'a duck'
L     it_should_behave_like 'a goose'
L   end
C RSpec/RepeatedSubjectCall
S Details
L Checks for repeated calls to subject missing that it is memoized.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it do
L     subject
L     expect { subject }.to not_change { A.count }
L   end
L 
L   it do
L     expect { subject }.to change { A.count }
L     expect { subject }.to not_change { A.count }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it do
L     expect { my_method }.to change { A.count }
L     expect { my_method }.to not_change { A.count }
L   end
L 
L   # also good
L   it do
L     expect { subject.a }.to change { A.count }
L     expect { subject.b }.to not_change { A.count }
L   end
C RSpec/ReturnFromStub
S Details
L Checks for consistent style of stub's return setting.
L 
L Enforces either `and_return` or block-style return in the cases
L where the returned value is constant. Ignores dynamic returned values
L are the result would be different
L 
L This cop can be configured using the `EnforcedStyle` option
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: and_return` (default)
L   # bad
L   allow(Foo).to receive(:bar) { "baz" }
L   expect(Foo).to receive(:bar) { "baz" }
L 
L   # good
L   allow(Foo).to receive(:bar).and_return("baz")
L   expect(Foo).to receive(:bar).and_return("baz")
L   # also good as the returned value is dynamic
L   allow(Foo).to receive(:bar) { bar.baz }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: block`
L   # bad
L   allow(Foo).to receive(:bar).and_return("baz")
L   expect(Foo).to receive(:bar).and_return("baz")
L 
L   # good
L   allow(Foo).to receive(:bar) { "baz" }
L   expect(Foo).to receive(:bar) { "baz" }
L   # also good as the returned value is dynamic
L   allow(Foo).to receive(:bar).and_return(bar.baz)
C RSpec/ScatteredLet
S Details
L Checks for let scattered across the example group.
L 
L Group lets together
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe Foo do
L     let(:foo) { 1 }
L     subject { Foo }
L     let(:bar) { 2 }
L     before { prepare }
L     let!(:baz) { 3 }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Foo do
L     let(:foo) { 1 }
L     let(:bar) { 2 }
L     let!(:baz) { 3 }
L     subject { Foo }
L     before { prepare }
L   end
C RSpec/ScatteredSetup
S Details
L Checks for setup scattered across multiple hooks in an example group.
L 
L Unify `before` and `after` hooks when possible.
L However, `around` hooks are allowed to be defined multiple times,
L as unifying them would typically make the code harder to read.
L Hooks defined in class methods are also ignored.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe Foo do
L     before { setup1 }
L     before { setup2 }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Foo do
L     before do
L       setup1
L       setup2
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Foo do
L     around { |example| before1; example.call; after1 }
L     around { |example| before2; example.call; after2 }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Foo do
L     before { setup1 }
L     def self.setup
L       before { setup2 }
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/SharedContext
S Details
L Checks for proper shared_context and shared_examples usage.
L 
L If there are no examples defined, use shared_context.
L If there is no setup defined, use shared_examples.
L 
L With `Strict: true`, `shared_context` is flagged whenever it contains
L any examples, even if it also contains setup code.
S Example
L   # bad
L   RSpec.shared_context 'only examples here' do
L     it 'does x' do
L     end
L 
L     it 'does y' do
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   RSpec.shared_examples 'only examples here' do
L     it 'does x' do
L     end
L 
L     it 'does y' do
L     end
L   end
S Example
L   # bad
L   RSpec.shared_examples 'only setup here' do
L     subject(:foo) { :bar }
L 
L     let(:baz) { :bazz }
L 
L     before do
L       something
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   RSpec.shared_context 'only setup here' do
L     subject(:foo) { :bar }
L 
L     let(:baz) { :bazz }
L 
L     before do
L       something
L     end
L   end
S Example: Strict: true
L   # bad - shared_context with examples is flagged
L   RSpec.shared_context 'setup and examples' do
L     let(:foo) { :bar }
L 
L     it 'does x' do
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good - split into separate shared_context and shared_examples
L   RSpec.shared_context 'setup' do
L     let(:foo) { :bar }
L   end
L 
L   RSpec.shared_examples 'examples' do
L     it 'does x' do
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/SharedExamples
S Details
L Checks for consistent style for shared example names.
L 
L Enforces either `string` or `symbol` for shared example names.
L 
L This cop can be configured using the `EnforcedStyle` option
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: string` (default)
L   # bad
L   it_behaves_like :foo_bar_baz
L   it_should_behave_like :foo_bar_baz
L   shared_examples :foo_bar_baz
L   shared_examples_for :foo_bar_baz
L   include_examples :foo_bar_baz
L 
L   # good
L   it_behaves_like 'foo bar baz'
L   it_should_behave_like 'foo bar baz'
L   shared_examples 'foo bar baz'
L   shared_examples_for 'foo bar baz'
L   include_examples 'foo bar baz'
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: symbol`
L   # bad
L   it_behaves_like 'foo bar baz'
L   it_should_behave_like 'foo bar baz'
L   shared_examples 'foo bar baz'
L   shared_examples_for 'foo bar baz'
L   include_examples 'foo bar baz'
L 
L   # good
L   it_behaves_like :foo_bar_baz
L   it_should_behave_like :foo_bar_baz
L   shared_examples :foo_bar_baz
L   shared_examples_for :foo_bar_baz
L   include_examples :foo_bar_baz
C RSpec/SingleArgumentMessageChain
S Details
L Checks that chains of messages contain more than one element.
S Example
L   # bad
L   allow(foo).to receive_message_chain(:bar).and_return(42)
L 
L   # good
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar).and_return(42)
L 
L   # also good
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar, :baz)
L   allow(foo).to receive("bar.baz")
C RSpec/SkipBlockInsideExample
S Details
L Checks for passing a block to `skip` within examples.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it 'does something' do
L     skip 'not yet implemented' do
L       do_something
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'does something' do
L     skip 'not yet implemented'
L     do_something
L   end
L 
L   # good - when outside example
L   skip 'not yet implemented' do
L   end
C RSpec/SortMetadata
S Details
L Sort RSpec metadata alphabetically.
L 
L Only the trailing metadata is sorted.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe 'Something', :b, :a
L   context 'Something', foo: 'bar', baz: true
L   it 'works', :b, :a, foo: 'bar', baz: true
L 
L   # good
L   describe 'Something', :a, :b
L   context 'Something', baz: true, foo: 'bar'
L   it 'works', :a, :b, baz: true, foo: 'bar'
L 
L   # good, trailing metadata is sorted
L   describe 'Something', 'description', :a, :b, :z
L   context 'Something', :z, variable, :a, :b
C RSpec/SpecFilePathFormat
S Details
L Checks that spec file paths are consistent and well-formed.
S Example
L   # bad
L   whatever_spec.rb         # describe MyClass
L   my_class_spec.rb         # describe MyClass, '#method'
L 
L   # good
L   my_class_spec.rb         # describe MyClass
L   my_class_method_spec.rb  # describe MyClass, '#method'
L   my_class/method_spec.rb  # describe MyClass, '#method'
S Example: `CustomTransform: {RuboCop=>rubocop, RSpec=>rspec}` (default)
L   # good
L   rubocop_spec.rb          # describe RuboCop
L   rspec_spec.rb            # describe RSpec
S Example: `IgnoreMethods: false` (default)
L   # bad
L   my_class_spec.rb         # describe MyClass, '#method'
S Example: `IgnoreMethods: true`
L   # good
L   my_class_spec.rb         # describe MyClass, '#method'
S Example: `IgnoreMetadata: {type=>routing}` (default)
L   # good
L   whatever_spec.rb         # describe MyClass, type: :routing do; end
S Example: `EnforcedInflector: active_support`
L   # Enable to use ActiveSupport's inflector for custom acronyms
L   # like HTTP, etc. Set to "default" by default.
L   # Configure `InflectorPath` with the path to the inflector file.
L   # The default is ./config/initializers/inflections.rb.
C RSpec/SpecFilePathSuffix
S Details
L Checks that spec file paths suffix are consistent and well-formed.
S Example
L   # bad
L   my_class/foo_specorb.rb   # describe MyClass
L   spec/models/user.rb       # describe User
L   spec/models/user_specxrb  # describe User
L 
L   # good
L   my_class_spec.rb          # describe MyClass
L 
L   # good - shared examples are allowed
L   spec/models/user.rb       # shared_examples_for 'foo'
C RSpec/StubbedMock
S Details
L Checks that message expectations do not have a configured response.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).with(42).and_return("hello world")
L 
L   # good (without spies)
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar).with(42).and_return("hello world")
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).with(42)
C RSpec/SubjectDeclaration
S Details
L Ensure that subject is defined using subject helper.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let(:subject) { foo }
L   let!(:subject) { foo }
L   subject(:subject) { foo }
L   subject!(:subject) { foo }
L 
L   # bad
L   block = -> {}
L   let(:subject, &block)
L 
L   # good
L   subject(:test_subject) { foo }
C RSpec/SubjectStub
S Details
L Checks for stubbed test subjects.
L 
L Checks nested subject stubs for innermost subject definition
L when subject is also defined in parent example groups.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe Article do
L     subject(:article) { Article.new }
L 
L     it 'indicates that the author is unknown' do
L       allow(article).to receive(:author).and_return(nil)
L       expect(article.description).to include('by an unknown author')
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   describe Article do
L     subject(:foo) { Article.new }
L 
L     context 'nested subject' do
L       subject(:article) { Article.new }
L 
L       it 'indicates that the author is unknown' do
L         allow(article).to receive(:author).and_return(nil)
L         expect(article.description).to include('by an unknown author')
L       end
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Article do
L     subject(:article) { Article.new(author: nil) }
L 
L     it 'indicates that the author is unknown' do
L       expect(article.description).to include('by an unknown author')
L     end
L   end
C RSpec/UndescriptiveLiteralsDescription
S Details
L Description should be descriptive.
L 
L If example group or example contains only `execute string`, numbers
L and regular expressions, the description is not clear.
S Example
L   # bad
L   describe `time` do
L    # ...
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   context /when foo/ do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   it 10000 do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe Foo do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   describe '#foo' do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   context "when #{foo} is bar" do
L     # ...
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   it 'does something' do
L     # ...
L   end
C RSpec/UnspecifiedException
S Details
L Checks for a specified error in checking raised errors.
L 
L Enforces one of an Exception type, a string, or a regular
L expression to match against the exception message as a parameter
L to `raise_error`
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect {
L     raise StandardError.new('error')
L   }.to raise_error
L 
L   # good
L   expect {
L     raise StandardError.new('error')
L   }.to raise_error(StandardError)
L 
L   expect {
L     raise StandardError.new('error')
L   }.to raise_error('error')
L 
L   expect {
L     raise StandardError.new('error')
L   }.to raise_error(/err/)
L 
L   expect { do_something }.not_to raise_error
C RSpec/VariableDefinition
S Details
L Checks that memoized helpers names are symbols or strings.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: symbols (default)
L   # bad
L   subject('user') { create_user }
L   let('user_name') { 'Adam' }
L 
L   # good
L   subject(:user) { create_user }
L   let(:user_name) { 'Adam' }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: strings
L   # bad
L   subject(:user) { create_user }
L   let(:user_name) { 'Adam' }
L 
L   # good
L   subject('user') { create_user }
L   let('user_name') { 'Adam' }
C RSpec/VariableName
S Details
L Checks that memoized helper names use the configured style.
L 
L Variables can be excluded from checking using the `AllowedPatterns`
L option.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: snake_case (default)
L   # bad
L   subject(:userName1) { 'Adam' }
L   let(:userName2) { 'Adam' }
L 
L   # good
L   subject(:user_name_1) { 'Adam' }
L   let(:user_name_2) { 'Adam' }
S Example: EnforcedStyle: camelCase
L   # bad
L   subject(:user_name_1) { 'Adam' }
L   let(:user_name_2) { 'Adam' }
L 
L   # good
L   subject(:userName1) { 'Adam' }
L   let(:userName2) { 'Adam' }
S Example: AllowedPatterns configuration
L   # rubocop.yml
L   # RSpec/VariableName:
L   #   EnforcedStyle: snake_case
L   #   AllowedPatterns:
L   #     - ^userFood
S Example
L   # okay because it matches the `^userFood` regex in `AllowedPatterns`
L   subject(:userFood_1) { 'spaghetti' }
L   let(:userFood_2) { 'fettuccine' }
C RSpec/VerifiedDoubleReference
S Details
L Checks for consistent verified double reference style.
S Safety
L   This cop is unsafe because the correction requires loading the class.
L   Loading before stubbing causes RSpec to only allow instance methods
L   to be stubbed.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) do
L     instance_double('ClassName', method_name: 'returned_value')
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     instance_double(ClassName, method_name: 'returned_value')
L   end
S Example: Reference is any dynamic variable. No enforcement
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     instance_double(@klass, method_name: 'returned_value')
L   end
C RSpec/VerifiedDoubles
S Details
L Prefer using verifying doubles over normal doubles.
S Example
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) do
L     double("ClassName", method_name: 'returned value')
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) do
L     spy("ClassName", method_name: 'returned value')
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     instance_double("ClassName", method_name: 'returned value')
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     class_double("ClassName", method_name: 'returned value')
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     object_double("some object", method_name: 'returned value')
L   end
S Example: `IgnoreNameless: true (default)`
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     double(method_name: 'returned value')
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     double
L   end
S Example: `IgnoreNameless: false`
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) do
L     double(method_name: 'returned value')
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) do
L     double
L   end
S Example: `IgnoreSymbolicNames: false (default)`
L   # bad
L   let(:foo) do
L     double(:foo)
L   end
S Example: `IgnoreSymbolicNames: true`
L   # good
L   let(:foo) do
L     double(:foo)
L   end
C RSpec/VoidExpect
S Details
L Checks void `expect()`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(something)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(something).to be(1)
C RSpec/Yield
S Details
L Checks for calling a block within a stub.
S Example
L   # bad
L   allow(foo).to receive(:bar) { |&block| block.call(1) }
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to receive(:bar).and_yield(1)
C RSpecRails/AvoidSetupHook
S Details
L Checks that tests use RSpec `before` instead of Rails `setup`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   setup do
L     allow(foo).to receive(:bar)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   before do
L     allow(foo).to receive(:bar)
L   end
C RSpecRails/FileFixture
S Details
L Prefer `file_fixture` over hardcoded paths into `spec/fixtures`.
S Safety
L   `file_fixture` uses the configured `file_fixture_path`, which defaults
L   to `spec/fixtures/files`. A file under `spec/fixtures` may need to be
L   moved or `file_fixture_path` configured before replacing the path.
S Example
L   # bad
L   Rails.root.join('spec', 'fixtures', 'files', 'example.pdf')
L 
L   # good
L   file_fixture('example.pdf')
C RSpecRails/HaveHttpStatus
S Details
L Checks that tests use `have_http_status` instead of equality matchers.
S Safety
L   The autocorrection is marked as unsafe because
L   `response.status` response is not always an HTTP response status.
S Example: `ResponseMethods: ['response', 'last_response']` (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(response.status).to be(200)
L   expect(last_response.code).to eq("200")
L 
L   # good
L   expect(response).to have_http_status(200)
L   expect(last_response).to have_http_status(200)
S Example: `ResponseMethods: ['foo_response']`
L   # bad
L   expect(foo_response.status).to be(200)
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo_response).to have_http_status(200)
L 
L   # also good
L   expect(response).to have_http_status(200)
L   expect(last_response).to have_http_status(200)
C RSpecRails/HttpStatus
S Details
L Enforces use of symbolic or numeric value to describe HTTP status.
L 
L This cop inspects only `have_http_status` calls.
L So, this cop does not check if a method starting with `be_*` is used
L when setting for `EnforcedStyle: symbolic` or
L `EnforcedStyle: numeric`.
L This cop is also capable of detecting unknown HTTP status codes.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: symbolic` (default)
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status 200 }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status 404 }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status "403" }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :ok }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :not_found }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :forbidden }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :success }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :error }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: numeric`
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :ok }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :not_found }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status "forbidden" }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status 200 }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status 404 }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status 403 }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :success }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :error }
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: be_status`
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :ok }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :not_found }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status "forbidden" }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status 200 }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status 404 }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status "403" }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to be_ok }
L   it { is_expected.to be_not_found }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :success }
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :error }
S Example
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :oki_doki }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :ok }
C RSpecRails/HttpStatusNameConsistency
S Details
L Enforces consistency by using the current HTTP status names.
S Example
L   # bad
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :unprocessable_entity }
L 
L   # good
L   it { is_expected.to have_http_status :unprocessable_content }
C RSpecRails/InferredSpecType
S Details
L Identifies redundant spec type.
L 
L After setting up rspec-rails, you will have enabled
L `config.infer_spec_type_from_file_location!` by default in
L spec/rails_helper.rb. This cop works in conjunction with this config.
L If you disable this config, disable this cop as well.
S Safety
L   This cop is marked as unsafe because
L   `config.infer_spec_type_from_file_location!` may not be enabled.
S Example
L   # bad
L   # spec/models/user_spec.rb
L   RSpec.describe User, type: :model do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # spec/models/user_spec.rb
L   RSpec.describe User do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # spec/models/user_spec.rb
L   RSpec.describe User, type: :common do
L   end
S Example: `Inferences: {services: service}`
L   # bad
L   # spec/services/user_spec.rb
L   RSpec.describe User, type: :service do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # spec/services/user_spec.rb
L   RSpec.describe User do
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   # spec/services/user_spec.rb
L   RSpec.describe User, type: :common do
L   end
C RSpecRails/MinitestAssertions
S Details
L Detects Minitest-like matchers.
L 
L Detects the use of Minitest-like matchers
L (those starting with `assert_` or `refute_`).
S Example
L   # bad
L   assert_equal(a, b)
L   assert_equal a, b, "must be equal"
L   assert_not_includes a, b
L   refute_equal(a, b)
L   assert_nil a
L   refute_empty(b)
L   assert_true(a)
L   assert_false(a)
L   assert_response :ok
L   assert_redirected_to '/users'
L 
L   # good
L   expect(b).to eq(a)
L   expect(b).to(eq(a), "must be equal")
L   expect(a).not_to include(b)
L   expect(b).not_to eq(a)
L   expect(a).to eq(nil)
L   expect(a).not_to be_empty
L   expect(a).to be(true)
L   expect(a).to be(false)
L   expect(response).to have_http_status(:ok)
L   expect(response).to redirect_to('/users')
C RSpecRails/NegationBeValid
S Details
L Enforces use of `be_invalid` or `not_to` for negated be_valid.
S Safety
L   This cop is unsafe because it cannot guarantee that
L   the test target is an instance of `ActiveModel::Validations``.
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: not_to` (default)
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).to be_invalid
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).not_to be_valid
L 
L   # good (with method chain)
L   expect(foo).to be_invalid.and be_odd
S Example: `EnforcedStyle: be_invalid`
L   # bad
L   expect(foo).not_to be_valid
L 
L   # good
L   expect(foo).to be_invalid
L 
L   # good (with method chain)
L   expect(foo).to be_invalid.or be_even
C RSpecRails/ReceivePerformLater
S Details
L Prefer `have_enqueued_job` over `receive(:perform_later)`.
L 
L The `have_enqueued_job` matcher is preferred for testing ActiveJob
L enqueuing. It is more explicit and provides better clarity than
L using `receive(:perform_later)`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   expect(MyJob).to receive(:perform_later)
L   do_something
L 
L   # bad
L   allow(MyJob).to receive(:perform_later)
L   do_something
L   expect(MyJob).to have_received(:perform_later)
L 
L   # bad
L   expect(MyJob).to receive(:perform_later).with(user, order)
L 
L   # good
L   expect { do_something }.to have_enqueued_job(MyJob)
L 
L   # good
L   expect { do_something }.to have_enqueued_job(MyJob).with(user, order)
L 
L   # good
L   expect { do_something }
L     .to have_enqueued_job(MyJob)
L     .on_queue('mailers')
L     .at(Date.tomorrow.noon)
C RSpecRails/TravelAround
S Details
L Checks for time travel helpers in `around` hooks.
L 
L In Rails example groups, `freeze_time` in a `before` hook does not need
L a block: Rails' `after_teardown` calls `travel_back` after each example.
L This also allows an example to use block-form time travel, which Rails
L does not allow inside another time travel block.
L See https://api.rubyonrails.org/classes/ActiveSupport/Testing/TimeHelpers.html
S Safety
L   The automatic `travel_back` requires both
L   `ActiveSupport::Testing::TimeHelpers` and RSpec Rails' lifecycle
L   hooks. RSpec Rails applies those hooks to example groups with Rails
L   `type:` metadata. File paths set that metadata only when
L   `infer_spec_type_from_file_location!` is enabled. In a plain RSpec
L   group that only includes TimeHelpers, use a block or call
L   `travel_back` explicitly.
L   See https://rspec.info/features/7-1/rspec-rails/directory-structure/
L 
L   Autocorrection can change execution order: `around` starts before
L   all `before` hooks, while `before` runs in normal hook order.
S Example
L   # bad
L   around do |example|
L     freeze_time do
L       example.run
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   around do |example|
L     freeze_time(&example)
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   before { freeze_time }
C Rails/ActionControllerFlashBeforeRender
S Details
L Using `flash` assignment before `render` in Rails controllers will persist the message for too long.
L Check https://guides.rubyonrails.org/action_controller_overview.html#flash-now
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because it replaces `flash` by `flash.now`.
L   Even though it is usually a mistake, it might be used intentionally.
S Example
L   # bad
L   class HomeController < ApplicationController
L     def create
L       flash[:alert] = "msg"
L       render :index
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class HomeController < ApplicationController
L     def create
L       flash.now[:alert] = "msg"
L       render :index
L     end
L   end
C Rails/ActionControllerTestCase
S Details
L Using `ActionController::TestCase` is discouraged and should be replaced by
L `ActionDispatch::IntegrationTest`. Controller tests are too close to the
L internals of a controller whereas integration tests mimic the browser/user.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because the API of each test case class is different.
L   Make sure to update each test of your controller test cases after changing the superclass.
S Example
L   # bad
L   class MyControllerTest < ActionController::TestCase
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class MyControllerTest < ActionDispatch::IntegrationTest
L   end
C Rails/ActionFilter
S Details
L Enforces the consistent use of action filter methods.
L 
L The cop is configurable and can enforce the use of the older
L something_filter methods or the newer something_action methods.
L 
L IMPORTANT: This cop is deprecated. Because the `*_filter` methods were removed in Rails 4.2,
L and that Rails version is no longer supported by RuboCop Rails. This cop will be removed in RuboCop Rails 3.0.
S Example: EnforcedStyle: action (default)
L   # bad
L   after_filter :do_stuff
L   append_around_filter :do_stuff
L   skip_after_filter :do_stuff
L 
L   # good
L   after_action :do_stuff
L   append_around_action :do_stuff
L   skip_after_action :do_stuff
S Example: EnforcedStyle: filter
L   # bad
L   after_action :do_stuff
L   append_around_action :do_stuff
L   skip_after_action :do_stuff
L 
L   # good
L   after_filter :do_stuff
L   append_around_filter :do_stuff
L   skip_after_filter :do_stuff
C Rails/ActionOrder
S Details
L Enforces consistent ordering of the standard Rails RESTful controller actions.
L 
L The cop is configurable and can enforce any ordering of the standard actions.
L All other methods are ignored. So, the actions specified in `ExpectedOrder` should be
L defined before actions not specified.
L 
L [source,yaml]
L ----
L  Rails/ActionOrder:
L    ExpectedOrder:
L      - index
L      - show
L      - new
L      - edit
L      - create
L      - update
L      - destroy
L ----
S Example
L   # bad
L   def index; end
L   def destroy; end
L   def show; end
L 
L   # good
L   def index; end
L   def show; end
L   def destroy; end
C Rails/ActiveRecordAliases
S Details
L Checks that ActiveRecord aliases are not used. The direct method names
L are more clear and easier to read.
S Safety
L   This cop is unsafe because custom `update_attributes` method call was changed to
L   `update` but the method name remained same in the method definition.
S Example
L   # bad
L   book.update_attributes!(author: 'Alice')
L 
L   # good
L   book.update!(author: 'Alice')
C Rails/ActiveRecordCallbacksOrder
S Details
L Checks that Active Record callbacks are declared
L in the order in which they will be executed.
S Example
L   # bad
L   class Person < ApplicationRecord
L     after_commit :after_commit_callback
L     before_validation :before_validation_callback
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Person < ApplicationRecord
L     before_validation :before_validation_callback
L     after_commit :after_commit_callback
L   end
C Rails/ActiveRecordOverride
S Details
L Checks for overriding built-in Active Record methods instead of using
L callbacks.
S Example
L   # bad
L   class Book < ApplicationRecord
L     def save
L       self.title = title.upcase!
L       super
L     end
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class Book < ApplicationRecord
L     before_save :upcase_title
L 
L     def upcase_title
L       self.title = title.upcase!
L     end
L   end
C Rails/ActiveSupportAliases
S Details
L Checks that ActiveSupport aliases to core ruby methods
L are not used.
S Example
L   # good
L   'some_string'.start_with?('prefix')
L   'some_string'.end_with?('suffix')
L   [1, 2, 'a'] << 'b'
L   [1, 2, 'a'].unshift('b')
L 
L   # bad
L   'some_string'.starts_with?('prefix')
L   'some_string'.ends_with?('suffix')
L   [1, 2, 'a'].append('b')
L   [1, 2, 'a'].prepend('b')
C Rails/ActiveSupportOnLoad
S Details
L Checks for Rails framework classes that are patched directly instead of using Active Support load hooks. Direct
L patching forcibly loads the framework referenced, using hooks defers loading until it's actually needed.
S Safety
L   While using lazy load hooks is recommended, it changes the order in which is code is loaded and may reveal
L   load order dependency bugs.
S Example
L   # bad
L   ActiveRecord::Base.include(MyClass)
L 
L   # good
L   ActiveSupport.on_load(:active_record) { include MyClass }
C Rails/AddColumnIndex
S Details
L Checks for migrations using `add_column` that have an `index`
L key. `add_column` does not accept `index`, but also does not raise an
L error for extra keys, so it is possible to mistakenly add the key without
L realizing it will not actually add an index.
S Example
L   # bad (will not add an index)
L   add_column :table, :column, :integer, index: true
L 
L   # good
L   add_column :table, :column, :integer
L   add_index :table, :column
C Rails/AfterCommitOverride
S Details
L Enforces that there is only one call to `after_commit`
L (and its aliases - `after_create_commit`, `after_update_commit`,
L and `after_destroy_commit`) with the same callback name per model.
S Example
L   # bad
L   # This won't be triggered.
L   after_create_commit :log_action
L 
L   # This will override the callback added by
L   # after_create_commit.
L   after_update_commit :log_action
L 
L   # bad
L   # This won't be triggered.
L   after_commit :log_action, on: :create
L   # This won't be triggered.
L   after_update_commit :log_action
L   # This will override both previous callbacks.
L   after_commit :log_action, on: :destroy
L 
L   # good
L   after_save_commit :log_action
L 
L   # good
L   after_create_commit :log_create_action
L   after_update_commit :log_update_action
C Rails/ApplicationController
S Details
L Checks that controllers subclass `ApplicationController`.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because it may let the logic from `ApplicationController`
L   sneak into a controller that is not purposed to inherit logic common among other controllers.
S Example
L  # good
L  class MyController < ApplicationController
L    # ...
L  end
L 
L  # bad
L  class MyController < ActionController::Base
L    # ...
L  end
C Rails/ApplicationJob
S Details
L Checks that jobs subclass `ApplicationJob` with Rails 5.0.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because it may let the logic from `ApplicationJob`
L   sneak into a job that is not purposed to inherit logic common among other jobs.
S Example
L  # good
L  class Rails5Job < ApplicationJob
L    # ...
L  end
L 
L  # bad
L  class Rails4Job < ActiveJob::Base
L    # ...
L  end
C Rails/ApplicationMailer
S Details
L Checks that mailers subclass `ApplicationMailer` with Rails 5.0.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because it may let the logic from `ApplicationMailer`
L   sneak into a mailer that is not purposed to inherit logic common among other mailers.
S Example
L  # good
L  class MyMailer < ApplicationMailer
L    # ...
L  end
L 
L  # bad
L  class MyMailer < ActionMailer::Base
L    # ...
L  end
C Rails/ApplicationRecord
S Details
L Checks that models subclass `ApplicationRecord` with Rails 5.0.
L 
L It is a common practice to define models inside migrations in order to retain forward
L compatibility by avoiding loading any application code. And so migration files are excluded
L by default for this cop.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because it may let the logic from `ApplicationRecord`
L   sneak into an Active Record model that is not purposed to inherit logic common among other
L   Active Record models.
S Example
L  # good
L  class Rails5Model < ApplicationRecord
L    # ...
L  end
L 
L  # bad
L  class Rails4Model < ActiveRecord::Base
L    # ...
L  end
C Rails/ArelStar
S Details
L Prevents usage of `"*"` on an Arel::Table column reference.
L 
L Using `arel_table["\*"]` causes the outputted string to be a literal
L quoted asterisk (e.g. `my_model`.`*`). This causes the
L database to look for a column named `\*` (or `"*"`) as opposed
L to expanding the column list as one would likely expect.
S Safety
L   This cop's autocorrection is unsafe because it turns a quoted `\*` into
L   an SQL `*`, unquoted. `\*` is a valid column name in certain databases
L   supported by Rails, and even though it is usually a mistake,
L   it might denote legitimate access to a column named `*`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   MyTable.arel_table["*"]
L 
L   # good
L   MyTable.arel_table[Arel.star]
C Rails/AssertNot
S Details
L Use `assert_not` instead of `assert !`.
S Example
L   # bad
L   assert !x
L 
L   # good
L   assert_not x
C Rails/AttributeDefaultBlockValue
S Details
L Looks for `attribute` class methods that specify a `:default` option
L which value is an array, string literal or method call without a block.
L It will accept all other values, such as string, symbol, integer and float literals
L as well as constants.
S Example
L   # bad
L   class User < ApplicationRecord
L     attribute :confirmed_at, :datetime, default: Time.zone.now
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class User < ApplicationRecord
L     attribute :confirmed_at, :datetime, default: -> { Time.zone.now }
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   class User < ApplicationRecord
L     attribute :roles, :string, array: true, default: []
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class User < ApplicationRecord
L     attribute :roles, :string, array: true, default: -> { [] }
L   end
L 
L   # good
L   class User < ApplicationRecord
L     attribute :roles, :string, array: true, default: [].freeze
L   end
L 
L   # bad
L   class User < ApplicationRecord
L     attribute :configuration, default: {}
L   end
L 
L   # good
